# Malaysia: migration guide

How to go to Malaysia from Bangladesh, visa by visa, step by step, for Bangladesh passport holders. \[3\] is an official source; \[N3\] is a news, NGO, research or guide source, listed under "Needs an official source"; a year, like \[N3 (2022)\], marks a source older than 2025. "Need to know" marks a point no source has yet confirmed. "You'll need:" lists what to have before starting a procedure.

Tk is the Bangladeshi taka (1 lakh is Tk 100,000); RM is the Malaysian ringgit. BMET (Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) is the Bangladesh government agency that registers people who want to work abroad and gives them emigration clearance \[[1](#o1), [2](#o2)\]. IMI (Jabatan Imigresen Malaysia, the Immigration Department of Malaysia) approves work visas and runs the eVISA (electronic visa) system on its MyVISA website, malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my \[[3](#o3), [4](#o4), [5](#o5)\]. Bangladeshis need a visa to enter Malaysia for any purpose, even a short visit \[[3](#o3), [6](#o6)\].

## Background

**In one minute.** A Malaysia job starts as a quota: permission for one Malaysian employer to bring in a set number of foreign workers \[[7](#o7)\]. Each slot becomes a "calling visa" (কলিং ভিসা) that is sold down a chain: the employer's demand letter is bought in Malaysia, passes through one of the few agencies Malaysia lets into its FWCMS visa system, then a licensed agency, then a village broker (দালাল), and every link takes a cut \[[N1](#n1), [N2](#n2), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [N4 (2018)](#n4)\]. The legal cap was Tk 78,990, but in 2022-24 workers paid about Tk 4.5 to 6 lakh, nearly all of it borrowed \[[8 (2022)](#o8), [N5](#n5), [N6](#n6), [N7 (2024)](#n7)\]. The job pays the RM1,700 minimum (about Tk 52,000; ringgit are converted throughout at RM1 = about Tk 30.6, the rate on 6 November 2026) plus overtime, and the debt takes one to three years to clear, longer if the job or the pay doesn't come \[[9 (2024)](#o9), [N8](#n8), [N9 (2023)](#n9), [N3 (2024)](#n3)\]. The work pass ties you to one employer, and a second medical in Malaysia decides whether you get it at all \[[5](#o5)\]. The corridor has shut three times, each time after a surge of departures and allegations against the agencies; the last shutdown, in 2024, stranded 17,777 workers who had paid \[[N10 (2024)](#n10), [N11 (2018)](#n11), [N12 (2024)](#n12)\]. Recruitment was announced again in October 2026, but by early November medical checks had not been approved and almost no one had flown \[[10](#o10), [N13](#n13), [11](#o11)\].

**Is it open now, and who can go?**

Announced, not yet moving. Bangladesh announced on 15 October 2026 that recruitment had restarted through agencies on Malaysia's list \[[10](#o10)\]. Medical checks were due to start on 31 October but did not, because no list of approved centres had been published \[[12](#o12), [N14](#n14)\]. On 4 November the expatriates' welfare minister, Ariful Haque Chowdhury, said Malaysia medicals had not been approved: a list of 46 centres had gone to Malaysia for vetting, and centres already testing Malaysia-bound workers were doing so illegally and would face action \[[N13](#n13)\]. A medical done before the official start is not accepted for clearance, and the Ministry has told people not to pay money, do medicals or hand over passports until it issues instructions \[[12](#o12), [N15](#n15)\]. BMET cleared 1,592 workers for Malaysia from 1 January to 6 November 2026; the minister hopes the first flights leave in December \[[11](#o11), [N16](#n16)\].

**Status.** Malaysia has agreed in principle to take 10,000 workers at zero cost, the first through BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Ltd, the government-owned recruiting agency) \[[10](#o10), [13](#o13)\]. The minister says they will not pay a single taka and will be picked by online lottery; the state minister, Nurul Haque Nur, said in late October they would still pay the airfare, the medical and a small BOESL service charge \[[N16](#n16), [N17](#n17)\]. BOESL's lottery draws from applicants registered on OEP (the Overseas Employment Platform, BMET's job-seeker website), so registering there is the way in; its first zero-cost circular, on 5 November, was for 112 men at DHL Supply Chain (Malaysia) (Step 1, item 3) \[[14](#o14), [N16](#n16)\]. Anyone selling a "zero-cost" or "BOESL" place is selling something the government says is free (Scams) \[[N16](#n16), [15](#o15)\]. No cap has been fixed for private agencies; they proposed at most Tk 2.5 to 3 lakh \[[N16](#n16)\]. Employers have begun filing demand letters (চাহিদাপত্র, job orders) through FWCMS (Foreign Workers Centralised Management System, Malaysia's online foreign-worker system), and one listed agency said it held orders for about 15,000 workers \[[N17](#n17)\]. The Bangladesh-Malaysia MoU (memorandum of understanding) ends on 31 December 2026; the minister says a new one will be signed that day and wants every licensed agency allowed in, so the rules may change in January \[[13](#o13), [N18](#n18)\].

**The channels.**

- **A private agency on Malaysia's list:** the route nearly everyone uses; about 4.5 lakh went this way in 2022-24 \[[N5](#n5), [N19 (2024)](#n19)\]. How the job passes through the agencies is under "How a job reaches you".
- **BOESL:** circulars on boesl.gov.bd, a lottery among OEP-registered applicants, then the employer's interview (Step 1, item 3) \[[14](#o14), [N16](#n16)\]. Only 1,381 went this way from October 2022 to May 2024 \[[N19 (2024)](#n19)\].
- **CLAB (Construction Labour Exchange Centre Berhad, Malaysia's government construction recruiter), through BOESL:** construction only; it rejected about a third of those it interviewed on health and suitability \[[N20](#n20)\].
- **Employment Pass:** professionals on at least RM5,000 a month \[[16](#o16)\].
- **No legal route:** domestic work (Bangladesh is not an approved source country) \[[7](#o7), [17](#o17)\]; women in general jobs (no agreement exists, and the High Commission in Kuala Lumpur tells women not to go) \[[N21](#n21)\]; and jobs in Sabah, where there is no recruitment agreement and rings collect passports and cash \[[N22](#n22)\]. The main irregular route is a tourist visa used to work ("What goes wrong most") \[[N23](#n23)\].

**Who qualifies.** Immigration sets three conditions: aged 18 to 45 when the employer applies, passed fit at a medical centre in Bangladesh, and a job in one of the five allowed sectors (manufacturing, construction, plantation, agriculture, services) \[[5](#o5)\]. There is no skill test except in construction, where since January 2025 a worker needs a CIDB (Construction Industry Development Board) skills certificate, tested at Khulna TTC (Step 1, item 2) \[[5](#o5), [18 (2024)](#o18), [19](#o19)\]. Job orders can be narrower: the DHL circular wanted men aged 22 to 35 with SSC or HSC, English and MS Excel \[[14](#o14)\]. Passing the Bangladesh medical is not the last health check: in Malaysia the employer must take you to a FOMEMA clinic within 30 days of arrival, and the work pass is issued only if you pass ("What ties you to the employer") \[[5](#o5)\]. **Need to know:** do any Bangladeshi women hold Malaysian factory work passes, and through which agencies did they go?

**Size.** About 8 lakh Bangladeshis held Malaysian work passes at the end of 2025, more than any other nationality \[[20](#o20)\].

**What closed it before.** Each time, a surge of departures, then allegations against the agencies running it, then a freeze \[[N24](#n24), [N10 (2024)](#n10), [N11 (2018)](#n11), [N17](#n17)\]. Malaysia froze hiring in 2009 and cancelled the 10-agency "G2G Plus" scheme in September 2018 \[[N10 (2024)](#n10), [N11 (2018)](#n11)\]. The last round, under the 2021 MoU, ended when Malaysia set 31 May 2024 as the last day for new workers to enter, after tens of thousands had paid up to USD 5,000 for jobs that did not exist \[[21 (2021)](#o21), [N10 (2024)](#n10), [N25 (2024)](#n25)\]. About 17,777 workers who had BMET clearance could not fly by the deadline \[[N12 (2024)](#n12)\]. Many got no refund and some a small one; Malaysia cleared 7,873 of them to go through BOESL, which charged them another Tk 1,62,500 each, and the first 60 flew on 25 November 2025, 18 months after the deadline \[[N26](#n26), [22](#o22), [N27](#n27), [N28](#n28)\]. Of about 4,000 who applied, 2,900 had been selected by mid-November 2025 \[[N26](#n26)\]. **Need to know:** how many of the 7,873 had flown by November 2026, and whether CLAB's 800 construction workers of December 2025 to January 2026 were among them.

**How a job reaches you, and who takes a cut**

The quota is the root: each approved slot becomes a calling visa that is bought and resold before it reaches a village \[[7](#o7), [N2](#n2), [N1](#n1)\]. The choke point is FWCMS, the Bestinet-built system every Bangladeshi worker's visa passes through: only BOESL and 25 private agencies chosen by Malaysia can process a visa in it, so every other agency pays one of them \[[23](#o23), [N29](#n29), [10](#o10), [N2](#n2)\]. On the street the whole arrangement is called the সিন্ডিকেট (syndicate) \[[N30](#n30)\].

**The quota.** The employer gets a quota and pays a yearly levy per worker, RM1,850 in manufacturing, construction and services and RM640 in plantation and agriculture in Peninsular Malaysia \[[7](#o7), [24](#o24)\]. A tiered levy that would charge more to firms that depend heavily on foreign workers is planned but not in force; manufacturers were still pressing for it in July 2026 \[[N31](#n31), [N32](#n32)\]. Quotas are approved on the employer's paper eligibility, so a quota can exist with no job behind it, and Malaysia's private employment agencies' association says agents apply for 100 workers when a company needs 10 \[[N33 (2023)](#n33), [N34 (2023)](#n34)\]. In October 2026 Immigration broke up an "A-to-B" ring, believed to be run by two Bangladeshi men, that got quotas on false company details and hired the workers out to other firms at RM3,800 to 5,000 profit a head \[[N35](#n35), [N36](#n36)\].

**Which employer.** Employer size and sector change the risk. Large export manufacturers under buyer pressure have repaid recruitment fees: after US import bans for forced labour, Top Glove paid workers over USD 30 million and FGV set aside RM112 million to reimburse fees to current and former migrant workers \[[N37 (2021)](#n37), [N38 (2024)](#n38)\]. The ghost-job cases are small firms with big quotas: services firms brought in 142,204 workers in 2023 against 20,000 needed, 161 Bangladeshis promised construction jobs had no work or pay for seven months, and a Kuala Lumpur company hired about 600 and gave them no work \[[N39 (2023)](#n39), [N40 (2024)](#n40), [N41 (2024)](#n41)\].

**Company visa, agent visa, free visa.** Workers sort jobs by who holds the visa. A কোম্পানি ভিসা (company visa) means you work for the company that applied for you; it pays the visa and the yearly renewal, and keeps your passport \[[N42 (2024)](#n42)\]. An "agent visa" puts you under an agent or subcontractor who hires you out: outsourcing companies were abolished in 2019, but researchers found in 2023 that some workers who go legally still land with one and are sent to work elsewhere, and can't tell until they arrive \[[N43 (2019)](#n43), [N44 (2023)](#n44)\]. A ফ্রি ভিসা (free visa) sells the right to work anywhere, which no work pass gives: every PLKS is tied to the employer that applied, free-visa workers pay an agent about RM5,000 (Tk 1.53 lakh) a year to renew, and agents hold back weeks or months of their pay \[[5](#o5), [N42 (2024)](#n42)\]. Working away from the company on your permit is an offence: Immigration's Ops Mahir raid in Ipoh in September 2026 detained 175 foreigners, mostly Bangladeshis, including people working outside the company named on their permit \[[N45](#n45)\]. What separates them is which company holds your quota and whether you work at its address \[[N42 (2024)](#n42), [N45](#n45)\]. The calling visa (the VDR approval) comes first and the e-visa after it, both through BOESL or one of the 25; Step 5 shows how to check them \[[10](#o10), [5](#o5)\].

**The demand letter: the first price.** A Bangladeshi agency buys the employer's demand letter \[[N1](#n1)\]. Agency owners say one now costs RM8,000 to 12,000 per worker (Tk 2.45 to 3.67 lakh), against RM2,000 to 3,000 before; in 2022-24 middlemen in Malaysia took Tk 1.6 to 2 lakh per worker for it \[[N1](#n1), [N3 (2024)](#n3)\]. Big agencies buy the demand and pay the middlemen around it, so small agencies can't send workers cheaply \[[N1](#n1)\]. **Need to know:** who in Malaysia sells demand letters (the employer's own staff, Malaysian agents, Bangladeshi-born brokers), and how the money is paid.

**Attestation.** No demand letter is accepted until the Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur attests it, against the employer's notarised commitment to give the workers work and pay \[[N46](#n46), [25 (2023)](#o25)\]. In 2019 the High Commissioner said the mission lacked the staff to check every case \[[N47 (2019)](#n47)\]. **Need to know:** what the High Commission checks before attesting a 2026 demand (a site visit, the quota approval, the levy receipt), and how long it takes.

**The processing agency: the toll gate.** Malaysia lists 338 Bangladeshi agencies: BOESL, the 25 "processing" agencies, and 312 associates (সহযোগী এজেন্সি), 10 under each of the 25 and 62 under BOESL \[[10](#o10), [26](#o26), [N48](#n48)\]. An associate may find employers, collect demand and select workers, but the calling visa and e-visa go through BOESL or one of the 25, which issues a no-objection certificate so the associate can get BMET clearance under its own licence \[[10](#o10)\]. In 2022-24, when 101 agencies held access, the toll was Tk 1.07 to 1.52 lakh per worker \[[N5](#n5), [N2](#n2)\]. For 2026, claims run from a Tk 6,000 processing fee to RM5,500 (about Tk 1.68 lakh), the figure BAIRA (Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) members allege \[[N49](#n49), [N2](#n2)\]. Even the government channel buys visas: of the Tk 1,62,500 BOESL charged stranded workers, its managing director said Tk 1 lakh was for buying visas \[[N27](#n27)\].

**Who controls it.** Bestinet, which built FWCMS, was founded by Aminul Islam Abdul Nor ("Amin"), a Bangladesh-born Malaysian; Malaysia's Auditor-General found it collected RM100 per worker without a formal government contract \[[23](#o23), [N50](#n50), [N51 (2023)](#n51)\]. The Daily Star reports that Amin and Ruhul Amin Swapan, owner of Catharsis International, led the 2022-24 syndicate; Bangladesh police sought their extradition in 2024, both deny wrongdoing, and Amin has not been charged \[[N52](#n52), [N53](#n53), [N25 (2024)](#n25), [N50](#n50)\]. In 2026 a Malaysian committee chose the 25 without the Bangladesh government, 17 of them had never sent a worker to Malaysia, and Prothom Alo reported allegations that Amin and Swapan again decided who joined \[[N2](#n2)\]. Bangladesh's ACC (Anti-Corruption Commission) has filed or approved cases against dozens of the 2022-24 agencies for charging up to five times the cap, and in July 2026 the Ministry cancelled 49 licences, Catharsis among them \[[N54](#n54), [N55](#n55), [N56](#n56)\]. Each agency's record, including these cases, is in the `agencies` folder.

**The broker and the village.** Most workers reach the agency through a broker: 78% of Bangladeshi workers in a 2023 ITUC (International Trade Union Confederation) survey used a sub-agent, and in 2018 an agency owner said a worker passes through five or six levels of brokers \[[N57 (2023)](#n57), [N4 (2018)](#n4)\]. The broker is often someone the worker knows: in October 2026 a neighbour asked a Sunamganj grocer for his passport and told him to have over Tk 7 lakh ready, and an owner of one of the 312 said he had already collected over 1,000 passports \[[N1](#n1)\]. Across Bangladesh's visa trade, BAIRA's secretary general says that when a visa comes through relatives the money changes hands mainly among the relatives, with the agency only doing the paperwork \[[N58 (2024)](#n58)\]. Unauthorised middlemen take Tk 50,000 to 1 lakh \[[N3 (2024)](#n3)\]. Only a sub-agent registered with BMET may now recruit (Step 1, "Before paying") \[[27 (2023)](#o27), [28](#o28)\]. **Need to know:** what a Bangladeshi worker or supervisor in a Malaysian factory charges a relative for a place on his own company's quota in 2026; what a village broker takes per Malaysia worker, and whether the worker, the agency or both pay him.

**What you pay, and what you earn and keep**

**The cap.** The Ministry's July 2022 order capped the worker's costs inside Bangladesh at Tk 78,990, with the employer paying everything in Malaysia, airfare both ways included (Step 1, "Before paying") \[[8 (2022)](#o8), [21 (2021)](#o21)\]. Some 2024 permission letters set less, Tk 41,240 or Tk 38,710 (Step 1, item 5) \[[29](#o29), [30 (2024)](#o30)\]. In April 2026 both governments said employers will bear the full cost, but no cap has been set for private agencies since the October announcement; the state minister says nobody followed the old one \[[N59](#n59), [N16](#n16), [N1](#n1)\].

**What people really pay.** Tk 5.44 lakh on average in 2022-24 (Verité's FFACT study), with press and workers' groups giving Tk 3.5 to 6 lakh \[[N5](#n5), [N6](#n6), [N60](#n60)\]. Workers call the price the চুক্তি (deal) struck with the broker: "went on a Tk 5 lakh deal" \[[N61](#n61)\]. For 2026, brokers collecting passports tell people to keep Tk 7 to 7.5 lakh ready and a listed agency's owner says no less than Tk 6 lakh, while the minister expects Tk 2.5 to 3 lakh \[[N1](#n1), [N17](#n17), [N18](#n18)\].

**Where a Tk 5.5 lakh deal goes.** The 2022-24 pieces reported per worker: the demand letter, Tk 1.6 to 2 lakh to middlemen in Malaysia; the syndicate toll, Tk 1.07 to 1.52 lakh; agents in Bangladesh, about Tk 1.42 lakh, of which an unauthorised middleman takes Tk 50,000 to 1 lakh; and official fees of about Tk 15,000 (medical Tk 8,000, welfare fee Tk 5,500, insurance Tk 1,000, clearance Tk 250, source tax Tk 800 to 1,200) \[[N5](#n5), [N2](#n2), [N50](#n50), [N3 (2024)](#n3), [31](#o31), [8 (2022)](#o8)\]. Together these come to about Tk 4.2 to 5.1 lakh \[[N5](#n5), [N2](#n2), [N50](#n50), [31](#o31), [8 (2022)](#o8)\]. The deal usually also covers the airfare (Tk 45,000 in BOESL's own 2025 costing), the visa and the medical, though the MoU puts all three on the employer \[[N26](#n26), [N62](#n62), [21 (2021)](#o21)\]. Even official items get padded: in the last round agencies took Tk 11,000 for a medical fixed at Tk 6,500 \[[N63](#n63)\]. **Need to know:** in 2026, how much of the চুক্তি is paid with the passport, how much when the calling visa comes, and how much before the flight or the BMET card.

**How it is paid.** In stages, much of it to a broker, with receipts for little of it; down payments for the passport and medical carry none \[[N64](#n64), [N65 (2024)](#n65)\]. A delay raises the price: a Cumilla man's Tk 3.6 lakh became Tk 5.7 lakh \[[N61](#n61)\]. Before flying, workers have been made to sign, and record on video, that they paid only the official fee, and in 2025 the police cited those signatures to close a trafficking case (Scams) \[[N62](#n62), [N66](#n66), [N29](#n29)\].

**How they borrow.** In a 2024 survey of 240 Malaysia workers, 96% had borrowed and 82% had two or more loans \[[N7 (2024)](#n7)\]. A typical package is a land mortgage (জমি বন্ধক), cattle sold and loans from relatives: a Natore farmer mortgaged his 16 decimals for Tk 1 lakh and sold four cows for Tk 4.5 lakh to pay a Paltan agency Tk 5.5 lakh, then could not fly \[[N65 (2024)](#n65)\]. Informal loans are usually paid straight to the broker, so nothing records them \[[N7 (2024)](#n7), [N64](#n64)\]. Workers in Malaysia borrow at 10% a month to renew their permits; a microcredit NGO in Bangladesh may charge at most 24% a year, the cap set by the MRA (Microcredit Regulatory Authority) in 2019 \[[N61](#n61), [N67 (2024)](#n67)\]. The government's PKB (Probashi Kallyan Bank) loan comes only after the visa, and many can't find its guarantors (Step 1, "Before paying") \[[32](#o32), [N68 (2023)](#n68)\]. **Need to know:** the monthly rate a village moneylender (মহাজন) charges on a Malaysia loan in 2026, and the usual terms of a land mortgage taken for one (amount per decimal, who works the land, when it is returned).

**What the job pays: a correct payslip.** Basic pay is at least RM1,700 a month (about Tk 52,000), RM8.72 an hour or RM65.38 a day on a six-day week \[[9 (2024)](#o9)\]. Overtime is paid at least 1.5 times the hourly rate, overtime on a rest day at least 2 times and on a public holiday at least 3 times, and overtime may not exceed 104 hours a month (Your rights at work) \[[33 (2023)](#o33), [N69](#n69)\]. Wages are due by the 7th of the next month \[[33 (2023)](#o33), [34 (2022)](#o34)\]. The deductions allowed: 2% for EPF (Employees Provident Fund, Malaysia's retirement fund), about RM34 on the basic, compulsory since October 2025 wages and matched by the employer's 2%; 0.5% for SOCSO (Malaysia's social security); rent of at most RM100 a month; and no more than half the wage in total \[[35](#o35), [36](#o36), [N70 (2020)](#n70), [33 (2023)](#o33)\]. The EPF money, both shares, is paid back in full when you leave for good, on the Check-Out Memo (Step 7, item 7; Leaving at the end of the job) \[[35](#o35)\]. The levy, the pass renewal and the FOMEMA medical are the employer's to pay \[[7](#o7), [37 (2022)](#o37)\]. RM65 a day for 12-hour days, as at a Pahang plywood factory in 2026 on 8-hour contracts, is the minimum day rate for normal hours alone, so the four extra hours were unpaid \[[N71](#n71), [9 (2024)](#o9)\]. The basic is to rise to RM2,000 from June 2027, but only at firms with yearly sales of RM50 million or more; smaller firms are exempt \[[38](#o38)\]. Food is the worker's own cost, and over 40% of income goes on the mess (shared lodging), transport and food \[[39 (2024)](#o39), [N9 (2023)](#n9)\].

**What goes unpaid.** In the 2024 survey 43% got less than promised \[[N7 (2024)](#n7)\]. The usual losses:

- Overtime unpaid or paid at a flat rate (the Pahang case above) \[[N71](#n71)\].
- Wages held for months, often before the company closes: up to eight months at Kawaguchi, nearly three at Aerofoam in 2026 \[[N72](#n72), [N73](#n73)\].
- The employer's costs taken from pay: the levy, the yearly renewal, hostel charges, uniforms (RM500 in one case) and "flight-ticket deposits" \[[N74](#n74), [N61](#n61), [N62](#n62), [N73](#n73)\].
- Time with no job, earning nothing while the loan runs \[[N61](#n61)\].

Workers who ask for the promised pay have been fired or told to do more overtime \[[N9 (2023)](#n9)\]. How to claim: If you aren't paid.

**Money sent home.** About 85% send money, 60% of them through banks and over 16% through hundi (an unlicensed money agent) \[[N75 (2023)](#n75)\]. Hundi survives because it converts at the kerb rate, which in 2022 was well above the bank rate, because the hundi man comes to the mess, and because an employer holding your passport shuts you out of banks \[[N76 (2022)](#n76), [N6](#n6), [N75 (2023)](#n75)\]. Against that: money sent through a bank or a licensed app earns the government's 2.5% incentive, continued in June 2026 \[[N77](#n77), [40](#o40)\]; hundi is an offence in Bangladesh, and Bangladesh Bank has frozen about 5,000 agent and MFS (mobile money) accounts used for it, after which families could not cash remittances received in them \[[N78 (2022)](#n78)\]; and Malaysian Immigration arrests hundi operators working behind restaurants and shops \[[N79 (2023)](#n79)\]. How to send: Sending money home.

**How long the debt takes.** At the 2022-24 price the cost equals at least a year's wages; counting living costs, Winrock put repayment at 21-22 months and a WARBE (Welfare Association for the Rights of Bangladeshi Emigrants) chairman says two to three years \[[N80 (2024)](#n80), [N9 (2023)](#n9), [N3 (2024)](#n3)\]. Meanwhile 73% spend half or more of their monthly pay on repayments \[[N7 (2024)](#n7)\]. Every month without work or pay, and every renewal the worker is made to pay, adds to it \[[N61](#n61)\]. **Need to know:** what a plantation worker takes home after food in 2026.

**What ties you to the employer**

Completely. The PLKS (Pas Lawatan Kerja Sementara, the work pass; the "permit", পারমিট) names one employer; only that employer can bring you in, renew the pass and send you home, and work for anyone else breaks it \[[5](#o5), [7](#o7), [41 (2006)](#o41), [N61](#n61)\]. The standard contract is 3 years, renewable, with 90 days' probation, and the air ticket home is due only after the contract is completed \[[39 (2024)](#o39), [14](#o14)\]; that last term is behind both the "flight-ticket deposit" and the false lari reports near a contract's end (below).

- **Enter:** tied. The employer holds the quota and applies for your VDR (Visa Dengan Rujukan, the calling visa approval); you wait in Bangladesh until it is approved, and can check its status yourself on Immigration's MyIMMs site (Step 3, item 2) \[[7](#o7), [5](#o5)\]. The employer must collect you at the airport within 6 hours of landing (Step 7, item 1) \[[5](#o5)\].
- **Stay:** the FOMEMA medical decides. The employer has 30 days after arrival to take you to a FOMEMA clinic, and the PLKS is issued only if you pass; if you fail, you are sent home on a Check-Out Memo with the debt unpaid \[[5](#o5)\]. Since December 2023 the check is yearly, at RM207 for men \[[N81](#n81)\]. FOMEMA rejects workers with diabetes, high blood pressure and high cholesterol, and neither worker nor employer gets the report \[[N81](#n81), [N82 (2024)](#n82)\]. A ring sold forged Bangladeshi passports to workers in the Klang Valley who had failed FOMEMA so they could sit it again \[[N83](#n83)\]. FOMEMA status can be checked by passport number (Step 7, item 2) \[[42](#o42)\]. **Need to know:** does any agency refund part of the package when a worker fails FOMEMA, and is this ever written into the চুক্তি?
- **Renew:** tied. Only the employer renews, once a year, up to 10 years (How long you can stay) \[[7](#o7), [5](#o5)\]. Workers call it ভিসা রিনিউ (visa renewal) \[[N42 (2024)](#n42)\]. The official cost in manufacturing, construction or services is about RM2,055 (Tk 63,000): the RM1,850 levy, RM60 for the pass, RM125 processing and RM20 visa, all payable by the employer \[[24](#o24), [7](#o7)\]. Workers report paying RM2,500 to 3,200 a year (Tk 77,000 to 98,000), often borrowed at 10% a month \[[N61](#n61)\]; a Kuala Lumpur restaurant worker who had paid a broker Tk 5 lakh needed another Tk 92,000 to renew \[[N61](#n61)\].
- **Quit:** 2 months' notice or 2 months' wages, and your own fare home; Immigration normally cancels the pass at once \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [33 (2023)](#o33), [N84 (2021)](#n84)\].
- **Change employer:** not on your own; only the employer can apply, and only if it closes, goes bankrupt, is restructured or dies (If you want to change employer) \[[5](#o5), [43 (2024)](#o43)\]. A Kuala Lumpur migrant lawyer calls a legal job change almost impossible and a main reason so many end up undocumented \[[N85 (2019)](#n85)\].
- **Leave:** tied. The employer applies for the Check-Out Memo and since 2025 must be present at the exit point (Leaving at the end of the job) \[[7](#o7), [N86](#n86)\]. Its RM500 security bond for you is refunded only if you leave on that memo and forfeited if you run \[[24](#o24)\]. That bond is why employers hold passports and demand deposits: at a Pahang plywood factory in 2026, workers had to pay RM2,500 before going home on annual leave \[[N71](#n71)\]. Missing the agreed return date counts as running away \[[5](#o5)\].

**Which law covers you.** The pass falls under the Immigration Act, so any breach of its conditions voids it at once \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. The work falls under the Employment Act 1955 in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan, and separate Labour Ordinances in Sabah and Sarawak \[[44](#o44)\]. In Sarawak a foreign worker is bound by a Labour Department licence to the job, workplace and licence holder named on it \[[45](#o45)\].

**Your passport in practice.** The law forbids the employer to keep it (Step 7, item 4) \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [46 (1997)](#o46)\], but employers routinely take passports on arrival "for safekeeping" without a receipt, and the US government's 2026 report calls retention widespread \[[47](#o47), [N74](#n74)\]. In an IOM study of 40 workers who became undocumented, every one had their passport held by the employer or agency \[[N87 (2023)](#n87)\]. Held passports are ransomed: the Kuala Lumpur company that gave about 600 Bangladeshis no work asked Tk 1.7 to 1.8 lakh each for theirs in 2024 \[[N41 (2024)](#n41)\]. Some have got passports back by complaining to the authorities \[[N73](#n73)\].

**What running away or an expired pass does to you.**

- **Running away (lari, Malay for "run").** The employer reports you, you are blacklisted, the bond is forfeited and the pass cancelled; employers also file false reports near the contract's end to avoid the fare home, or after a complaint (If your employer reports you absent (lari)) \[[24](#o24), [N84 (2021)](#n84), [47](#o47)\]. You become what Malaysia calls PATI (Pendatang Asing Tanpa Izin, foreigner without permission) and Bangladeshis call অবৈধ (illegal) \[[N88](#n88), [N41 (2024)](#n41)\].
- **An expired pass** makes you an overstayer: fine, jail or a compound, then removal and a blacklist (What can get you jailed) \[[41 (2006)](#o41), [48](#o48), [N89 (2022)](#n89)\]. Most undocumented workers got there this way: all 40 in the IOM study arrived legally and left their jobs because of exploitation, and recruitment debt kept them in Malaysia afterwards \[[N87 (2023)](#n87)\].
- **Buying your way back to legal.** No legalisation programme is open; the last, RTK (the Labour Recalibration Programme), ended on 31 December 2023 \[[N90](#n90)\]. Past rounds were a market: agents charged RM6,000 to 10,000 against an official maximum of RM3,900, and rings sold fake renewals and PLKS stickers at RM2,500 to 6,000 \[[N91 (2018)](#n91), [N92 (2023)](#n92), [N93 (2023)](#n93)\]. The only official way out is home under PRM 2.0, with a compound of RM300 for breaking pass conditions or RM500 for overstaying (Going home without valid papers) \[[49](#o49)\].

**Need to know:** once Immigration cancels a quitting worker's pass, how many days he has to leave, and whether he can get the Check-Out Memo without the employer; whether outsourcing or shell companies still sponsor Bangladeshis on PLKS to work anywhere in 2026, and what that "visa" sells for; how long a worker reported as lari (not deported) stays blacklisted.

**What goes wrong most, and what people wrongly believe**

Money owed is the commonest complaint: unpaid, late or cut wages, then jobs that don't exist or don't match the promise \[[47](#o47), [N94](#n94), [N95](#n95)\]. The Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur has had about 450 complaints since 2022, about wage arrears, visa renewal, recruitment and job disputes, and says it settled over 330 by mediation \[[N94](#n94)\]. In January to June 2026 it helped about 400 people with labour disputes, unpaid wages, accidents and immigration problems, and issued 6,853 travel passes (ট্রাভেল পাস, a one-way document home), 1,319 of them to people in detention camps \[[N96](#n96)\]. Before departure the loss is money taken and no departure: in 2023 BMET resolved 865 of 2,380 complaints, and most people got back less than half of what they lost \[[50 (2024)](#o50), [N97 (2024)](#n97)\]. OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a Bangladeshi migrants' organisation) finds 75% leave without having seen a contract \[[N98](#n98)\].

**What people believe, and the facts.**

- **"I've paid the full price, so a job is waiting."** The quota surplus ("How a job reaches you") makes ghost jobs structural: 161 Bangladeshis promised RM1,500 construction jobs had no work or pay for seven months, and their employer asked RM6,000 to return their passports \[[N40 (2024)](#n40)\]. Activists and researchers estimate 100,000 to 200,000 of about 800,000 Bangladeshis in Malaysia are jobless, unpaid and in debt \[[N99 (2024)](#n99)\]. Before paying the bulk, the employer can be checked on SSM (Malaysia's company registry) and the calling visa on MyIMMs (Step 1, item 6; Step 3, item 2) \[[51](#o51), [52](#o52), [5](#o5)\].
- **"Passing the medical in Dhaka means I'm in."** FOMEMA in Malaysia decides, every year, and failing it means going home with the debt ("What ties you to the employer") \[[5](#o5), [N81](#n81)\].
- **"Go on a tourist visa; the work permit gets done there."** Travel agents sell exactly this: a Paltan travel agency owner promised work permits, sent two people on visit visas, held them and demanded Tk 5 lakh from their families \[[N100](#n100)\]. A visit pass allows no work and can't become a work pass inside Malaysia; the worker must be outside Malaysia while the employer applies (Changing visa type) \[[53](#o53), [7](#o7)\]. In one July 2025 operation 123 Bangladeshis holding tourist visas were refused entry at KLIA (Kuala Lumpur International Airport) \[[N101](#n101)\].
- **"Malaysia will legalise us again."** A January 2025 rumour said RTK 3.0 was coming; Immigration's Director General said no decision had been taken \[[N102](#n102)\]. Rings sold fake places in past rounds \[[N103 (2024)](#n103)\].
- **"A student visa lets you work."** Prospective students are routinely told this \[[N104](#n104)\]. Students may work only in breaks of over 7 days, up to 20 hours a week, in a few listed jobs, and those breaking the rules are deported (Can students work?) \[[54](#o54), [55](#o55), [N104](#n104)\].
- **"The approved agency list is on Facebook."** Posts in late 2025 that Malaysia had approved 25 Bangladeshi agencies were false; Malaysia had told neither the High Commission nor the Ministry (Scams) \[[56](#o56)\].

**Irregular routes and how they end.**

- **Overland through Thailand.** Brokers fly people to Cambodia; they cross into Thailand on foot, are moved in stages to the south, and are brought into Malaysia across the Kelantan border \[[N105](#n105), [N106](#n106)\]. The price is Tk 7 lakh (April 2026) or up to RM15,000 (July 2026), more than the legal route \[[N105](#n105), [N106](#n106), [N5](#n5)\]. In April 2026, 21 Bangladeshis found hidden in a resort in southern Thailand were charged there with illegal entry; in July 2026, 15 caught in a Cheras transit house on the day they crossed were taken to the Putrajaya immigration depot \[[N105](#n105), [N106](#n106)\].
- **Through the airport.** A "counter setting" syndicate sold entry packages on Facebook and Telegram: agents booked dates with immigration officers by WhatsApp, and the group, arriving as tourists, was sent to a "special counter", with officers taking RM200 to RM2,500 per worker; the MACC (Malaysian Anti-Corruption Commission) arrested 60 people over it in 2024, 49 of them immigration officers \[[N107 (2024)](#n107)\]. Travellers refused entry are sent back within 24 hours \[[N108](#n108)\]. **Need to know:** what a tourist-visa entry arranged for work in Malaysia sells for in Dhaka in 2026, and the street word for it.
- **Where overstaying ends.** Arrest, a crowded immigration depot where 465 detainees died in 2021 to 2025, and removal with no appeal (What can get you jailed) \[[41 (2006)](#o41), [N109](#n109), [N110](#n110)\]. Deportees pay their own tickets or their families or embassy do, and they are blacklisted \[[N111](#n111)\]. Forged papers bought to stay, such as fake e-PLKS copies at about RM150, carry jail and caning (Scams) \[[N112](#n112), [57](#o57)\].

## Visas not open to Bangladeshis

- **Visa-free visit:** Bangladeshis need a visa \[[3](#o3), [6](#o6)\]
- **ASEAN (Association of Southeast Asian Nations) visa-free visit of under a month:** ASEAN nationals only (except Myanmar) \[[6](#o6)\]
- **14-day visa-free visit:** Myanmar only \[[3](#o3)\]
- **Visa exemption for Indian nationals:** India only \[[6](#o6)\]
- **Visa exemption for Chinese nationals:** China only \[[3](#o3)\]
- **eNTRI (electronic travel registration):** Indian nationals only \[[58](#o58)\]
- **Visa on arrival:** Chinese and Indian nationals only \[[59](#o59)\]
- **Transit without visa (120 hours):** Suspended \[[60](#o60), [59](#o59)\]
- **Multiple-entry visa for an ordinary tourist or family visit:** Limited to eligible nationalities and to fly-and-cruise, business, medical and wedding trips \[[3](#o3)\]
- **Autogates (automatic passport gates) for visitors:** 10 countries only, not Bangladesh \[[61](#o61)\]
- **Foreign domestic helper (housemaid) pass:** Bangladesh is not an approved source country \[[7](#o7), [17](#o17)\]
- **Graduate Pass (one year's stay after a Malaysian degree):** Bangladesh is not among the 32 eligible nationalities \[[55](#o55), [62](#o62)\]
- **Visa exemption for diplomatic and official passports:** Not for ordinary passports \[[63](#o63)\]
- **Malaysia-Thailand Border Pass:** Malaysian citizens of the border states only \[[64](#o64)\]
- **APEC (Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation) Business Travel Card:** Malaysian citizens only \[[65](#o65)\]
- **Work & Holiday Visa:** Malaysian and Australian nationals aged 18 to 30 only \[[66](#o66)\]
- **Section 66 endorsement (to live or work in Sabah):** Malaysian civil servants and their dependants only \[[67](#o67)\]

## Work visa: company worker (PLKS)

The PLKS is valid 12 months at a time and renewed by the employer \[[5](#o5)\]. Bangladeshis may work only in manufacturing, construction, agriculture, plantation and services, except sectors where intake is frozen \[[5](#o5)\]. Jobs in Sabah and Sarawak fall under those state governments \[[5](#o5)\]. You must be 18 to 45 when the employer applies \[[5](#o5)\]. The employer pays the levy, the visa, the pass and a deposit \[[7](#o7)\]. Under the Bangladesh-Malaysia MoU, in force until 31 December 2026, the employer pays all Malaysian-side costs, including your airfare \[[29](#o29), [8 (2022)](#o8), [13](#o13)\].

**How a job reaches you.** A Bangladeshi recruiting agency on Malaysia's approved list collects the employer's job order (demand) \[[10](#o10)\], which the Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur attests \[[N113](#n113), [68](#o68)\], and selects workers under Bangladesh's rules \[[10](#o10)\]. BOESL or one of the 25 processing agencies handles the Malaysian visa paperwork \[[10](#o10)\]; the recruiting agency gets your BMET clearance under its own licence \[[10](#o10)\]. The employer applies for your visa through FWCMS only after you pass the medical check in Bangladesh \[[5](#o5), [26](#o26), [23](#o23)\].

**Is recruitment open now?** Recruitment restarted in October 2026 through agencies on Malaysia's list \[[10](#o10)\]. In their 8 April 2026 joint statement both governments said employers will bear the full cost of recruitment \[[N59](#n59)\]. Malaysia has agreed in principle to take 10,000 workers at no cost to them, airfare included, the first batch through BOESL \[[10](#o10), [13](#o13)\]; on 28 October the state minister estimated that batch would cost each worker Tk 1.55 to 1.60 lakh \[[N46](#n46)\]. The Ministry says no worker may be made to bear extra cost for the job \[[10](#o10)\]. **Need to know:** the most a private agency may take since the October 2026 reopening \[[N114](#n114), [N115](#n115)\], whether the employer still pays the airfare, and what terms the new MoU due on 31 December (Background) will set.

### Step 1: Find a real job

1. **Register on the Overseas Employment Platform (OEP),** BMET's website for job-seekers. Agencies must pick workers from BMET's database of registered job-seekers, and BMET clears no one outside it \[[21 (2021)](#o21), [29](#o29)\].

   You'll need: your NID (national ID card); a phone that can receive codes; a bKash, Nagad or other mobile-money account with Tk 200; and your passport details, education and work skills \[[69](#o69), [70](#o70), [2](#o2), [N116](#n116)\].

   1. In Google Chrome, open [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration) \[[70](#o70), [69](#o69)\].
   2. Type your name in English exactly as on your NID (no dots; spaces where the NID has them), your mobile and NID numbers, and your date of birth, division and district as on the NID \[[69](#o69), [70](#o70)\]. Type the captcha numbers, accept the terms and press "যাচাই করুন" (Verify) \[[69](#o69)\].
   3. Type the code (OTP) sent to your phone, press Submit, then "সংরক্ষণ করুন ও এগিয়ে যান" (Save and continue) \[[70](#o70)\].
   4. Fill in the form, then press "পেমেন্ট করতে এগিয়ে যান" (Proceed to payment), "পেমেন্ট করুন" (Pay), Mobile Banking and your service; enter your account number, the code and your PIN to pay the Tk 200 government fee \[[70](#o70), [2](#o2)\].
   5. Download and print your registration card, which carries your BMET number; you can download it again at [oep.gov.bd/employee-card](https://www.oep.gov.bd/employee-card) with that number, without logging in \[[70](#o70), [71](#o71), [N116](#n116)\].
   6. Take 2 printed copies of the registration slip to give your fingerprints \[[2](#o2)\] at any DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office, BMET's district office), TTC (Technical Training Centre) or IMT (Institute of Marine Technology) \[[72 (2023)](#o72)\]. DEMO addresses, phones and emails: [bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922e139933eb65569e2af6c](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922e139933eb65569e2af6c) \[[73](#o73)\]. **Need to know:** whether fingerprinting costs anything, the hours, and what you get back.

   **Check it is real:** register at employee.oep.gov.bd, the address BMET's district office gives \[[2](#o2)\]; use no middlemen and pay fees only through the proper channel, against a receipt \[[1](#o1)\]. **If it fails:** if "Verify" shows an error, check your name, NID number and date of birth match the NID exactly \[[70](#o70)\]; otherwise call the OEP hotline, 09614445271 \[[71](#o71), [74](#o74)\].

2. **Learn a trade if you need one.** BMET's TTCs teach 55 trades \[[1](#o1)\]; every TTC, with its principal's name, address, phone and email, is listed at [bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922dd2c933eb65569e13cd6](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922dd2c933eb65569e13cd6) \[[75](#o75), [76](#o76)\]. Each TTC posts admission notices with courses, minimum education, dates and fees; the Bangladesh-Korea TTC (BKTTC) in Dhaka, for example, posts them at bkttc.dhaka.gov.bd \[[77](#o77)\]. BKTTC's 3- or 6-month courses (360 or 720 hours), in trades such as welding and plumbing, end with an NSDA (National Skills Development Authority) skills certificate \[[78](#o78), [77](#o77)\].

   You'll need: your OEP registration; the course's minimum education (at BKTTC, JSC, the Junior School Certificate, for welding); and a current, clear photo in your OEP profile \[[77](#o77), [70](#o70)\].

   1. Log in at [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/login-with-otp) with your mobile number and the code sent to it \[[70](#o70)\].
   2. Open the training portal (ট্রেনিং পোর্টাল), then Training, then Trade/Skills; choose your division, district and centre and press Search \[[70](#o70), [77](#o77)\].
   3. Pick the trade, press Apply, check your photo matches your face now, then press Continue, "Process to Enroll" and Confirm; "My Course" shows your applications \[[70](#o70)\].
   4. Sit the admission test on the date in the notice; BKTTC posts results on its notice board and website and sends them by SMS \[[77](#o77)\].
   5. Pay the fee against a money receipt (BKTTC: Tk 748 for 3 months, Tk 1,484 for 6 months); courses run by funded projects are free \[[77](#o77), [1](#o1)\]. At BKTTC, regular trainees with at least 80% attendance get Tk 150 a day for transport and food when funds are allocated \[[77](#o77)\].

   **Scams:** if anyone offers a TTC place or exam pass for money, tell the nearest law-enforcement officer \[[19](#o19)\]. Complain about a TTC to its principal (phone and email on BMET's TTC list above) \[[76](#o76)\], then to BMET's Director (Employment), diremi@bmet.gov.bd, 01711111544 \[[1](#o1)\]. The government's migrant call centre, 16135, takes calls on any migration problem \[[79](#o79)\].

   **Construction jobs:** Malaysia requires foreign construction workers to hold a construction skills certificate (SKKP) from their own country, checked by CIDB (Malaysia's Construction Industry Development Board), and to finish an online safety and health course (eSICW) \[[18 (2024)](#o18)\]. The CIDB skill test runs at Khulna TTC (Teligati, Khulna; 041-2870047 or 01716966627; ttckhu@gmail.com) and is to start in Dhaka, Chattogram and Rajshahi \[[19](#o19), [76](#o76)\]. **Need to know:** whether the test still runs at Khulna TTC, how to register, the fee, and the certificate you get.

3. **Apply for government (BOESL) jobs.** BOESL posts Malaysia job circulars on [boesl.gov.bd](https://www.boesl.gov.bd/) \[[80](#o80)\]. Its portal, brms.boesl.gov.bd, has been closed since 11 October 2026 for a technical fault; follow its notice board and [facebook.com/boesl.gov.bd](https://www.facebook.com/boesl.gov.bd/) \[[81](#o81), [82](#o82)\]. Each circular sets its own rules; the example below is its 5 November 2026 zero-cost circular for DHL Supply Chain (Malaysia), for men, at RM1,700 basic pay a month \[[14](#o14)\].

   You'll need: a passport valid for at least 18 to 24 months; your OEP registration; your education and experience details; and, for the interview, 4 colour passport photos on white, colour copies of every passport you have held, original education and experience certificates, your NID, and a CV showing computer skills (MS Excel), numeracy, any driving licence and any overseas or warehouse experience \[[14](#o14)\].

   1. Check the circular's posts, age limit (22 to 35 in the example), education, skills and window \[[14](#o14)\].
   2. Apply online through the circular's link (in the example, [employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration](https://employee.oep.gov.bd/employee/registration)) with your passport number, education and experience, for one post only, within the window; incomplete or wrong applications are rejected \[[14](#o14)\].
   3. A software lottery picks a shortlist, which BOESL posts on its website; check your name there \[[14](#o14)\].
   4. If shortlisted, bring the documents above to the interview, where the employer's representative tests you and chooses \[[14](#o14)\]; a driving licence and work experience count in your favour \[[14](#o14)\].

   In the example, workers pay nothing at any stage \[[14](#o14)\]. Even selection does not guarantee a job \[[14](#o14)\]. BOESL's places are few: only 1,381 went through it at no cost from October 2022 to May 2024 \[[N19 (2024)](#n19)\], and UN experts said thousands recruited through it paid over five times the official fees \[[N117](#n117), [N66](#n66)\]. BOESL has no agents or sub-agents, never takes cash and takes money only after final selection, by bank pay order; gangs use its name to sell Malaysia jobs \[[83](#o83)\]. Office: Probashi Kallyan Bhaban (5th floor), 71-72 Eskaton Garden, Ramna, Dhaka; 02-58311838, 02-48319125, 02-48317515 or 01765411653; info@boesl.gov.bd \[[14](#o14), [83](#o83)\]. **Need to know:** which BOESL Malaysia jobs charge the worker, and how much \[[N115](#n115)\]; whether all 10,000 zero-cost places go through BOESL circulars \[[N114](#n114)\].

4. **Or go through a private agency.** Only agencies on Malaysia's list can recruit: open [fwcms.com.my/affiliates](https://fwcms.com.my/affiliates/#recruitment-agencies), choose Recruitment Agencies, then Bangladesh \[[26](#o26), [10](#o10)\].

   You'll need: the agency's name and RL number (the recruiting licence number every licensed Bangladeshi agency has) \[[84](#o84)\].

   1. Find the agency on the FWCMS list \[[26](#o26)\].
   2. On BMET's list, [oep.gov.bd/agencies](https://www.oep.gov.bd/agencies), check the same RL number shows status Active (not Expired, Cancelled or Suspended) and an unexpired validity date; note its registered address and office phone \[[84](#o84)\].
   3. Go to the agency at that address, not to a middleman's office \[[84](#o84), [1](#o1)\].
   4. Check the demand letter, contract, job details, salary and migration cost \[[19](#o19)\] against the permission letter (item 5).

   **What happens in practice:** being on both lists does not stop overcharging (Background, What workers pay) \[[N118 (2024)](#n118), [N5](#n5)\]. Some companies bring in more workers than they have work for, or promise jobs that don't exist, and supply them to other companies, hiding it with fake papers \[[N119](#n119), [N36](#n36)\]; in 2023 service employers brought in 142,204 foreign workers against 20,000 needed, some on quotas obtained with fake documents \[[N39 (2023)](#n39)\]. Some arrived with valid work permits to find no job (Step 7, item 1) \[[N120](#n120), [N95](#n95)\]; many left without seeing a contract \[[N98](#n98)\].

   **Which agencies.** Letter and worker counts here are our own count of the permission letters BMET posted from June 2023 to December 2025; workers means posts approved, not people who went (how we counted: the agencies list in this repo). BOESL and the 25 processing agencies handle the Malaysian visa processing (calling visa, e-visa); the other 312 agencies on the FWCMS list are associates, each listed under one of them, and cannot send workers directly \[[10](#o10), [26](#o26), [N2](#n2)\]. Between January 2004 and 23 August 2026, eight of the 25 sent 1,612 workers to Malaysia between them (Prothom Alo's analysis of BMET data); the other 17 had sent none (Background) \[[N2](#n2)\]. FWCMS and OEP give different addresses for several of the 25, including Rifa International (Banani on FWCMS, Purana Paltan on OEP), The Anchor Care (Savar DOHS, Dhamrai), Brothers Trading & Contracting (Banani, Shantinagar) and Goodness Service (Banani, Naya Paltan); check the RL number on OEP \[[26](#o26), [84](#o84)\]. The eight, and anything reported about them:

   - **General Trading Company (RL0320)** and **Rifa International (RL0693):** among 57 agencies the ACC asked BMET about on 23 July 2026, in a case alleging illegal wealth from trafficking women abroad with forged documents \[[N2](#n2), [84](#o84)\].
   - **United Gulf Services (RL1036):** owned by the wife of Abdul Hai, whose Greenland Overseas was in the earlier syndicate (Prothom Alo, September 2026) \[[N2](#n2), [84](#o84)\]; Greenland's owners are among those the ACC charged in September 2025 over Malaysia fees \[[N121](#n121)\].
   - **The Anchor Care Global Migration and Consultant (RL1765):** 11 of the 371 workers it had cleared went to Malaysia \[[N2](#n2), [84](#o84)\].
   - **Al-Shupto Overseas (RL0580)** (owner: Background), **Brothers Trading & Contracting (RL0671)**, **Country Employment Agency (RL1197)** and **Neighbour Associates (RL0746)**: nothing reported against them \[[N2](#n2), [84](#o84)\].

   Of the 17 without a Malaysia record, **Goodness Service (RL2068)** is also among the 57 in the ACC's inquiry, and by early October 2026 the High Commission had attested a demand under it for 28 workers for the NSK Trade City supermarket chain \[[N2](#n2), [N113](#n113), [84](#o84)\]. The FWCMS list still names associates whose licences are cancelled or suspended on OEP: RS International (RL1428, under The Anchor Care) and T S Overseas (RL1755, under Country Employment Agency), cancelled on 1 June 2026 over allegedly sending 30 young men to Russia's war front or risky work, and Triple A Overseas (RL2630, under BOESL; see "Before paying") \[[26](#o26), [N122](#n122), [84](#o84)\]. Bangladesh has no official grading of agencies, and the state minister said he could not confidently recommend one even to people from his own constituency \[[N123](#n123)\]. **Need to know:** which agencies place Bangladeshis in the promised job at a fair cost. Every licensed agency's record across all countries, including the 2022-24 syndicate agencies and their cases, is in the `agencies` folder of this knowledge base.

   **If a relative in Malaysia offers a job or a "visa".** A Malaysian job reaches a Bangladeshi only through an employer's approved quota and an agency on Malaysia's list \[[5](#o5), [10](#o10), [26](#o26)\]; check the offer as in items 4 to 6, whoever makes it. **Need to know:** how often relatives abroad resell calling visas, and what happens to the workers they send.

5. **Find and check the permission letter for your job.** For each Malaysia job order the Ministry or BMET issues a permission letter naming the agency and its RL number, the employer, the jobs, salary, age range, hours, food, housing, airfare, contract length and the most the agency may take from each worker \[[85](#o85), [29](#o29), [86 (2024)](#o86), [68](#o68)\].

   You'll need: the agency's RL number, and the employer's name and salary the agency gave you \[[85](#o85)\].

   1. Open [bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/miscellaneous-infos) and search "Malaysia" (or মালয়েশিয়া) \[[85](#o85)\].
   2. Open the entry with your agency's RL number; titles give the letter number and RL number, like "DN25-225 RL1274" \[[85](#o85)\].
   3. Compare its employer, salary and maximum fee with what the agency told you \[[29](#o29), [86 (2024)](#o86), [68](#o68)\]. Its English "Recruitment Permission Letter" page shows a permission ID, the block visa number (Malaysia's approval reference for the group of visas), the visa issue date and the employer's name and city; check they match your visa and demand letter \[[29](#o29), [87 (2023)](#o87)\].
   4. Take the demand letter or visa (if stamped, with a copy of the passport page) to your DEMO or BMET's head office, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000; BMET checks it free within 3 working days \[[19](#o19), [1](#o1), [88 (2021)](#o88)\]. If it is fake, complain as under "If an agency or broker takes your money" below.

   As of 7 November 2026 BMET's list had no Malaysia permission letter from the 2026 reopening; the newest was dated 27 February 2025 \[[89](#o89)\]. **Need to know:** whether BMET will post them.

6. **Check the employer exists** through SSM e-Info, the online shop of Malaysia's Companies Commission (SSM, Suruhanjaya Syarikat Malaysia) \[[51](#o51), [90](#o90)\].

   You'll need: the employer's full name or Malaysian registration number from the demand letter; an email address; and a credit or debit card or Malaysian online banking (FPX) \[[52](#o52)\].

   1. Register and log in at [ssm-einfo.my](https://www.ssm-einfo.my/) \[[52](#o52)\].
   2. Top up your account; card and FPX payments clear instantly \[[52](#o52)\].
   3. Search the name or number and buy the Company Profile, RM15.40 \[[52](#o52), [51](#o51)\].
   4. Save the PDF at once; it stays in Transaction History for only 7 days \[[52](#o52)\].
   5. Check the status, registered and business addresses and nature of business \[[90](#o90)\]; "Existing" means not being wound up, struck off or dissolved \[[90](#o90)\]. Compare name and address with the demand letter.

   **If it fails:** if you can't get the document, call the e-Info call centre, 1300 30 4636 \[[52](#o52)\]. **Need to know:** what to do if the company can't be found or doesn't match the demand letter.

**Before paying:**

- **Know what you should pay.** The Ministry's last Malaysia cap, Tk 78,990, covered everything the worker pays in Bangladesh (passport, medical, registration, welfare fee, insurance, smart card, pre-departure training, clothing, agency service charge and other costs); the employer paid the security deposit, pass, insurance and air tickets \[[8 (2022)](#o8)\]. Your job's permission letter states its maximum, in some letters nothing \[[85](#o85), [30 (2024)](#o30)\]. The agency must display in writing at its office the migration cost fixed for your destination and the monthly wage \[[28](#o28)\]; compare both with your permission letter (item 5).
- **Pay only by cheque, bank draft or pay order into the agency's own bank account, and keep every receipt** \[[29](#o29), [19](#o19)\]. Agencies and middlemen have started collecting passports from people who want to go \[[N1](#n1)\]; on 10 September 2026 a BMET mobile court sealed Triple A Overseas (RL-2630), fined it Tk 50,000 and seized passports it had collected with promises of Malaysian visas \[[N124](#n124)\]. The Ministry has warned against paying anything before the Malaysia process is finalised, or handing over a passport or money without confirming approval \[[N2](#n2), [12](#o12)\].
- **If a local broker brings you the job.** An agency may use only a sub-agent registered with BMET, and must list its registered sub-agents on its own website \[[27 (2023)](#o27), [28](#o28)\]. A registered sub-agent has an office with a signboard showing his name, registration number and date and the agency's name and licence number, and his BMET registration certificate on display inside \[[28](#o28)\]. The agency and the sub-agent are both liable for what he does, and a worker cheated by a sub-agent is compensated from the Tk 3 lakh security he lodges with the agency \[[28](#o28)\]. **Need to know:** whether BMET, any DEMO or any agency has yet published a list of registered sub-agents \[[28](#o28)\].
- **Get the contract and a Bangla translation before you go.** The agency must sign a contract with you before BMET clearance and give you a Bangla translation attested by BMET \[[29](#o29)\]; check the contract is attested by the destination country's embassy \[[19](#o19)\]. Keep your copy: a wage claim in Malaysia asks for it \[[91 (2017)](#o91)\], and some employers swap it on arrival for one the worker can't read \[[47](#o47)\]. **Need to know:** how to check the BMET attestation, and what to do if the agency won't hand over the contract.
- **If you need to borrow,** Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB), the government bank for migrant workers, lends up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral at 9% simple interest, repaid monthly over up to 3 years, and decides within 7 working days \[[32](#o32)\]. It lends only once you have a valid visa and does not arrange visas \[[N68 (2023)](#n68)\]. Informal loans are usually paid straight to a third party, leaving no record of what was paid \[[N7 (2024)](#n7)\].

  You'll need: the free form; 3 recent photos; copies of your NID, passport, visa and BMET card (BMET's smart card); the contract if you have it; 3 signed MICR cheque leaves from your own active bank account, with its statement; and a PKB savings account opened with at least Tk 500 \[[32](#o32)\]. Two guarantors: one well-off person (3 signed cheque leaves, a bank statement, proof of income) and one family member, each with a photo, NID and proof of address \[[32](#o32)\]; many applicants can't get the loan for lack of guarantors \[[N68 (2023)](#n68)\]. You and they must live permanently in the branch's area; you must be 18 to 60 with no loan in default \[[32](#o32)\].

  1. Apply at your area's PKB branch (list linked from PKB's loan page on mygov.bd), or online on the [mygov.bd](https://www.mygov.bd/services/info?id=BDGS-1726556874) migration loan page with "আবেদন করুন" (Apply) \[[92 (2019)](#o92)\]. You can apply only once until the loan is approved or refused \[[92 (2019)](#o92)\].
  2. Online, you get application and tracking numbers by SMS and can check status with your mobile and tracking numbers \[[92 (2019)](#o92)\].
  3. Pay 0.5% processing and 0.5% documentation fees plus VAT and Tk 900 per lakh for risk cover; bring 16 revenue stamps of Tk 10, and 3 adhesive stamps of Tk 100 per guarantor \[[32](#o32)\].
  4. PKB sends an SMS when the loan is approved \[[N68 (2023)](#n68)\].

  PKB helpline: 16238, or +880 9677-787878 from abroad \[[32](#o32)\]; loans department: DGM, Loans and Advances, 01716-175560, migration@pkb.gov.bd \[[93](#o93)\]. To complain, write to PKB's grievance officer, GM (Admin and Accounts), +88-02-48321195, gm2@pkb.gov.bd (30 working days), then the DMD, dmd@pkb.gov.bd (20), then the MD, md@pkb.gov.bd (60) \[[93](#o93)\]. **Need to know:** whether the BMET card, which comes with emigration clearance, is needed before approval \[[19](#o19)\], and what to do if the loan is refused.

**If an agency or broker takes your money and doesn't send you, or sends you to a different job:**

1. **Complain to BMET.** It takes complaints from people cheated before going, after going or after coming back; it is free and meant to be settled within 30 working days \[[1](#o1)\].

   You'll need: a copy of your NID or passport; every receipt, bank slip or mobile-money record of what you paid; the contract, visa or work permit if you have them; any letters, messages, audio or video from the agency or broker \[[N125](#n125), [94](#o94)\]; and written down, the agency's name and RL number, the name and phone of whoever took the money, dates and amounts, and the employer's name and address \[[50 (2024)](#o50), [94](#o94)\].

   1. Fill in BMET's complaint form against a recruiting agent, or write an application to BMET's Director General, with your proof \[[50 (2024)](#o50), [1](#o1), [88 (2021)](#o88)\]. Give your name, father's or husband's name, address, phone and email, the type of complaint (for example money misappropriation, or not sending workers abroad), everyone affected, the agency's address, and the details above \[[50 (2024)](#o50), [94](#o94)\].
   2. Hand it in at BMET's complaint cell, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000, and get the acknowledgement receipt with your complaint ID \[[1](#o1), [88 (2021)](#o88), [50 (2024)](#o50)\]. The responsible officer is BMET's Director (Employment), 01711111544, diremi@bmet.gov.bd \[[1](#o1)\]; BMET's main number is 02-49357972 \[[88 (2021)](#o88)\]. **Need to know:** whether the online form at ovijog.bmet.gov.bd, which takes evidence files and shows status, is in service \[[94](#o94)\].
   3. BMET investigates, hears both sides, usually in Dhaka, and recommends a decision to the Ministry, which decides \[[1](#o1), [N125](#n125)\]. If a guilty agency won't pay, BMET can take the money from its security deposit \[[N125](#n125)\].

   **What happens in practice:** it usually takes 4 months to a year \[[N97 (2024)](#n97)\]; workers with full documents get more back \[[N97 (2024)](#n97)\]. In 2023 BMET resolved about a third of 2,380 complaints and most victims got back less than half their loss; in August 2023 the average award was about Tk 74,000 against Tk 3.5 to 5 lakh paid \[[N97 (2024)](#n97)\]. Workers who paid middlemen with no written agreement have only their testimony, passport and BMET card to show \[[N97 (2024)](#n97)\]. A complaint proved against a registered sub-agent can cost him his registration \[[28](#o28)\].

2. **If BMET does not settle it,** go up the ladder.

   You'll need: your complaint ID and copies of everything you gave BMET \[[50 (2024)](#o50), [1](#o1)\].

   1. Write to BMET's Additional Director General (Employment), adgemp@bmet.gov.bd, 02-48321949 or 01720105492 \[[1](#o1)\].
   2. Then write to the Ministry's Secretary, secretary@probashi.gov.bd, who has 1 month \[[1](#o1)\].
   3. Then file on the Cabinet Division's Grievance Redress System at [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/), which has 3 months \[[1](#o1)\].

3. **Or complain straight to the Ministry.** It is free; action starts within 3 to 5 working days, both sides are heard, and it is meant to be settled within 30 working days \[[95](#o95)\].

   You'll need: an application, documents proving the complaint, your full address, and the agency's RL number and address \[[95](#o95)\].

   1. Send them to the Ministry's Enforcement-2 branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255, or to the Ministry at Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000 \[[95](#o95), [96](#o96)\].
   2. The branch also holds mobile courts (on-the-spot courts under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013) on its own initiative, free, within 7 working days, giving immediate remedies to people cheated before or after going \[[95](#o95)\].
   3. For advice, call the 24-hour migrant call centre, 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) \[[79](#o79)\].

4. **Courts and NGOs.** You can go to court alongside a BMET complaint, but many workers can't keep a case going without money or legal aid \[[N125](#n125)\]; with NGO legal help, some have sued agents or brokers under the 2013 Act \[[N97 (2024)](#n97)\], which since a 2024 amendment also covers sub-agents and other intermediaries \[[N126](#n126)\].

   You'll need: the same papers as for the BMET complaint \[[N125](#n125)\].

   1. Free legal advice: the government legal aid helpline, 16699 (+8809612316699 from abroad), run by NLASO (National Legal Aid Services Organization), which has a legal aid office in each district \[[97](#o97), [98](#o98)\].
   2. A village court can order money owed under a contract, receipt or other document repaid, up to Tk 3 lakh \[[N125](#n125)\].
   3. Migrant NGOs such as WARBE (Welfare Association for the Rights of Bangladeshi Emigrants) and the Ovibashi Odhikar Forum settle some disputes by village mediation and pass agency cases to BMET \[[N97 (2024)](#n97)\]; WARBE does not take fraud by local sub-agents \[[N125](#n125)\].

   **Need to know:** which court and case type to use against an agency or broker, and its cost; where to file at the village court and its fee; how to contact WARBE and the Ovibashi Odhikar Forum.

### Step 2: Medical check in Bangladesh (Bio-Medical)

Every worker must pass a medical check (the "Bio-Medical") at a Bangladesh centre accredited by FWCMS before the employer can apply for the visa in Step 3 \[[26](#o26)\]. A medical done before 31 October 2026 is not accepted for immigration clearance \[[12](#o12)\].

1. **Get the medical slip from your agency and test at an approved centre.** Your agency makes your MiGRAMS slip; no fee may be charged for it \[[31](#o31)\]. The agency gives the centre your papers and VDR visa papers (Step 3) \[[12](#o12)\]. **Need to know:** which comes first, the medical or the VDR (FWCMS requires the medical before the VDR application \[[26](#o26)\]; the Ministry requires VDR papers at the medical \[[12](#o12)\]), whether the centre asks for your passport, and how to check a MiGRAMS slip is real.

   You'll need: the medical slip and papers from your agency, and Tk 8,000, the fee for general workers \[[12](#o12), [31](#o31)\].

   1. Find a centre at [fwcms.com.my/affiliates](https://fwcms.com.my/affiliates/), under Accredited Medical Centres, Bangladesh (name and address of each) \[[26](#o26)\]. You may use any centre on the list, not only the one the agency names \[[31](#o31)\].
   2. Pay Tk 8,000, no more, and keep the receipt: a complaint needs evidence \[[31](#o31), [12](#o12)\].
   3. If you pass, the employer can apply for your visa \[[26](#o26)\]. If you move to another agency, the first must send your medical report on without delay \[[31](#o31)\].

   **Check it is real and scams:** brokers and dishonest agencies offer unauthorised medicals to get passports and money, and have charged for medicals for jobs that never came \[[12](#o12), [N63](#n63)\]; BAIRA and its members say that in the last round at least five times as many people did the Malaysia medical as went, and agencies took Tk 11,000 against the fixed Tk 6,500 \[[N63](#n63)\]. Hand over nothing until the centre is confirmed on the FWCMS list, and test only after the agency gives you a MiGRAMS slip for a real job \[[12](#o12), [26](#o26), [31](#o31)\]. The list is not enough: Human Diagnostic and Medical Centre, Box Culvert Road, Paltan, Dhaka, was still on it in November 2026 after a mobile court sealed it on 1 October for testing Malaysia-bound workers without approval \[[26](#o26), [N127](#n127), [N128](#n128)\]; the government says it will check listed centres and close those with irregularities \[[N129](#n129)\]. **Need to know:** where to check that an FWCMS-listed centre is also approved by the Ministry. A centre that breaks the Ministry's instructions loses its approval \[[12](#o12)\].

   **If a centre overcharges, harasses you or tests without approval, or a broker took your passport or money for a medical:**

   1. Email the Ministry at secretary@probashi.gov.bd with the date, place, names of the people or centre, and evidence (receipt, photos, messages) \[[12](#o12), [31](#o31)\]. For example: "To the Secretary, Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment. On [date], [centre, address] charged me Tk [amount] for my Malaysia medical, against the fixed Tk 8,000. My agency is [name, RL number]. Receipt attached. Please take action and return the extra money. [Name, phone, NID]." \[[31](#o31)\]
   2. Send the same to the Ministry's enforcement branch (sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255) with your full address and the agency's RL number and address; free, action within 3 to 5 working days, settled within 30 \[[95](#o95)\].
   3. If an agency is involved, also complain to the Director General of BMET at BMET's complaint cell, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka, or your DEMO, as in "If an agency or broker takes your money" (Step 1); free, BMET holds a hearing, 30 working days \[[1](#o1), [88 (2021)](#o88), [73](#o73), [19](#o19)\].
   4. If a broker kept your passport, file an online GD (general diary) at [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) (Step 4) \[[99](#o99)\].
   5. Call the 24-hour migrant call centre, 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) \[[79](#o79)\].

   The Ministry's mobile courts (on-the-spot courts under the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013) give a free remedy within 7 working days \[[95](#o95)\].

### Step 3: The employer gets your visa approval (VDR)

1. **The employer applies; you wait in Bangladesh.** After quota approval and paying the levy, the employer applies online through FWCMS for your VDR (Visa Dengan Rujukan, visa with reference; FWCMS calls it the Calling Visa) with the Home Ministry approval letter, levy receipt, a security deposit or guarantee, and your passport copy and photo; you stay in Bangladesh until it is approved \[[7](#o7), [5](#o5), [23](#o23)\]. The deposit, visa fee, pass and levy are the employer's costs, but some deduct the levy from wages, often secretly \[[7](#o7), [N74](#n74)\].

   **If the levy is taken from your wages:** complain to the Labour Department (JTK) through the Working for Workers (WFW) app (Android or iOS; confidential, trackable), 03-8000 8000 or jtksm@mohr.gov.my \[[100](#o100), [101](#o101), [91 (2017)](#o91)\]; tell the Bangladesh High Commission's labour wing (Step 7, item 1) \[[102](#o102)\]; call 16135 \[[79](#o79)\].

2. **Track the approval.** You'll need the employer's company registration number (or an individual employer's Malaysian identity card number) and the application number \[[103](#o103)\]. On IMI's [MyIMMs eServices](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main?pra) choose "Status Permohonan Pembantu Rumah Asing / Pekerja Asing" and enter both: BARU means processing, LULUS approved, BAYAR paid, CETAK visa sticker printed, TOLAK rejected, BATAL cancelled, TANGGUH deferred \[[103](#o103)\]. A complete eVDR (online VDR) should be approved within 5 working days for Peninsular Malaysia, 14 for Sabah, 30 for Sarawak \[[104](#o104)\].

   **If the VDR is refused:** an agency that fails to send workers gets no further Ministry permissions until it repays them \[[39 (2024)](#o39)\]; claim your money back through BMET and the Ministry as in Step 2 \[[1](#o1), [95](#o95)\].

3. **The entry visa is issued in Dhaka.** After IMI approves, the High Commission of Malaysia in Dhaka issues the VDR \[[53](#o53)\]. Worker visa applications go to its authorised visa application agencies, not to the High Commission itself \[[63](#o63), [53](#o53)\]. The Tk 600 visa fee is the employer's; an RM105 (about Tk 2,500) processing fee and the agency's own charge also apply \[[53](#o53), [7](#o7), [63](#o63)\]. **Need to know:** who gives the worker the VDR application number, who pays the processing fee and agency charge, and which agencies handle worker visas; ask the visa section, dhaka@imi.gov.my, +88 02 4108 1918, House 19, Road 6, Baridhara Diplomatic Enclave, Dhaka, Sunday to Thursday, 8.30 to 4.30 \[[63](#o63), [105](#o105)\].

### Step 4: Passport, police clearance and certificates

1. **Get an e-passport valid long enough:** at least 18 to 24 months left \[[14](#o14)\]; the work pass can be renewed only while your passport has more than 12 months left \[[5](#o5)\]. E-passports are issued for 5 or 10 years \[[106](#o106)\].

   You'll need: your original NID (compulsory over age 20) and, for some jobs, technical certificates such as a driving licence \[[107](#o107)\].

   1. On [epassport.gov.bd](https://www.epassport.gov.bd/) choose your present-address district and police station to get your Regional Passport Office, and fill in the form exactly as on your NID; nothing needs attesting \[[108](#o108), [107](#o107)\]. A Bangladeshi abroad can correct the age on a reissued passport by at most 8 years, on the basis of the birth registration \[[N130](#n130)\]. **Need to know:** whether brokers alter ages or names on passports for Malaysia jobs, and what that does to PLKS renewal and EPF withdrawal.
   2. Pay only through A-Challan, DGePay or ShurjoPay (card, bKash, Nagad or Rocket), or at a bank through A-Challan \[[106](#o106)\]. A 48-page passport costs Tk 4,025 (regular), Tk 6,325 (express) or Tk 8,625 (super express) for 5 years; Tk 5,750, Tk 8,050 or Tk 10,350 for 10 years \[[106](#o106)\].
   3. Enrol (photo and fingerprints) at that office with the printed appointment summary and form, your original NID, and the bank slip if you paid at a bank \[[109](#o109)\].
   4. With an online-verified NID there is no police verification; delivery takes up to 15, 7 or 2 working days from enrolment (regular, express, super express) \[[110](#o110), [106](#o106)\].

   **To complain:** passport helpline 16445 (09666716445 from abroad, 24 hours) \[[111 (2023)](#o111)\]; then the office's grievance redress officer, listed on its website (for example the Dhaka Secretariat office: 01538855234, rpo_secretariat@passport.gov.bd) \[[112](#o112)\]; then [grs.gov.bd](https://www.grs.gov.bd/) \[[112](#o112)\].

   **If it is lost or stolen:** file a GD quickly at the nearest police station \[[107](#o107)\], or at [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) or the Online GD app with your NID number, a mobile number (for an SMS code) and a live photo, entering what was lost, the district, the police station, and the time and place \[[99](#o99)\]. You get a digital GD copy with its number and the investigating officer's details (help: gd@police.gov.bd, 01320001428 on WhatsApp) \[[99](#o99)\]. Re-apply with copies of the old passport and the GD \[[107](#o107)\].

2. **Police clearance only if asked;** it is not on IMI's VDR list or BMET's clearance lists \[[7](#o7), [29](#o29), [1](#o1)\]. **Need to know:** whether Malaysian employers or agencies still ask for one.

   If asked, you'll need a passport valid at least 3 months, a present address that is one of your passport's addresses, and a scan of the passport data page attested by a first-class gazetted officer \[[113](#o113)\]. Apply at [pcc.police.gov.bd](https://pcc.police.gov.bd/) \[[113](#o113)\]; pay Tk 1,500 by treasury challan, code 1-7301-0001-2681, at any Bangladesh Bank or Sonali Bank branch, or online by card with a service charge \[[113](#o113)\]. The police verify you at your present address; track it by texting PCC S and your reference number to 26969 \[[113](#o113)\]. Collect it with a copy of your passport or application, or, if you are abroad, through someone with an attested authorisation letter giving their name and NID number \[[113](#o113)\]. Helpdesk: Sunday to Thursday, 9 am to 5 pm, 01320001824, 01320001825, help.pcc@police.gov.bd \[[113](#o113)\].

### Step 5: Check your visa and pass are real

1. **Agency, job and visa approval:** as in Step 1 (FWCMS list, OEP, BMET permission letter) and Step 3 (MyIMMs) \[[26](#o26), [84](#o84), [85](#o85), [103](#o103)\].
2. **At BMET:** take the visa and a copy of the stamped passport page to BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka (02-49357972), or your DEMO; BMET checks job visas and demand letters free within 3 working days, as BMET's online visa check doesn't cover Malaysia \[[19](#o19), [1](#o1), [88 (2021)](#o88), [73](#o73)\].
3. **Your BMET clearance (Step 6):** at [portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check) \[[114](#o114)\].
4. **After arrival:** IMI's [ePass check](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/checkEpassValid?semakan) shows name, nationality, pass type and expiry when you enter the ePass number (like EP141011C23105313148), passport number and nationality; "Pas Tidak Sah" means not valid \[[115](#o115)\]. **Need to know:** where a worker finds their ePass number.

**If any check shows a fake visa, pass, job or clearance:** stop paying and keep your passport \[[12](#o12)\]. Complain to BMET's complaint cell, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka, or your DEMO, with your passport copy, contract, visa, messages with the agency and proof of payment \[[1](#o1), [N125](#n125), [88 (2021)](#o88), [73](#o73)\], and send the same to the Ministry's enforcement branch (sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255) \[[95](#o95)\]. Ask the High Commission of Malaysia whether the visa is real (dhaka@imi.gov.my, +88 02 4108 1918) \[[63](#o63), [105](#o105)\]. Report whoever took your money or passport through the online GD \[[99](#o99)\], and call 16135 \[[79](#o79)\].

### Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance and the flight

1. **Do the compulsory 3-day pre-departure orientation (PDO)** at a BMET training institute (list with contacts on [BMET's website](https://bmet.gov.bd/pages/static-pages/6922dd2c933eb65569e13cd6)): going legally at low cost, avoiding broker fraud, and what to do after arrival \[[1](#o1), [75](#o75), [76](#o76), [116](#o116)\]. You'll need your OEP registration and passport or NID number \[[19](#o19), [117](#o117)\]. Apply on OEP, then download the enrollment card at [training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-enrollment-card](https://training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-enrollment-card) with your passport or NID number and the captcha \[[19](#o19), [117](#o117)\]. Attend all 3 days and pay against a money receipt (Tk 200 at Feni Technical Training Centre, which says to pay no more) \[[1](#o1), [118](#o118)\]. The only real certificate is the one you download at [training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate](https://training.oep.gov.bd/pdo-certificate) with your passport or NID number \[[119](#o119)\].

2. **Check the contract before clearance.** BMET's Malaysia permission letters require the agency to sign a contract with you before clearance and give you a BMET-attested Bangla translation \[[29](#o29)\]. Get it at least 2 days before you go and check the job, employer and address, workplace, length, monthly pay, hours, overtime, leave, airfare, medical care, injury and death compensation, food and housing, and return of the body \[[116](#o116)\]. **If there is no contract or translation, or it differs from your permission letter:** don't go to clearance; complain to BMET and the Ministry as in Step 5, and call 16135 \[[1](#o1), [95](#o95), [79](#o79)\].
