3. **BMET clearance (the smart card).** After the visa, the agency applies with, for Malaysia, the demand letter, power of attorney and skill certificates, for workers from BMET's database only; BMET's emigration branch clears within 3 working days \[[1](#o1), [29](#o29)\]. Your costs \[[8 (2022)](#o8)\]: Tk 5,500 to the Wage Earners' Welfare Board (the government's welfare board for migrant workers), a one-time Tk 1,000 insurance premium (5 years' cover, Tk 10 lakh), a Tk 250 clearance fee, and source tax of Tk 800 (unskilled) or Tk 1,200 (skilled) on a group visa, paid only through the proper channel against a receipt \[[120](#o120), [19](#o19), [1](#o1)\]. **Need to know:** whether the Tk 250 fee still applies; the Ministry reportedly abolished the smart card fee in December 2024 \[[N131 (2024)](#n131)\].

   **If you got your visa yourself,** apply at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka or the Chattogram DEMO with an application, the visa copy, any contract, an attested copy of your OEP card, a declaration on Tk 300 stamp paper and the original pay order for the welfare fee and insurance \[[1](#o1)\].

   **Check it is real** at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check; airport welfare desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet stop workers with no or a fake smart card \[[114](#o114), [1](#o1), [121 (2024)](#o121)\].

4. **The ticket.** The employer pays your air fare both ways, and BMET clears you only once that is ensured; an agency representative must fly with you or meet you in Malaysia \[[39 (2024)](#o39), [34 (2022)](#o34), [29](#o29)\].

   **Check the booking is real:** agencies have handed workers fake tickets minutes before the flight \[[N132 (2024)](#n132)\]. Enter the six-character PNR (booking reference) from your e-ticket on the airline's site: Biman's Manage Booking, with your last name \[[N133](#n133)\]; Malaysia Airlines' [Manage Booking](https://www.malaysiaairlines.com/ph/en/plan-trip/booking-and-services.html), with your last name \[[N134](#n134)\]; US-Bangla's [Manage Your Booking](https://usbair.com/manage-your-booking), or use the ticket number \[[N135](#n135)\]; AirAsia's [Check-in](https://www.airasia.com/check-in/en/gb), with your first name or surname \[[N136](#n136)\].

   **Scam:** last-minute demands, such as Tk 50,000, for the ticket, which is the employer's cost \[[N132 (2024)](#n132), [39 (2024)](#o39)\]. **If asked for ticket money or the ticket is fake:** don't pay; complain to BMET and the Ministry as in Step 5 with the ticket and agency details, and call 16135 \[[1](#o1), [95](#o95), [79](#o79)\].

5. **Before you fly,** carry your passport, contract, visa, BMET clearance, medical report, ticket and receipts \[[116](#o116)\], and the Bangladesh High Commission's contacts: No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur; 03-2604 0946, 48 or 49; labour wing +60 3 2604 0934 \[[122](#o122), [102](#o102)\]. Leave one set of copies at home and carry 3, and have a bank account in your name and one in a family member's \[[19](#o19)\]. Keep money and papers in your cabin bag and the checked bag within 20 kg \[[116](#o116)\]. Never carry a packet whose contents you don't know \[[19](#o19)\].

6. **Register the free MDAC (Malaysia Digital Arrival Card)** within 3 days before arrival (the day you submit counts) at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main); workers on a VDR are not exempt \[[4](#o4), [123](#o123), [N137](#n137), [124 (2024)](#o124), [125](#o125), [126](#o126)\]. The form asks your name, passport number and expiry, date and place of birth, nationality, sex, email, mobile, arrival and departure dates, flight number, mode of travel and last port of embarkation \[[126](#o126)\]; you get a PIN, and check the registration under "Check Registration" with your passport number, nationality and PIN \[[127](#o127)\]; show it with your passport at the Immigration counter \[[125](#o125)\]. **Scam:** fake MDAC sites charge for the card (IMI has flagged malaysiacard.com; sponsored search ads lead to others), so use only the IMI address \[[124 (2024)](#o124), [N137](#n137), [N138](#n138)\].

### Step 7: After arrival

1. **Wait for your employer at the airport.** Enter only through official entry points with your VDR and visa; the employer must collect you within 6 hours, take you to the hostel and pay your transport to the workplace \[[5](#o5), [128 (2024)](#o128), [37 (2022)](#o37)\]. **If no one comes or there is no job:** call the agency representative who must meet the flight \[[29](#o29)\], then the High Commission's labour wing (+603 2604 0934, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd, or +601 2290 3252, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd) \[[102](#o102)\], and 16135 (+880 9610102030 from abroad) \[[79](#o79)\].

   **If there is no job.** In one case 161 Bangladeshis promised RM1,500 construction jobs waited seven months without work or pay while the employer held their passports and asked RM6,000 for them \[[N40 (2024)](#n40)\]. Don't take other work, which ends the pass (What can get you jailed) \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]; file at the Labour Office (If you aren't paid, route 3) and get free help (route 4): in that case the Labour Department told the employer to pay wages owed since arrival and either send the workers home or help them find new employers \[[N40 (2024)](#n40)\]. In December 2023, 171 Bangladeshis were arrested in Kota Tinggi, Johor, when they went to the police to report their agent \[[N39 (2023)](#n39)\]. **Need to know:** whether a worker whose pass was never issued can report to the police without being arrested.

2. **FOMEMA medical within 30 days.** FOMEMA appoints the clinics for foreign workers' medical checks \[[7](#o7)\]. The employer must take you to a FOMEMA clinic within 30 days of arrival and pay the fee (RM207 for men, RM217 for women (which includes a pregnancy test), plus 8% sales and service tax) \[[5](#o5), [37 (2022)](#o37), [129](#o129)\]. It includes a chest X-ray, blood tests and a urine drug test for opiates, methamphetamine and cannabis; results usually come within 7 working days \[[130](#o130), [131](#o131)\]. Check yours on IMI's [FOMEMA Application Status](https://eservices.imi.gov.my/myimms/FomemaStatus?lang=en) with your passport number and BANGLADESH \[[42](#o42)\]; the employer checks it on the FOMEMA portal, and neither of you sees the report itself \[[132](#o132), [N81](#n81)\].

   **If the employer hasn't taken you within 30 days:** without a FOMEMA fit result there is no work pass (PLKS) \[[5](#o5)\]; complain to the Labour Department (WFW app or 03-8000 8000), tell the labour wing (item 1) and call 16135 \[[100](#o100), [101](#o101), [91 (2017)](#o91), [102](#o102), [79](#o79)\].

   **If you are found unfit:** no PLKS; employers complain that FOMEMA rejects workers even for treatable conditions such as diabetes, high blood pressure and high cholesterol \[[N81](#n81)\]. You are sent home with a Check-Out Memo (the employer's application for a foreign worker to leave), and the employer pays the fare \[[5](#o5), [7](#o7), [39 (2024)](#o39)\]. Only the employer or agency can appeal, for a fee, on portal.fomema.my within 2 weeks; the case closes if extra test results don't arrive within 4 weeks \[[133](#o133)\]. Confirmed tuberculosis, malaria, pregnancy, syphilis, HIV and hepatitis B can't be appealed \[[133](#o133)\]. Ask the employer in writing to appeal, and tell the labour wing \[[102](#o102)\]. Using a false passport to sit FOMEMA again carries 2 to 10 years' jail (What can get you jailed) \[[57](#o57)\].

3. **PLKS sticker and i-Kad.** The sticker is printed once IMI approves your PLKS and the employer pays (CETAK on MyIMMs) \[[103](#o103)\]. Every PLKS holder gets an i-Kad (foreign worker identity card), free and without applying; the employer collects it from Immigration 7 days after IMI's email \[[134](#o134)\]. Carry it always and show it when asked \[[134](#o134)\]. If you don't get it, complain to the Labour Department (WFW app or 03-8000 8000), then the labour wing \[[100](#o100), [101](#o101), [91 (2017)](#o91), [102](#o102)\].

4. **Keep your own passport.** Your employer may not keep your passport; hand it over only for the medical, PLKS, i-Kad and renewal, and get it back \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [37 (2022)](#o37)\]. Holding someone else's passport without lawful authority carries a fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both \[[46 (1997)](#o46), [135](#o135)\]. **If your employer keeps it:** ask for it back in writing and keep a copy \[[34 (2022)](#o34)\]; complain to the Labour Department (WFW app, 03-8000 8000 or jtksm@mohr.gov.my) and tell the labour wing \[[100](#o100), [101](#o101), [91 (2017)](#o91), [102](#o102)\]; for unpaid wages, see If you aren't paid. **Need to know:** whether a report to the Malaysian police or IMI gets a passport back, and where to make it.

5. **Wages into your own bank account,** monthly by the 7th of the next month; cash only if you ask in writing and the Labour Department approves \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [136 (2022)](#o136)\]. The employer is expected to open it \[[128 (2024)](#o128)\]. At Maybank, for example, open it at a branch (the app needs a Malaysian identity card) with your passport and at least RM250, and perhaps your work permit, visa and an employer's letter \[[137](#o137)\].

6. **SIM card.** Register every prepaid SIM yourself, in person, at an operator's service centre or authorised dealer with your original passport and work-permit document \[[138](#o138)\]. The limit is 2 prepaid SIMs per operator; activation is confirmed within 12 hours \[[138](#o138)\]. Don't use a SIM that was active before you registered it \[[138](#o138)\]. Don't give documents or codes to anyone offering to "help"; each operator checks free which numbers are in your name \[[138](#o138)\].

7. **Social security, insurance and the retirement fund.** The employer must register you with PERKESO (SOCSO, Malaysia's social security organisation) for the Employment Injury Scheme (accidents, work diseases, death at work; employer pays 1.25% of wages) and the Invalidity Scheme (critical illness or death in Malaysia before 60 from any cause; employer 0.5%, you 0.5%) \[[36](#o36)\]. You get a 12-digit social security number; SOCSO: 1-300-22-8000 \[[139 (2019)](#o139), [140 (2022)](#o140)\]. Not registering or paying is an offence for the employer \[[36](#o36)\].

   SPIKPA, the compulsory hospital and surgery insurance, costs RM120 a year; the employer buys it with the PLKS application and may deduct it from wages with Labour Department permission, and cover starts once you are registered and it is paid \[[141](#o141), [N139](#n139), [142](#o142)\]. At a government hospital show your valid passport; you pay no deposit and only the part of a bill above the cover limit \[[142](#o142)\]. An unpaid government hospital bill blocks your PLKS renewal \[[142](#o142)\].

   You and your employer each pay 2% of your wages into the EPF (Employees Provident Fund, the retirement fund); the employer may deduct your share, and domestic helpers are not covered \[[35](#o35)\]. EPF registers you automatically; visit any EPF office, no appointment, to update your details and give your thumbprint \[[35](#o35)\].

   **When you leave Malaysia, take out your EPF savings (Leaving Country Withdrawal)** \[[143](#o143)\]: get the Check-Out Memo from Immigration as proof of leaving \[[143](#o143), [35](#o35)\]; prepare form KWSP 9K (AHL) and its checklist, a passport copy and a bank document confirming the account holder, with copies certified by an EPF officer, your High Commission or your employer \[[143](#o143), [35](#o35)\]; apply at an EPF office's self-service terminal, as it can't be done online \[[143](#o143)\]. The money goes into an active account in your name at an EPF panel bank \[[143](#o143)\]. After leaving, post the application to EPF, Jabatan Pengurusan Transaksi, Menara KWSP, No.1 Persiaran Kwasa Utama, Kwasa Damansara, Seksyen U4, 40150 Shah Alam, Selangor \[[143](#o143)\]. EPF: +603-8922 6000 \[[143](#o143)\].

8. **If you came without BMET clearance,** join the Wage Earners' Welfare Board for its insurance and help: apply at [portal.wewb.gov.bd](https://portal.wewb.gov.bd/) with your passport and work permit; the labour wing processes it, for the ringgit equivalent of Tk 6,500, life insurance included \[[114](#o114)\]. Workers with BMET clearance are already covered (check at portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check) \[[114](#o114)\].

### Your rights at work

Your terms may be no worse than the Employment Act 1955 (the main labour law in Peninsular Malaysia): a worse term is void and replaced by the Act, and no contract may stop you joining a registered trade union \[[37 (2022)](#o37), [33 (2023)](#o33)\]. The employer or its agent must explain the terms when you are selected, the contract you sign must carry the same terms, and you are entitled to your own copy from the employer named on your work permit \[[37 (2022)](#o37)\]. Under the Bangladesh-Malaysia agreement the contract is in Malay, Bangla and English, and the English counts \[[34 (2022)](#o34)\]. These rights are Peninsular Malaysia's and Labuan's; Sabah and Sarawak have their own Labour Ordinances \[[44](#o44)\]. For any broken right, use the routes under If you aren't paid.

**Hours and overtime.** At most 8 hours a day and 45 a week, no more than 5 hours in a row without a 30-minute break, and never over 12 hours a day except in emergencies \[[33 (2023)](#o33), [136 (2022)](#o136)\]. Overtime is only at the employer's request with your agreement, paid at least 1.5 times the hourly rate (for monthly pay: the monthly wage divided by 26, then by your normal daily hours) \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [33 (2023)](#o33)\].

**Rest days and holidays.** One rest day a week, on which you can't be made to work except in continuous shift work or emergencies \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [33 (2023)](#o33)\]. Work on it within normal hours pays, on monthly pay, half a day's pay for up to half the normal hours and a full day's for more, on top of the monthly wage, and on daily pay one or two days' wages; beyond normal hours, at least 2 times the hourly rate \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. 11 paid public holidays a year; working one earns 2 extra days' wages, and overtime that day at least 3 times the hourly rate \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. Absence without consent on the working day before or after a holiday loses that holiday's pay unless you have a reasonable excuse \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\].

**Leave.** Paid annual leave is 8, 12 or 16 days a year (under 2, 2 to 5, over 5 years' service) \[[144](#o144)\]; paid sick leave 14, 18 or 22 days on the same bands, plus up to 60 days if a doctor certifies hospital care, so 74, 78 or 82 days in all \[[33 (2023)](#o33), [145 (2024)](#o145)\]; it needs a doctor's certificate and notice to the employer within 48 hours, or it counts as absence \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. Maternity leave is 98 days \[[144](#o144)\]. A married man gets 7 days' paid paternity leave per birth, up to 5 births, after 12 months' service, if he tells the employer at least 30 days before the expected birth \[[136 (2022)](#o136)\].

**Pregnancy.** A pregnant worker can't be dismissed except for wilful breach of contract, misconduct or closure of the business \[[145 (2024)](#o145), [136 (2022)](#o136)\]; for the work pass, see Bringing your family.

**Pay.** Wages are paid monthly by the 7th of the next month into a bank account in your name, in cash only if you ask in writing and the Labour Department approves; overtime, rest-day and holiday pay by the end of the next wage period \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [33 (2023)](#o33), [136 (2022)](#o136)\]. Your basic wage must meet the minimum wage and equal a local worker's for the same job \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [37 (2022)](#o37)\]. The employer may not dictate where, how or with whom you spend it \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. The minimum is RM1,700 a month or RM8.72 an hour (by the day: RM65.38 on a 6-day week, RM78.46 on 5 days, RM98.08 on 4) \[[9 (2024)](#o9)\], for every employer, including for piece, task, trip or commission pay \[[9 (2024)](#o9)\]. The government has agreed to raise it to RM2,000 a month from June 2027, with small and medium businesses selling under RM50 million a year exempted \[[38](#o38)\]. **Need to know:** whether the Minimum Wages Order setting RM2,000 has been made.

**Deductions.** Only what the Employment Act allows \[[144](#o144), [33 (2023)](#o33)\]. Rent, food or payments to anyone else need your written request and Labour Department approval, given for rent and food only if they benefit you \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. All deductions together may not exceed half a month's wages \[[33 (2023)](#o33), [34 (2022)](#o34)\]. In Sarawak nothing may be deducted for recruitment or repatriation, and foreign workers get local workers' rate \[[45](#o45)\].

**Housing.** Employer housing, under Act 446 (the Employees' Minimum Standards of Housing, Accommodations and Amenities Act 1990), needs a Labour Department Certificate for Accommodation, men and women live separately, and the person in charge must take ill workers to a clinic or hospital \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [37 (2022)](#o37), [146 (2021)](#o146)\]. Each worker gets his own bed with a 4-inch mattress, pillow, blanket and lockable cupboard \[[N70 (2020)](#n70)\], at least 3.6 square metres in a bedroom (3 in a dormitory), and no more than 6 share a bathroom and toilet (15 in a dormitory) \[[N70 (2020)](#n70)\]. On estates and other workplaces outside city, municipal and Federal Territory areas, employer housing must have free piped water and electricity, and no rent may be charged \[[146 (2021)](#o146)\]. In practice many employers still house workers in hostels that break Act 446, and some leave workers to buy their own beds and kitchen equipment \[[N140](#n140)\]. Elsewhere rent can be taken from wages only within limits set by regulations \[[146 (2021)](#o146)\]; the most is RM100 a month per worker \[[N70 (2020)](#n70)\]. If the job ends with under 4 weeks' notice, you may stay up to 4 weeks \[[146 (2021)](#o146)\]. The Labour Department can inspect at any time without notice \[[146 (2021)](#o146)\]. **Need to know:** whether the RM100 limit has changed since 2020.

**Your passport.** The employer may not keep it (Step 7, item 4) \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [46 (1997)](#o46)\]; if it is kept, you can also complain to SUHAKAM (the Human Rights Commission of Malaysia, If you aren't paid, route 4) \[[147](#o147)\].

**Forced labour.** An employer who threatens, deceives or forces you to work and stops you leaving can be fined up to RM100,000, jailed up to 2 years, or both \[[136 (2022)](#o136)\]. Call the 24-hour emergency line of Tenaganita (a Malaysian NGO for migrant workers), the labour wing or SUHAKAM (If you aren't paid) \[[N141](#n141), [148](#o148), [147](#o147)\].

**Sexual harassment.** Report it to the Labour Department's Director General, who can direct the employer to inquire \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. A woman worker or a close relative in Bangladesh can also apply free to the Ministry, aswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, with passport, smart card and residence-permit copies, the agency's details and the accused person's contacts; it acts within 7 working days \[[95](#o95)\]. **Need to know:** how a worker files a complaint with the Director General.

**Ending the job.** Under the agreement contract, either side gives 2 months' notice or 2 months' wages \[[34 (2022)](#o34)\]. Not paying wages counts as the employer breaking the contract; either side may end it without notice for a wilful breach by the other, and you may leave at once if you or your dependants face immediate violence or disease you didn't agree to risk \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. Being absent more than 2 working days in a row without leave or a reasonable excuse told to the employer counts as you breaking it \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. Dismissal for misconduct needs an inquiry first; suspension during it lasts at most 2 weeks on at least half pay \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. In a redundancy, foreign workers in a similar job go before any local worker \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. After 12 months, if the employer ends the job (except for misconduct), you get at least 10 days' wages per year of service under 2 years, 15 days for 2 to 5 years, 20 days for 5 or more, pro rata \[[N142](#n142), [33 (2023)](#o33)\].

**Savings and pension.** The employer registers you with EPF (the retirement fund) and pays 2% of your wage, with 2% deducted from you, by the 15th of the next month; you withdraw it all when you leave for good (Leaving at the end of the job) \[[35](#o35), [143](#o143)\]. PERKESO (social security, see Step 7) covers work accidents, and critical illness or death in Malaysia before 60 from any cause, and pays invalidity and survivors' pensions (If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job) \[[36](#o36)\]. Bangladesh's Probash pension is open to Bangladeshis aged 18 to 50 working abroad \[[149](#o149)\]:

1. You'll need: a bank account, mobile number, email and a foreign-currency card \[[149](#o149)\]. You can name a nominee, who gets the money if you die \[[149](#o149)\].
2. Register yourself, in your own name, at [upension.gov.bd](https://www.upension.gov.bd/) under Probash, with no third person; your ID, rate and payment dates come by SMS and email, and any Sonali Bank branch is the front office \[[149](#o149)\].
3. Pay Tk 1,000, 2,000, 5,000, 7,500 or 10,000 a month, 3, 9 or 12 months at a time, by card to Sonali Bank; 2.5% is added from abroad \[[150](#o150), [149](#o149)\].
4. What you get: after 10 years of unbroken payments, a monthly pension into your bank from age 60 without applying (Tk 1,000 a month for 20 years gives about Tk 4,927 a month); if you die, all you paid plus profit goes to your nominee \[[149](#o149), [150](#o150)\]. On returning home you can switch to the Progoti or Surokkha scheme \[[149](#o149)\].

**Need to know:** the full list of details the form asks for, whether registering costs anything, how to pay without a foreign-currency card, and what to do if registration or payment fails.

### How long you can stay

**12 months at a time, up to 10 years.** The PLKS lasts 12 months \[[5](#o5)\]. Only the employer renews it, from 3 months before expiry, and pays any late penalty \[[7](#o7), [34 (2022)](#o34)\]. Renewal needs a passport valid over 12 months, a FOMEMA medical every year, and SPIKPA hospital insurance (except in plantations) \[[5](#o5), [131](#o131), [24](#o24)\]. A new 48-page, 5-year e-passport from the High Commission in Kuala Lumpur costs a worker RM172 (RM140 fee plus a RM32 ESKL charge for the appointment, enrolment and delivery) \[[151](#o151)\]; Bangladesh missions charge workers USD 50 for 10 years (express USD 45 for 5 years, USD 75 for 10) \[[106](#o106)\]. A renewal filed after expiry counts as overstay \[[5](#o5)\]. The limit is 10 years in total, or 3 for workers registered under the 6P legalisation programme \[[5](#o5), [24](#o24), [37 (2022)](#o37)\].

**If the employer doesn't renew,** you risk arrest \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. Check your pass on Immigration's ePass check (Step 5) \[[115](#o115)\]; if it is near expiry and unrenewed, email the labour wing and contact Tenaganita (If you aren't paid) \[[148](#o148), [N143](#n143)\]. **Need to know:** whether the Labour Department takes a complaint about a pass that isn't renewed, and whether Bangladeshi workers' renewals are still done only at Immigration Putrajaya \[[152 (2021)](#o152)\] now that work-pass applications go through FWCMS \[[153](#o153)\].

**No family, marriage or permanent residence.** PLKS holders can't bring family, may not marry a Malaysian or another foreigner while on the pass, can't apply for permanent residence, and must go home when the pass ends \[[5](#o5), [7](#o7), [N144 (2021)](#n144), [N145 (2021)](#n145)\].

### What can get you jailed

**No valid pass or passport.** Entering illegally, or failing to show a valid passport and pass when asked: fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both, and up to 6 strokes of the cane, or a compound (out-of-court fine) up to RM3,000 \[[57](#o57)\]. Carry your i-Kad at all times \[[134](#o134)\]. Overstaying an expired or cancelled pass: fine of at least RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail or both, or a compound up to RM3,000 \[[41 (2006)](#o41), [48](#o48), [57](#o57)\]. Breaking a pass condition, such as working for another employer, ends the pass at once \[[41 (2006)](#o41), [5](#o5)\]; PLKS holders may not work as frontliners (customer-facing staff) \[[5](#o5)\].

**After arrest.** Someone whose pass has expired or been cancelled is removed whether or not prosecuted, held as long as removal takes, with no appeal \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. Detainees in Immigration depots may contact family by phone, letter or visit with the commander's approval \[[154 (2021)](#o154), [N110](#n110)\]. On entering a depot your biometrics are taken and you are blacklisted \[[N89 (2022)](#n89)\]. Depots have held more people than their capacity (22,045 against 21,530), 465 detainees died in them in 2021 to 2025, and former detainees describe medical care denied or delayed \[[N109](#n109), [N110](#n110)\].

1. Visitors first need an Immigration permission letter, from the Depot and Deportation Management Division at Immigration headquarters, Putrajaya, for Klang Valley (the Kuala Lumpur area), or the state Immigration Enforcement Division elsewhere; give the detainee's body or police report number \[[154 (2021)](#o154)\]. **Need to know:** whether the letter costs anything and how long it takes.
2. Call the High Commission's consular line, +603 2604 0949, or email the labour wing; its officials visit camps \[[122](#o122), [148](#o148), [N96](#n96)\].
3. Going home needs only an air ticket if the passport is valid, and the camp arranges the flight and the trip to the airport; otherwise the camp gets a free travel permit from the High Commission \[[148](#o148)\].

**False papers.** Making, forging or using a forged visa or pass: fine of at least RM30,000, 5 to 10 years' jail, up to 6 strokes \[[57](#o57)\]. A false passport: RM15,000 to RM100,000, 2 to 10 years, up to 6 strokes \[[57](#o57)\]. Letting someone else hold your passport, intending or knowing it may be used by another person, is also an offence \[[46 (1997)](#o46)\].

**Drugs.** Trafficking means death or life imprisonment, plus at least 12 strokes if not sentenced to death \[[N146](#n146), [155 (2023)](#o155), [156](#o156)\]. Never carry a packet whose contents you don't know \[[19](#o19)\].

**Banned goods.** Obscene pictures or films on any media, daggers, flick knives and cloth printed with Quran verses may not be brought in; declare anything dutiable and expect your bags to be opened \[[156](#o156)\].

### If you aren't paid

**First collect proof.** Photograph your contract, payslips, attendance records, passport and pass, and note the employer's name and address \[[91 (2017)](#o91), [N84 (2021)](#n84)\]. Find the company's registered name and address on [SSM e-Info](https://www.ssm-einfo.my/), the Companies Commission's search, by name or registration number; a profile costs RM15.40 \[[91 (2017)](#o91), [51](#o51)\]. Without a written contract you are still presumed an employee if the other person controls your work or hours, provides tools, or pays you regularly as most of your income \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. If you work for a subcontractor, the main contractor is also liable for up to 3 months' unpaid wages if you complain within 90 days of the due date \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. If the employer seems about to run off to avoid paying, complain to a Magistrate, who can require a bond or order arrest \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]; **Need to know:** how a worker reaches a Magistrate. Use the routes below in order; several at once is fine.

**1. Working for Workers (WFW) app**, the Ministry of Human Resources' complaint channel \[[100](#o100), [101](#o101)\].

You'll need: a smartphone; your name, mobile, passport number, gender and nationality; the company's name, phone and workplace address (state, district and postcode) \[[N147 (2021)](#n147)\].

1. Install "WFW" from Google Play or the App Store (official page: [workforworkers.com.my](https://www.workforworkers.com.my/sapn-portal/index)) and register \[[101](#o101), [157](#o157), [N147 (2021)](#n147)\].
2. Choose the complaint type (unpaid, late or below-minimum wages, no overtime pay, unlawful deductions, housing) and describe what happened \[[N147 (2021)](#n147), [100](#o100)\].
3. What you get back: the complaint goes to the labour authorities, your identity is kept confidential, and you track it in the app; no Labour Office visit is needed \[[101](#o101), [157](#o157)\]. At launch the ministry said complaints would be dealt with within 3 to 7 days \[[N147 (2021)](#n147)\].
4. Reality check: claims without payslips are hard to prove; complainers have been warned off, falsely reported absent or had visas cancelled (If your employer reports you absent (lari)) \[[47](#o47), [N99 (2024)](#n99), [N62](#n62)\].

**Need to know:** whether the app costs anything or works in Bangla, and what to do if a complaint gets no response.

**2. The High Commission's labour wing**, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur \[[122](#o122)\]. You'll need: the details on the route 5 checklist \[[95](#o95)\].

1. Email counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, ss.lab.kl@gmail.com or fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com, or call +601 2290 3252; a group can send one email \[[148](#o148), [102](#o102), [N148](#n148)\].
2. What happens: it calls the employer in, visits the workplace and attends Labour Department hearings, and takes ignored cases to the Ministry of Human Resources \[[N148](#n148), [N149 (2023)](#n149)\].
3. If it doesn't act, apply to the Ministry (route 5), which tells it to \[[95](#o95)\].
4. Reality check: it says it settled over 330 of about 450 complaints since 2022 by mediation \[[N94](#n94)\], recovered RM11.42 million on 2,857 compensation claims in 2025 and RM5.89 million on 1,381 claims in January to June 2026 \[[N150](#n150)\], and gave about 400 workers consular and legal help in January to June 2026 \[[N96](#n96)\]; other workers say complaints to it and the Labour Department brought nothing until a migrant rights group stepped in \[[N151](#n151), [N71](#n71)\].

**Need to know:** how long the labour wing takes to reply and whether it charges anything.

**3. Labour Court claim**, the Labour Department's (JTK's) free wage tribunal, with no upper limit \[[91 (2017)](#o91), [33 (2023)](#o33)\].

You'll need: your passport (or a copy), the employer's name and address, payslips, contract or appointment letter, any termination letter, arrival date and witnesses' names \[[91 (2017)](#o91), [N84 (2021)](#n84), [33 (2023)](#o33)\].

1. For free advice call 03-8000 8000. Then give a sworn statement to an officer at the nearest Labour Office (Pejabat Tenaga Kerja), or complain by letter or to jtksm@mohr.gov.my \[[91 (2017)](#o91), [33 (2023)](#o33), [N84 (2021)](#n84)\].
2. The officer sets a mention date (a first short hearing), summons the employer and tells you when and where; bring your witnesses \[[91 (2017)](#o91), [33 (2023)](#o33)\].
3. Attend every date; an absent employer can lose by default \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. Workers with the same claim can file together through representatives \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]. No lawyers, but a trade union can help \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\].
4. What you get back: a written order to pay, with up to 8% yearly interest, enforced through the courts (for example by seizing property) if unpaid \[[33 (2023)](#o33), [91 (2017)](#o91)\]. Either side can appeal to the High Court within 14 days \[[91 (2017)](#o91)\].
5. Time: about a month if settled, about 3 months if heard \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\]. Challenge a misconduct dismissal within 60 days of being told of it \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\].
6. If your pass ends mid-case: take a Labour Office letter showing the pending case to Immigration's Foreign Workers Division for a Special Pass (one month, extendable; usually granted with at least two extensions; decided within 3 working days; RM100). It doesn't let you work \[[N84 (2021)](#n84), [104](#o104), [135](#o135), [158](#o158)\].
7. Reality check: about half of claims settle at the mention and about 85% of those heard are decided for the worker, but 38% are withdrawn or dismissed for non-attendance, usually because the pass expired \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\]; employers often stall until the pass runs out, hence step 6 \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\]. An order is not money: in one case a tribunal ordered over RM3 million in wages, but the workers got only RM251,000 after the employer withheld pay for months and closed \[[N72](#n72)\]. JTK's complaint pages and hotline are not in Bangla \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\]. The employer may not stop you going to the Labour Court \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\].

**Need to know:** the official list of Labour Office addresses, whether a worker can apply for the Special Pass himself, and whether each extension costs RM100.

**In Sabah and Sarawak** complain to the state's own Labour Department, under its Labour Ordinance \[[159](#o159), [160](#o160)\].

- **Sarawak.** Complain to the labour office nearest the workplace: in writing or on its Borang Aduan Pekerja form, by post, fax or email; in person; by phone; on SISPAA (the Malaysian government's online complaints system); or on the WFW app \[[161](#o161)\]. Give your and the employer's name, address and phone, the issues, and your contract and payslips \[[161](#o161)\]. A Labour Court claim is made in person by sworn statement, with your passport, the employer's name and address, contract, pay details and the amount claimed \[[160](#o160)\]. Headquarters: Tingkat 13, Bangunan Sultan Iskandar, Jalan Simpang Tiga, 93532 Kuching; 082-242261; jtknsarawak@mohr.gov.my \[[161](#o161)\].
- **Sabah.** Complain to JTK Sabah, jtksabah.gov.my \[[159](#o159)\]. **Need to know:** JTK Sabah's office addresses and phone numbers, and whether it takes WFW complaints.

**4. Free help in Malaysia.** Representation matters: unrepresented workers in the 2024 Pengerang mediation settled for about half their RM2.1 million claim \[[N152 (2024)](#n152)\], while 74 workers helped by Tenaganita won a Labour Office order for RM1.54 million \[[N153](#n153)\]. The Welfare Board has no safe house in Malaysia \[[162](#o162)\].

- Tenaganita (an NGO that takes migrant workers' cases): 12 Jalan 6/11, 46000 Petaling Jaya, Selangor, +603 7770 3671 or 3691, Monday to Friday 9:30 to 6:00, general@tenaganita.net; 24-hour emergency lines for abuse, rescue, arrest or detention +6012 335 0512 and +6012 339 5350 \[[N143](#n143), [N141](#n141)\]. NGOs help workers gather the documents a Labour Department claim needs \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\].
- Bar Council Legal Aid Centres give non-citizens free legal advice in every Peninsular state, walk-in or by appointment; bring your documents \[[N154](#n154)\]. Kuala Lumpur: Unit 3.03, Level 3, Wisma Badan Peguam Malaysia, 2 Leboh Pasar Besar, 50050 Kuala Lumpur, +603 2691 1121 or 2692 1122, lac.kl@malaysianbar.org.my \[[N154](#n154)\]. They advise and help negotiate; representation is rare, criminal only, and may cost money \[[N84 (2021)](#n84), [N154](#n154)\].
- SUHAKAM investigates complaints from anyone, including non-citizens, about exploitation or unpaid wages, and recommends action to the authorities \[[147](#o147)\]. Email aduan@suhakam.org.my, call +603-2612 5635 or 5636, write to 17th Floor, Menara Aras Raya, Jalan Raja Laut, Kuala Lumpur, or use "New Complaint" on suhakam.org.my and keep the Ticket ID \[[147](#o147)\].
- MTUC (the Malaysian Trades Union Congress) has run a migrant worker centre at 10-5 Jalan USJ 9/5T, Subang Business Centre, 47620 Subang Jaya, Selangor, +603 8024 2953 or +6017 680 9384 \[[N155 (2018)](#n155)\]. **Need to know:** whether it still takes cases.

**5. Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, Bangladesh** (you or your family). You'll need: what happened; proof you are a migrant worker (passport, work permit, BMET smart card, Malaysian ID number); the employer's name and address; what you are owed \[[95](#o95)\].

1. Send it free to the Welfare-1 branch: saswelfare@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030251 \[[95](#o95)\].
2. What you get back: within 7 working days the Ministry asks the labour wing or the Wage Earners' Welfare Board to act, and tells you by letter \[[95](#o95)\].
3. Advice any time: 16135 in Bangladesh (free) or +880 9610102030 from abroad \[[79](#o79)\].

**Injured at work:** claim through PERKESO (If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job) \[[36](#o36)\]; to claim from the employer, file at the Labour Office with payslips, contract and attendance record, and copy the High Commission \[[128 (2024)](#o128)\].

### If you want to change employer

**You can't apply yourself.** PLKS holders may not change employer or sector on their own, and the agreement contract bars it \[[5](#o5), [34 (2022)](#o34)\]. Only the employer or its appointed company representative deals with Immigration; using an agent is an offence \[[152 (2021)](#o152)\].

**When it is allowed.** Only if the employer is bankrupt, closed, has suffered a disaster such as a fire or economic crisis and can't keep its workers, has been restructured or renamed, or has died \[[43 (2024)](#o43)\]; the new employer must be in the same sector and allowed foreign workers \[[43 (2024)](#o43)\]; your PLKS must have 3 months left \[[43 (2024)](#o43)\]. Not open in your 10th year or later, to workers legalised through 6P, RTK (Labour Recalibration) or other programmes, or to domestic helpers \[[43 (2024)](#o43)\]. **Need to know:** whether cross-sector moves are now possible: Immigration says they need Home Ministry (KDN) permission \[[152 (2021)](#o152)\], but they have been reported as allowed \[[N156](#n156)\].

**How the employers do it.**

1. Both employers register on ePPAx (eppax.gov.my, the Labour Department's employer system); the old employer applies naming the new one, which uploads its documents within 7 days \[[163 (2023)](#o163), [164 (2023)](#o164)\].
2. The Labour Department investigates and issues a support letter \[[163 (2023)](#o163), [43 (2024)](#o43)\].
3. The old employer (or heir) submits the free Ministry of Human Resources form and documents at the foreign workers division, Level 5, Setia Perkasa 3, Kompleks Setia Perkasa, 62546 Putrajaya, 03-8886 6501, Monday to Thursday 8.30 am to 4.30 pm, queue numbers 8.30 am to noon only \[[43 (2024)](#o43)\].

**Need to know:** how long a change of employer takes.

### If your employer reports you absent (lari)

**What counts.** Leaving the workplace without telling the employer, intending to escape, or not returning on the agreed date after a trip home \[[5](#o5), [24](#o24)\].

**What follows.** The employer reports you to Immigration with its ID, your passport copy, the runaway form and a police report, and tells the Labour Department within 14 days \[[5](#o5), [24](#o24), [136 (2022)](#o136), [33 (2023)](#o33)\]. You are blacklisted and its security bond is forfeited \[[5](#o5), [24](#o24)\]. Immigration normally cancels a quitting worker's pass at once, leaving you without a valid pass \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\]. False reports are filed near the contract's end to avoid the fare home, or after a complaint \[[47](#o47)\].

**What you can do.**

1. Keep photos of your pass, payslips and attendance records, and details of any WFW or Labour Office complaint \[[91 (2017)](#o91), [101](#o101)\].
2. If you leave over abuse or unpaid wages, file at the Labour Office and email the labour wing \[[128 (2024)](#o128), [148](#o148)\]; the pending-case letter usually gets you a Special Pass (If you aren't paid, route 3) \[[N84 (2021)](#n84)\].
3. If you are arrested, family can call Tenaganita's 24-hour line and email the labour wing \[[N141](#n141), [148](#o148)\].

**Need to know:** how a worker finds out about a report or blacklisting (Immigration's online check is for citizens only \[[165](#o165)\]), and how to clear a false one.

### Leaving at the end of the job

1. **Check-Out Memo** (Memo Periksa Keluar, Immigration's approval to leave for home). The employer applies to Immigration with the form, your original passport and a ticket; you leave on the date and through the exit point on the memo, and the employer is responsible for you until then \[[7](#o7)\]. The Home Minister announced in May 2025 that employers must also be present at the exit point \[[N86](#n86)\].
2. **Ticket home.** The employer pays when the contract ends, when it ends the contract early (unless you breached it), when you leave because it broke the contract, or after serious work injury or illness; you pay if you resign or are dismissed for misconduct \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [37 (2022)](#o37)\].
3. **What you are owed:** wages by the last day of the job, or within 3 days if you end it without notice \[[33 (2023)](#o33)\]; all outstanding wages if sent home early, untaken annual leave (unless dismissed for misconduct), and termination benefits if the employer ended the job after 12 months (Your rights at work) \[[34 (2022)](#o34), [33 (2023)](#o33), [N142](#n142)\].
4. **Withdraw your EPF before you fly.** You'll need: Form KWSP 9K (AHL) and checklist, a copy of your valid passport (or a High Commission travel permit), the Check-Out Memo, and a bank document for an active account at an EPF panel bank in the passport details EPF holds \[[143](#o143), [166](#o166)\].
   1. Apply as soon as you have the memo \[[166](#o166)\].
   2. Apply in person at the self-service terminal in an EPF office, with copies certified by an EPF officer, the High Commission or your employer; helpline +603-8922 6000 \[[143](#o143)\]. **Need to know:** EPF's official list of offices, whether withdrawal costs anything, and how long payment takes.
   3. You get all your savings in ringgit into that account, so keep it open, or a banker's cheque if payment fails \[[143](#o143), [166](#o166)\].
   4. Already left? Use EPF's overseas application: post the forms, certified by a Malaysian high commission or notary, to EPF, Jabatan Pengurusan Transaksi, Menara KWSP, No.1 Persiaran Kwasa Utama, Kwasa Damansara, Seksyen U4, 40150 Shah Alam, Selangor; the money is sent by bank transfer \[[143](#o143)\].
   5. The EPF account then closes; returning workers register again \[[35](#o35)\].
5. **To leave** you need a passport valid 6 months and a valid pass \[[167](#o167)\].

### Going home on leave

The yearly PLKS renewal includes a multiple-entry visa for Bangladeshi workers, with no separate application \[[N157](#n157)\]. Agree your return date with the employer; missing it counts as running away \[[5](#o5), [24](#o24)\]. **Need to know:** how a worker checks the multiple-entry visa was issued.

### Bringing your family

PLKS workers can't bring family \[[5](#o5), [7](#o7)\]. See Family passes for professionals' routes and Having a child in Malaysia for registering a birth \[[168](#o168)\].

**Pregnancy and the work pass.** The yearly FOMEMA medical includes a pregnancy test for women \[[129](#o129), [131](#o131)\]. Confirmed pregnancy means unfit, with no appeal, and the worker is sent home with a Check-Out Memo \[[133](#o133), [5](#o5)\]. **Need to know:** what happens to a worker who becomes pregnant between medicals.

### Scams

**Done to workers.** In Bangladesh, complain free to BMET at ovijog.bmet.gov.bd (name who took the money, upload receipts and papers) or by application to its Director General; it aims to settle within 30 working days \[[94](#o94), [1](#o1)\]. The Ministry's enforcement branch also takes agency complaints: sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd, 02-41030255 \[[95](#o95)\]. In Malaysia, email the labour wing \[[148](#o148)\].

- **Social media posts on Malaysia-approved agencies** have been false \[[56](#o56)\]; check the FWCMS list instead (Step 1, item 4) \[[26](#o26)\].
- **The fee video.** Agencies make workers record a video at their Dhaka office saying they paid only the official fee when they paid far more \[[N62](#n62)\]; keep your own proof of every payment \[[N125](#n125)\].
- **Fake passes and permit "agents".** Rings sell fake e-PLKS (electronic work pass) copies for about RM150 and fake police reports for about RM70 \[[N112](#n112)\]; police in Kulim, Kedah, broke a Bangladeshi-run ring selling fake work permits and fake UNHCR cards for RM80 to 250 in 2026 \[[N158](#n158)\]. Only your employer deals with your pass; anyone else offering is breaking the law \[[152 (2021)](#o152), [7](#o7)\]. Check it yourself (Step 5) \[[115](#o115)\].
- **Sabah jobs.** There is no agreement for recruiting Bangladeshis to Sabah; rings have taken passports and cash for Sabah jobs \[[N22](#n22)\].
- **No job on arrival.** Employers sign a High Commission commitment to put workers to work at once; if broken, email the labour wing \[[25 (2023)](#o25), [148](#o148)\].

**Tried by workers.** Forged visas, passes and passports carry jail and caning (What can get you jailed), including passports bought to resit a failed FOMEMA medical \[[57](#o57), [N83](#n83), [N159](#n159)\].

## Work visa: domestic worker

Not open to Bangladeshis. Immigration's approved source countries for domestic helpers are Indonesia, Thailand, Cambodia, the Philippines, Sri Lanka, India, Vietnam, Laos and Nepal \[[17](#o17), [7](#o7)\].

## Student visa (Student Pass)

A Student Pass lets you stay in Malaysia to study full time at an approved institution; it is not a work visa \[[55](#o55), [54](#o54)\]. You apply through EMGS (Education Malaysia Global Services, a company under Malaysia's Ministry of Higher Education, KPT), for public and private institutions alike \[[169](#o169), [54](#o54), [170](#o170)\]; the Immigration Department of Malaysia (IMI) gives final approval \[[54](#o54)\].

### Step 1: Check the institution and the course

Check before you pay anyone: agents earn commission from institutions and push the colleges paying most \[[N104](#n104)\].

1. **Check the institution may take foreign students.** A private institution needs KPT approval, an accredited programme and the Home Ministry's (KDN's) permission to take international students; a public university needs no separate check \[[54](#o54)\]. KPT's Department of Higher Education (JPT) lists private institutions with that permission, updated monthly \[[171](#o171), [172](#o172)\]. On [jpt.mohe.gov.my, Senarai Daftar dan Statistik IPT Swasta](https://jpt.mohe.gov.my/portal/index.php/ms/ipt/ipt-swasta/25-senarai-daftar-dan-statistik-ipt-swasta), click the download icon beside "Senarai Daftar IPTS Kelulusan KDN" \[[171](#o171)\], open "Tahun 2026", then the "AKTIF" (active) file with the highest number (in November 2026, file 7, the list up to 31 July 2026, updated 20 August 2026) \[[173](#o173), [174](#o174), [175](#o175)\]. Search it for the institution's name; the campus address must match the offer letter \[[175](#o175)\]. If the institution is missing, is in the "TIDAK AKTIF" (inactive) file of the same number, or is on the page's list of cancelled approvals ("Senarai IPTS Yang Telah Diluluskan Tindakan Pembatalan Kelulusan Penubuhan"), it can't get you a Student Pass, since IMI needs its KDN approval letter, in force, with every new application \[[174](#o174), [176](#o176), [171](#o171), [54](#o54)\].
2. **Check the course is accredited** on the [Malaysian Qualifications Register (MQR)](https://www2.mqa.gov.my/mqr/) of MQA (the Malaysian Qualifications Agency): type the course in "Name of Qualification", choose the level and press Search \[[177](#o177)\]. Only accredited programmes appear, and only for the campus named; a course run anywhere else is not accredited \[[177](#o177)\]. "No record" can mean provisional accreditation only, never accredited, or failed \[[177](#o177)\].
3. **A language or training centre** needs approval from the Ministry of Education (KPM) and the Ministry of Human Resources (KESUMA), plus KDN's permission to take international students \[[54](#o54)\], and its students can't work part time or bring family \[[54](#o54)\]. To check one, contact KPM's Private Education Division: 03-8884 9536, bps@moe.gov.my, Aras 3, Blok E2, Kompleks E, 62604 Putrajaya; it acknowledges enquiries within 3 working days \[[178](#o178)\]. **Need to know:** a public list of KPM- or KESUMA-approved centres allowed to take international students, and where to check a KESUMA-approved centre.
4. **Check you qualify:** 12 years of schooling and a recognised exam \[[55](#o55)\], and an English certificate (MUET, the Malaysian University English Test; IELTS; TOEFL iBT or Essentials; PTE Academic; Cambridge English; Linguaskill; OET; or ELS CIEP), without which the application may be delayed and the pass won't cover the whole course \[[179](#o179)\].
5. **Check the offer letter is real.** Agents have given Bangladeshi students fake ones \[[N160](#n160)\]. The institution's international office can confirm by email or phone that it is genuine and the course is running \[[N104](#n104)\]. **Need to know:** an official EMGS or KPT channel for checking an offer letter, and where to find the international office's genuine contacts.

### Step 2: Apply from Bangladesh

Apply from outside Malaysia \[[54](#o54)\]. You or the institution can create the application \[[54](#o54)\], but only one you create and pay for yourself on the Education Malaysia website can be refunded to you \[[180](#o180)\]. You can track it without an agent \[[181](#o181), [N104](#n104)\].

1. **Get and accept an offer letter;** the form can't be submitted without it \[[55](#o55), [179](#o179)\].
2. **You'll need:** a 35 x 45 mm photo on white; your passport's data, visa and observation pages, the passport valid at least 18 months (for a pass covering the whole course, EMGS recommends 12 months beyond the course); the offer letter, all pages, as one PDF; EMGS's Health Declaration Form; your certificates and transcripts; and your English certificate \[[179](#o179), [182 (2019)](#o182)\]. Keep your passport: agents have held students' passports to demand more money \[[N104](#n104), [N160](#n160)\].
3. **Open an account and apply** on [visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my](https://visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my/) or the EMGS HUB app \[[55](#o55)\]. Enter the institution and course exactly as in the offer letter, your name exactly as in the two coded lines at the bottom of your passport's data page, and the passport number on the offer letter \[[182 (2019)](#o182)\]. EMGS then asks the institution to confirm \[[182 (2019)](#o182)\].
4. **Pay** through the institution, by telegraphic transfer (bank-to-bank international transfer: converted to US dollars, plus USD 50), or by Visa or MasterCard on the EMGS site (plus 3%) \[[182 (2019)](#o182)\]. Tuition handed to agents has failed to reach institutions \[[N160](#n160)\]. EMGS's [fee calculator](https://visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my/student-visa/calculate-application-fees) gives, for a new application at a university or private higher education institution: processing RM1,728; eVAL (electronic Visa Approval Letter, IMI's approval to enter as a student) RM159; medical screening in Malaysia RM340 (RM350 in Sarawak); Student Pass RM60; i-Kad (student identity card) RM50, or RM60.60 couriered; EMGS health insurance RM498 to RM644, or a policy through your institution; visa RM20 \[[170](#o170), [183](#o183)\]. 8% service tax is added to processing fees \[[170](#o170)\]. Public-university students may do the medical at their university \[[170](#o170)\]. If a card payment fails but your bank took the money, email enquiry@emgs.com.my with the bank's confirmation, transaction ID and application number \[[182 (2019)](#o182)\].
5. **Refunds.** The first year's processing and eVAL fees aren't refunded once invoiced; convenience fees and service tax never are \[[180](#o180)\]. Before the institution decides, you get a full refund if you ask within 90 days of paying \[[180](#o180)\], through the online refund form in your account, logged in with the email you applied with; card payments come back by telegraphic transfer \[[180](#o180)\].
6. **Lodge the RM750 personal bond** (the Bangladeshi rate) with the institution, which signs a bond with Immigration for you; it is returned when you finish or withdraw without breaking immigration rules \[[184 (2023)](#o184), [179](#o179)\]. The form can be handed in after arrival, for the Student Pass sticker \[[179](#o179)\]. **Need to know:** how and when the institution takes the money, the receipt, and how to claim it back.
7. **If EMGS asks for more documents,** send them within 14 working days of the return date \[[54](#o54)\], or the application closes, and a refund must be asked for within 6 months of closing \[[180](#o180)\].
8. **Wait.** EMGS checks your academic papers, then IMI checks your passport and security and issues the eVAL \[[54](#o54)\]; EMGS's charter is 14 working days for a complete application \[[55](#o55)\].

**If you're refused,** you can appeal \[[180](#o180)\]; the institution's international office handles the appeal or a new application with corrected documents \[[N161](#n161)\]. Ask for any refund within 90 days of EMGS's notice \[[180](#o180)\]. **Need to know:** the appeal form, fee and deadline.

### Step 3: eVAL and visa

The eVAL is valid 6 months; you may not enter before it is issued \[[55](#o55)\]. Then apply on [MyVISA](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/), from outside Malaysia, for a Single Entry Visa under Visa With Reference (VDR), the category needing an approval letter (the eVAL) \[[54](#o54), [55](#o55), [185](#o185), [3](#o3), [4](#o4)\]. Account and payment work as in Tourist visa, Step 2 \[[3](#o3)\]; the fee is RM20 \[[183](#o183)\]. **Need to know:** whether MyVISA's RM105 processing charge applies to students.

### Step 4: How to check your application is real

1. **Track it** on [visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my](https://visa.educationmalaysia.gov.my/): Student Visa, Track My Application, enter passport number and nationality, tick the terms box, press Track My Application \[[181](#o181)\]. IMI's own status page sends Student Pass checks to EMGS \[[186](#o186)\].
2. **At 70%** Immigration has approved your eVAL; download it as a PDF from your EMGS account \[[N161](#n161)\]. If progress stops, ask the institution's international office, which must confirm your application, or EMGS via [educationmalaysia.gov.my/get-in-touch/contact-us](https://educationmalaysia.gov.my/get-in-touch/contact-us) \[[182 (2019)](#o182), [187](#o187)\].
3. **Need to know:** how to check that an eVAL document itself is genuine.

### Step 5: Arrival and the pass

1. **Register the MDAC** (Malaysia Digital Arrival Card) within 3 days before arriving (see Tourist visa, Step 4) \[[4](#o4)\]; long-term pass holders are exempt \[[123](#o123)\].
2. **At Kuala Lumpur International Airport (KLIA),** go to EMGS's 24-hour International Student Arrival Centre (ISAC) before immigration (Terminal 1: Level 3 arrival hall; Terminal 2: Level 3A after the Skybridge); its staff help you through immigration, with priority lanes for students \[[188](#o188)\]. **Need to know:** which documents ISAC asks to see, and whether it charges anything.
3. **Within 7 working days do the medical** at an EMGS panel clinic, listed on EMGS's site and app; without it EMGS can't start your pass \[[189](#o189), [190](#o190)\]. If you fail, appeal at the same clinic within 3 days of being told \[[55](#o55)\]; if that fails, ask for any refund within 90 days of EMGS's notice \[[180](#o180)\]. **Need to know:** how to book the medical, and the appeal's cost, documents and possible outcomes.
4. **The institution gets your pass sticker** with your original passport, its letter, EMGS's support letter, your stamped personal bond and at least 12 months' Malaysian health insurance \[[54](#o54)\]; IMI's client charter gives 5 working days \[[104](#o104)\].

### Keeping the pass

The pass usually lasts up to a year; renew as early as 3 months before expiry \[[55](#o55)\], yourself or through the institution, on STARS (EMGS's Student Application Registration System) or the EMGS website \[[54](#o54), [55](#o55)\]. You'll need a 35 x 45 mm photo on white, copies of your passport's data, visa and observation pages, and your certificates and transcripts \[[179](#o179)\]. You need a CGPA (grade point average) of at least 2.0 and 80% attendance, or an explanation letter from the institution \[[179](#o179)\]. Renewal costs RM270 to EMGS (private institution) and RM60 to IMI \[[170](#o170), [54](#o54)\]. **Need to know:** how long a renewal takes. Breaking any condition can get the pass cancelled \[[54](#o54)\].

**Changing course:** the institution may file a Variation application once, within 18 months after your first semester, if you finished at least one semester at bachelor's level or above; otherwise it ends your pass with a Check-Out Memo and you leave Malaysia before applying again \[[55](#o55)\]. With an eVAL but no pass yet, you must leave to change course \[[55](#o55)\].

### Can students work?

Only university and private higher education students may work, not language or training centre students \[[54](#o54)\]: in semester breaks or holidays over 7 days, at most 20 hours a week, in restaurants, petrol stations, mini markets, hotels or on campus \[[54](#o54), [55](#o55)\]; never as a cashier, front desk worker, hotel singer, masseur, musician or guest relations officer, or student recruiter \[[54](#o54), [55](#o55)\]. Students working outside these rules are deported, sometimes fined or jailed \[[N104](#n104)\]. There is no agreement giving Bangladeshi students graduate passes to work; Malaysia's Higher Education Minister called that claim false \[[N162](#n162)\].

To get permission first: you'll need the job offer letter, the institution's supporting letter with the break dates, your results and attendance report, copies of your data page and Student Pass, and the employer's Labour Department (JTK) approval to employ a foreigner under section 60K of the Employment Act 1955 \[[54](#o54)\]. Go in person with an institution representative to IMI's Student Pass Unit at headquarters or the state Immigration office, which decides after checking the documents \[[55](#o55), [54](#o54)\]. **Need to know:** the fee and how long a decision takes.

### Family

Only master's and PhD students may bring dependants (spouse, children under 18, disabled children of any age, parents and parents-in-law); a Bangladeshi bachelor's student can't, and dependants may not work \[[54](#o54), [55](#o55)\]. The institution applies on STARS \[[54](#o54), [55](#o55)\]. You'll need: Forms IMM.12 and IMM.38; the institution's letters requesting the pass and confirming your Student Pass; a copy of every page of the dependant's passport, cover included (valid 18 months or more) and of your data page and pass; a 3.5 x 5.0 cm photo on white; 12 months' health insurance; a stamped personal bond (new applications); proof of support, such as 3 months' bank statements or a scholarship letter; and the marriage or birth certificate \[[54](#o54)\]. Bring the originals; incomplete applications aren't processed \[[54](#o54)\]. Bangla documents need a certified translation; a dependant already in Malaysia needs relationship documents certified by the Bangladesh High Commission \[[54](#o54)\]. EMGS charges RM250 per dependant (RM140 to renew), each dependant buys insurance, and IMI charges RM90 for the Long-Term Social Visit Pass \[[191](#o191), [54](#o54)\]. **Need to know:** how long a dependant's application takes and how the result is shown. That pass has a category for close family of a foreign student \[[192](#o192)\]; **Need to know:** whether it covers a bachelor's student's family. For a child born in Malaysia, see Having a child in Malaysia \[[168](#o168)\].

### School pupils

IMI gives Student Passes to foreign pre-school and school children \[[54](#o54)\] with a place at a government or government-aided school under KPM, or at an international, expatriate, private or religious school or Chinese independent secondary school registered with KPM's Private Education Division; an international school also needs KDN's permission \[[54](#o54)\]. The school's representative, a parent or a legal guardian applies, new or renewal, at IMI's Student Pass Unit at headquarters, the state Visa, Pass and Permit Division, or a branch offering Student Passes, by the school's address \[[54](#o54)\]. You'll need: Forms IMM.14 and IMM.38 \[[54](#o54)\]; a copy of every page of the child's passport, cover included (renewal: data page and current pass), valid 18 months or more \[[54](#o54)\]; a 3.5 x 5.0 cm photo on white \[[54](#o54)\]; 12 months' health insurance \[[54](#o54)\]; a stamped personal bond (new) \[[54](#o54)\]; for a government or aided school, the state or district education office's approval letter \[[54](#o54)\]; and the originals \[[54](#o54)\]. It costs RM60 plus the visa fee \[[54](#o54)\]; IMI's client charter gives 5 working days \[[104](#o104)\]. The child's parents, legal guardian or adoptive parents, and siblings under 7 may get a Long-Term Social Visit Pass as companions, at RM90, with no work or business \[[54](#o54)\]. **Need to know:** the extra documents for international and private schools, and whether a refusal can be appealed.

### Scams

**Done to students.** Besides commission-driven college choices, fake offer letters, lost tuition and held passports (Steps 1 and 2) \[[N104](#n104), [N160](#n160)\], agents claim a student visa lets you work freely; it doesn't \[[N104](#n104), [54](#o54)\]. Agents have charged families Tk 6 to 8 lakh or more for admission and a visa, steering students to barely functioning colleges \[[N104](#n104)\]; agencies have left EMGS applications stuck for months and, after arrival, moved students from the promised university into a Bangladeshi-owned English language centre, whose students can't work \[[N160](#n160)\].

**Tried by applicants.** Getting a pass by a false statement, or using someone else's, carries a fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years' jail \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\].

**If an agent cheated you or holds your passport,** in this order:
1. **Bangladesh police.** Register on [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) or the Online GD app with your NID, a mobile number (for a one-time code) and a live photo; pick the district and station, describe what happened, when and where, and attach receipts and messages \[[99](#o99)\]. A GD (General Diary) comes back as a digital copy with its number and the investigating officer's details; for a case, take the printout or complaint code to that station \[[99](#o99)\]. Or call the CID (Criminal Investigation Department) hotline, 01320-019999, or control room, 01320-019998, which wants to hear of anyone holding passports or demanding unusual visa sums \[[193](#o193), [N100](#n100)\].
2. **The Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare's Complaint Resolution Cell,** minister.expat@gmail.com \[[194](#o194)\]. Sample: "My son [name, passport no.] paid [agency, address] Tk [amount] on [dates] for admission to [institution] in Malaysia. The fees were not paid and the agency holds his passport. Receipts attached. Please help recover the passport and money." **Need to know:** whether the Ministry acts on complaints about education agents, which hold no recruiting licence.
3. **In Malaysia,** the Bangladesh High Commission's consular wing: consular.kualalumpur@gmail.com, +603 2604 0946, mission.kualalumpur@mofa.gov.bd, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur \[[102](#o102), [122](#o122)\]. In an emergency call 999 (free) for the Royal Malaysia Police \[[195](#o195)\]. **Need to know:** whether the High Commission helps students recover passports held by agents.
4. **Against the institution,** KPT's SiSPAA complaints system, [mohe.spab.gov.my](https://mohe.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do) \[[196](#o196), [197](#o197)\]: register ("Daftar Baharu"), log in, choose "Aduan/Maklum Balas Baharu"; "Semak Aduan/Maklum Balas" shows progress \[[197](#o197), [198](#o198)\]. Give full name, passport number, phone and email, and attach the offer letter and receipts, or KPT may reject it; it doesn't take private money disputes with agents \[[197](#o197)\]. **Need to know:** how long KPT takes to reply.
5. **Free legal help:** OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a Bangladeshi migrants' organisation) advises and files cases for migrant workers, families and trafficking survivors: helpline 01842773300, 4th Floor, 466 Sawdagar Garden, Dania Post Office Road, Dhaka 1236 \[[N163](#n163)\]. **Need to know:** whether OKUP takes the cases of cheated students.

## Tourist visa (social visit)

Bangladeshis need an eVISA to visit for tourism, family or short business, and may stay up to 30 days \[[3](#o3), [63](#o63)\]. Ordinary visits get a single-entry visa; multiple-entry is only for business, medical treatment, Fly & Cruise and wedding tourism \[[3](#o3)\]. It never allows work or study (see Don't work) \[[199](#o199)\].

### Step 1: Prepare

You'll need: a passport valid over 6 months from travel, with at least 3 blank pages \[[4](#o4), [3](#o3)\]; a studio photo, 35 x 50 mm, white background, full face, under 6 months old, no selfies or scans \[[3](#o3), [4](#o4)\]; scans of the data page and the page with your previous passport number \[[4](#o4)\]; a confirmed return ticket \[[4](#o4), [3](#o3)\]; proof of paid accommodation, or, staying with friends or relatives, a cover letter to the immigration officer with your details, the address, and the host's name, Malaysian identity card number and phone \[[4](#o4), [3](#o3)\]; proof of money (cash, card or e-money backed by 3 months' bank statements) \[[3](#o3)\]; for a child, the birth certificate \[[3](#o3)\]; and a credit card, or Alipay or WeChat Pay, to pay \[[3](#o3)\]. Merge several files for one upload field into one \[[3](#o3)\].

### Step 2: Apply on MyVISA

1. **Use only [malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/),** IMI's only official eVISA site; you can't apply from inside Malaysia, and third-party applications can't be verified \[[3](#o3)\]. If an agent applies, only the agent sees the status, and IMI takes no responsibility \[[3](#o3)\]; agents in Bangladesh charge Tk 5,000 to 8,000 for an eVISA you can apply for yourself \[[N164](#n164)\]. MyVISA has shut for weeks at a time (from 10 February 2026, and again in July 2026), suspending all applications and approvals until a notice says it is back \[[N165](#n165), [200](#o200), [201](#o201)\].
2. **Open an account:** under Individual, Sign Up, fill in the form and CAPTCHA (picture code); change the emailed temporary password at first login \[[3](#o3)\]. Log in with email, the emailed 6-digit code and password \[[3](#o3)\]. One account holds 5 applications, for up to 5 people \[[3](#o3)\].
3. **Apply on a weekday, 9 am to 5 pm Bangladesh time** (the only hours open to Bangladesh) \[[202](#o202)\], at least 14 days before travel \[[3](#o3)\]. Write your name exactly as in your passport; with no surname, leave Surname blank or put the full name in Given Name \[[3](#o3)\]. Unpaid drafts expire after 7 working days \[[4](#o4)\].
4. **Pay** RM20 visa fee, RM105 processing and 0.8% (card) or 1.7% (e-wallet) convenience charge; nothing is refunded, even on refusal \[[3](#o3), [183](#o183)\]. Visa agencies add their own fee \[[63](#o63)\].
5. **Watch the status** \[[199](#o199)\]. "Document Submission or Information Update Required": upload what is asked. "Interview Requested": confirm and attend, online or in person as the High Commission sets. No response in 5 days means automatic rejection \[[199](#o199)\]. A decision takes 2 to 7 working days, restarting after any re-upload \[[3](#o3), [199](#o199)\].
6. **Download and print the eVISA** (Actions, Download eVISA) \[[3](#o3)\]. It allows one journey within 6 months of issue, whatever the travel date in that window \[[199](#o199), [3](#o3)\]; it cancels any earlier visa still active \[[3](#o3)\]. A wrong detail from your own mistake means applying and paying again \[[199](#o199)\].
7. **If refused,** there is no appeal, and no reason need be given; you can reapply and pay again \[[3](#o3), [53](#o53)\]. Questions and complaints: High Commission of Malaysia, Dhaka, dhaka@imi.gov.my (visa section), +88 02 4108 1918 (visa), House 19, Road 6, Baridhara Diplomatic Enclave, Sunday to Thursday 8.30 am to 4.30 pm \[[53](#o53), [105](#o105)\].

### Step 3: Check your eVISA is real

At [malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/evisa/check-evisa](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/evisa/check-evisa) enter your passport number, the visa sticker number and the CAPTCHA \[[203](#o203)\]; only official-site applications can be checked \[[3](#o3)\]. **Need to know:** what a genuine eVISA looks like and where its sticker number is printed.

### Step 4: Fill in the Malaysia Digital Arrival Card (MDAC)

1. **When:** within 3 days before arrival, the submission day included \[[4](#o4), [126](#o126)\].
2. **Where:** only [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/mdac/main), with no fee mentioned \[[125](#o125)\]. Fake sites charge, such as malaysiacard.com (USD 29.99), and appear as sponsored search results \[[124 (2024)](#o124), [N137](#n137)\].
3. **You'll need:** name, passport number, date and place of birth, nationality, sex, passport expiry, email, mobile, arrival and departure dates, flight number, mode of travel and last airport of departure \[[126](#o126)\].
4. **Check it** under "Check Registration" with passport number, nationality and PIN \[[127](#o127)\]; show it with your passport at immigration \[[125](#o125)\].

### Step 5: At the border

**At Dhaka airport,** immigration police stop travellers (Malaysia included) whose visa, BMET clearance (a worker's emigration clearance), papers or answers don't match \[[N166](#n166)\]. Carry what Malaysia checks: eVISA, return ticket, paid accommodation and proof of money \[[3](#o3)\]. **Need to know:** what to do if Bangladeshi immigration stops you, and whether you can complain or travel later.

**At the Malaysian airport,** the officer checks passport, eVISA, MDAC, return ticket and paid accommodation \[[3](#o3), [125](#o125)\]. A visa doesn't guarantee entry: you can be refused if you can't explain your plans, or can't prove you aren't a "prohibited immigrant", which includes lacking means of support \[[3](#o3), [53](#o53), [204](#o204), [41 (2006)](#o41)\]. Autogates aren't open to Bangladeshis \[[61](#o61)\]. Bangladeshis holding valid tourist visas are regularly turned back at KLIA for fake hotel bookings, no return ticket, too little money or no satisfactory reason \[[N167](#n167), [N168](#n168), [N101](#n101)\]; travellers are told to carry RM500 to RM1,000, and officers may check phone photos \[[N169](#n169)\]. Those refused go on a "Not to Land" list and the airline returns them within about 24 hours \[[N108](#n108), [N170 (2023)](#n170)\].

**If refused entry:** the Immigration Act's appeal to the Minister covers only Permit holders, not visitors \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. **Need to know:** whether a refused visitor can appeal, any official minimum sum of money, and what "Not to Land" means for future visits.

### Scams

**Done to travellers.** Fraudsters bring Bangladeshi women on tourist visas promising jobs, though no agreement exists to recruit Bangladeshi women for Malaysia \[[N21](#n21)\]. Rings in Kuala Lumpur sell Bangladeshis forged European visas for up to RM20,000 \[[N171](#n171)\]; a man promised Italy was sent with fake Malaysian and French visas and returned from the Malaysian airport \[[N172](#n172)\]. Travel agencies have promised work permits, sent people on visit visas, held them and demanded ransom from families \[[N100](#n100)\].

**Tried by travellers.** Getting a pass by a false statement carries a fine up to RM10,000 or up to 5 years' jail \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\].

**If someone sold you a "job on a tourist visa", holds your passport, or holds you in Malaysia,** in this order:
1. **Held or in danger in Malaysia:** call 999 for the Royal Malaysia Police (PDRM) \[[195](#o195)\], then the Bangladesh High Commission's consular wing, +603 2604 0946, consular.kualalumpur@gmail.com \[[102](#o102)\].
2. **Family calls the Probashi call centre** of WEWB (the Wage Earners' Welfare Board, the government body for migrant workers' welfare), for problems of migrants and families: 16135 toll free in Bangladesh, +880 9610102030 from abroad, 24 hours \[[79](#o79)\].
3. **Bangladesh police:** online at [gd.police.gov.bd](https://gd.police.gov.bd/) or at the station (see Student visa, Scams), or the CID hotline, 01320-019999, for anyone holding passports or demanding unusual visa sums \[[99](#o99), [193](#o193), [N100](#n100)\].
4. **Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment** (Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000) \[[96](#o96)\]. Against a recruiting agency, email the enforcement branch, sasenforcement@probashi.gov.bd (02-41030255), with your proof, full address and the agency's RL (licence) number and address; free, action within 3 to 5 working days, settled within 30 \[[95](#o95)\]. Against a licensed agency that sent you abroad without permission, apply to the Secretary with your BMET smart card, ticket, contract and passport copies; free, settled within 60 working days of BMET's recommendation \[[95](#o95)\]. Anything else: the Complaint Resolution Cell, minister.expat@gmail.com \[[194](#o194)\]. Sample: "I, [name, passport no.], paid [agent, address, phone] Tk [amount] on [date] for a job in Malaysia. I was sent on a tourist visa on [date] and [what happened]. Receipts and messages attached."
5. **Free legal help:** OKUP (see Student visa, Scams) helps trafficking survivors and migrants' families, helpline 01842773300 \[[N163](#n163)\].

### Don't work

A visit pass allows no work, work training or study \[[199](#o199), [53](#o53)\] and lapses once its conditions are broken \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. Overstaying brings fines and can bar re-entry for a set period \[[3](#o3)\]. A visit pass is extended only for illness, an accident or war at home, with evidence and a return ticket, by applying in person on Form IMM.55 \[[205](#o205)\]. A directive of 8 June 2026 ordered a nationwide multi-agency crackdown on people working on tourist, student and other short passes \[[N173](#n173)\].

## Family visit sponsored by a Malaysian citizen (VDR)

A Malaysian citizen (the sponsor) can invite a Bangladeshi relative through a VDR (Visa Dengan Rujukan, visa with reference): the sponsor applies in Malaysia, Immigration headquarters approves, and a Malaysian mission issues the visa \[[206](#o206), [207 (2023)](#o207), [6](#o6)\]. Proof of relationship: for parents or parents-in-law, the sponsor's spouse's marriage certificate, birth certificate and passport, plus a notarial certificate endorsed by the Malaysian mission and Wisma Putra (Malaysia's foreign ministry); for a child under 7, the birth certificate and the sponsor's marriage certificate; for a spouse, the marriage certificate \[[206](#o206)\]. Other relatives also need documentary proof \[[206](#o206)\]. No work, work training or study \[[53](#o53)\].

### Step 1: Send your papers to the sponsor

You'll need: a passport valid more than 6 months from travel, every printed page scanned; 2 photos, 3.5 x 5.0 cm; the relationship documents above; and, for the sponsor's form, your marital history, job in Bangladesh with proof, earlier visits to and work in Malaysia, spouse and children, address and planned length of stay \[[207 (2023)](#o207), [208](#o208), [206](#o206), [3](#o3)\].

1. **Parents or parents-in-law: get the notarial certificate endorsed** \[[206](#o206)\]. High Commission of Malaysia in Dhaka: House 19, Road 6, Baridhara Diplomatic Enclave; dhaka@imi.gov.my, +88 02 4108 1918; Sunday to Thursday 8.30 am to 4.30 pm \[[105](#o105)\]. Wisma Putra's Consular Division takes documents walk-in from the person or a representative with ID, usually ready the same or next working day: Wisma Putra No 1, Jalan Wisma Putra, Precinct 2, 62602 Putrajaya; 03-8000 8000; sah_admin@kln.gov.my; Monday to Thursday 8.30 am to 4 pm, Friday 8.30 to 12 and 2.45 to 4 pm \[[209 (2023)](#o209)\]. Non-English documents need an English translation \[[209 (2023)](#o209)\]. **Need to know:** what the certificate must state and which notary may make it; the fees and times in Dhaka and Putrajaya; whether the Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur must authenticate it first (Wisma Putra's general rule for documents from abroad) \[[209 (2023)](#o209)\]; and whether the sponsor can take it to Wisma Putra.
2. **Send the sponsor copies of everything,** with your name in capitals, sex, place and country of birth, date of birth, nationality and passport details exactly as in your passport, for Form Imm.12 \[[208](#o208)\]. Ask the sponsor to name the High Commission in Dhaka as the issuing mission \[[207 (2023)](#o207), [105](#o105)\].

### Step 2: The sponsor applies in Malaysia

You'll need (the sponsor): MyKad (Malaysian identity card); the 3 latest payslips showing RM2,000 a month or more, or, if self-employed, the SSM (Companies Commission of Malaysia) business registration and 3 months' bank statements; an employer's confirmation letter; and the papers from Step 1 \[[207 (2023)](#o207), [206](#o206)\].

1. **Download the forms** from [IMI's VDR checklist](https://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/SENARAI-SEMAK-Borang-VDR.pdf): Form Imm.12, the sponsor's declaration (Surat Akuan Penaja), the bond and the sponsor information form (Borang Maklumat Tambahan Penaja) \[[207 (2023)](#o207)\].
2. **Type Form Imm.12** in 2 copies, with the relative's photo on each \[[208](#o208), [206](#o206)\].
3. **Swear the declaration** (job, employer, monthly income and full responsibility for the relative) before a Commissioner for Oaths at a government court, a Sessions Court judge, magistrate or notary public \[[207 (2023)](#o207)\].
4. **Sign a personal bond, stamped RM10 at LHDN** (the Inland Revenue Board); the sponsor forfeits its sum if the relative breaks the visit's conditions \[[206](#o206), [210](#o210)\]. A cash security bond applies only to citizens of China and Nigeria \[[206](#o206)\].
5. **Add a cover letter** (Surat Iringan) and the employer's letter \[[207 (2023)](#o207)\].
6. **Submit in person;** the sponsor signs the sponsor form before an Immigration officer \[[206](#o206), [207 (2023)](#o207)\]. VDRs are decided by the Visa, Pass and Permit Division, Immigration headquarters, No. 15, Tingkat 1-7, Blok 2G4, Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya; 03-8000 8000 \[[206](#o206), [211](#o211)\]. Incomplete applications or ones without documentary proof are not accepted \[[206](#o206)\].
7. **Wait about 7 working days;** the result is a visa approval letter (eVAL) \[[206](#o206), [207 (2023)](#o207), [199](#o199), [3](#o3)\].

**Need to know:** whether a Bangladeshi relative's sponsor pays a cash deposit (the form asks about one, wang cagaran, and IMI's bond rate for Bangladeshis is RM750) \[[207 (2023)](#o207), [212](#o212)\]; what the cover letter must contain; whether a state Immigration office also takes the application, and the counter hours; any IMI fee for the approval; how the approval reaches the relative; and whether a refusal can be appealed.

### Step 3: Apply for the eVISA

Bangladeshis still need an eVISA with the VDR approval \[[213](#o213)\]. You'll need: a scan of your passport's data page, the approval letter, an email address, and a card or e-wallet \[[3](#o3), [183](#o183)\].

1.
