Apply on [malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/) as in Tourist visa, Step 2, choosing Visa With Reference, with details exactly as on the approval letter; any difference must be corrected by the issuing office before you pay \[[3](#o3), [4](#o4), [199](#o199)\]. From Bangladesh the portal takes applications on weekdays, 9 am to 5 pm Bangladesh time \[[202](#o202)\].
2. Pay RM20 visa fee, RM105 processing and 0.8% (card) or 1.7% (e-wallet); nothing is refunded \[[183](#o183), [3](#o3)\]. If charged twice, or the status stays "New Application", don't pay again; use MyVISA's 24-hour Live Chat \[[3](#o3)\].
3. Download and print the eVISA (Actions, Download eVISA) and check it at [malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/evisa/check-evisa](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/evisa/check-evisa) with your passport number and the visa sticker number \[[3](#o3), [203](#o203)\].

### Step 4: Travel

Register the MDAC (Malaysia Digital Arrival Card) within 3 days before arrival and carry the papers the border officer checks (see Tourist visa, Step 5) \[[4](#o4), [3](#o3)\].

### Don't work, scams and complaints

Working ends a visit pass (see What can get you jailed); Immigration arrests Bangladeshis working on visit passes, and the sponsor loses the bond \[[53](#o53), [41 (2006)](#o41), [N174](#n174), [N175](#n175), [210](#o210)\]. False statements are an offence (see Tourist visa, Scams) \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. **Need to know:** scams on this route, such as fake sponsors or brokers charging for a "family VDR". Immigration matters go only through Immigration counters or its official portal; no officer or third party may handle them outside those channels \[[N176](#n176)\]. If a sponsor or broker cheats you:

1. **Before paying,** check a phone number, bank account or company name on the Royal Malaysia Police's Semak Mule portal \[[214](#o214)\].
2. **In Malaysia,** report at the nearest police station; in an emergency call 999, free \[[214](#o214), [195](#o195)\]. If you paid online, first call the National Scam Response Centre (NSRC), 997, 24 hours, within 24 hours so the money can be blocked, and tell your bank \[[214](#o214)\].
3. **If an official asked for a bribe,** report to the MACC: 1-800-88-6000 (free), [portaladuan.sprm.gov.my](https://portaladuan.sprm.gov.my/), info@sprm.gov.my, SMS +6019-6000 696; No. 2, Lebuh Wawasan, Presint 7, 62250 Putrajaya \[[215](#o215)\].
4. **For help as a Bangladeshi,** contact the Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur (see Contacts) \[[122](#o122), [102](#o102)\].

## Business visit

A social visit pass covers meetings, conferences, inspections, audits, signing agreements, investment surveys and seminars, not professional or technical work or a job \[[205](#o205), [216](#o216), [41 (2006)](#o41)\]. For up to 30 days of urgent professional work the Malaysian company gets PLS@Xpats permission (Step 3); longer work needs a Professional Visit Pass or Employment Pass \[[217 (2022)](#o217), [218](#o218)\].

### Step 1: Get the papers

You'll need: a passport valid more than 6 months with 3 blank pages; a studio photo; bank statements; proof of paid accommodation; a return ticket; and your company's dispatch letter or an invitation from a Malaysian government body or government-linked company (see Tourist visa, Step 1) \[[4](#o4), [3](#o3)\].

### Step 2: Apply for the business eVISA on MyVISA

1. Apply as in Tourist visa, Steps 2 and 3, choosing the business purpose and uploading the letter and ticket; one account holds up to 5 travellers' applications \[[3](#o3), [203](#o203)\].
2. A business multiple-entry visa (6 months, up to 30 days per entry) is limited to 31 eligible nationalities; Bangladesh is one of the 31 nationalities that need an eVISA \[[199](#o199), [3](#o3)\]. **Need to know:** whether these are the same 31, so that Bangladeshis can choose multiple entry.
3. Pay RM20 visa fee, RM105 processing and the card or e-wallet charge, non-refundable; the decision takes 2 to 7 working days, and a refusal has no reasons or published appeal, though you can reapply and pay again \[[183](#o183), [3](#o3)\].

### Step 3: Short professional work: PLS@Xpats

You'll need (the company): its request letter; a copy of every page of your passport, valid 6 months or more; its SSM registration; the work or project schedule; and your return ticket \[[217 (2022)](#o217)\].

1. The company applies free at [mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats/main](https://mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats/main), without ESD (IMI's Expatriate Services Division) registration; it takes 3 working days, for anyone 18 or over, not blacklisted and not Israeli \[[217 (2022)](#o217)\].
2. The approval is valid 30 days from approval for one entry and at most 30 days' work, not extendable, in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan only, with no family pass \[[217 (2022)](#o217)\].
3. You still need the eVISA, and must carry the approval letter or be refused entry for this work \[[217 (2022)](#o217)\]. Questions: eapp.imi.gov.my enquiry form \[[219](#o219)\].

### Scams and complaints

Fake invitations: a syndicate sold Bangladeshis fake invitation letters (such as to sports competitions), with return tickets and hotel bookings to fool officers, for RM2,000 to RM5,000 \[[N177 (2024)](#n177)\]. Don't pay anyone to "get" the visa: it is applied for and paid only online, for the Step 2 fees, and no officer or third party may handle it outside official channels \[[3](#o3), [N176](#n176)\]. False statements are an offence (see Tourist visa, Scams) \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. If cheated, in Malaysia use the route under Family visit (VDR), Don't work, scams and complaints \[[214](#o214), [215](#o215)\]; in Bangladesh, if an agency or broker took money for a "business visa" job, complain to BMET (see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Scams) \[[1](#o1), [95](#o95)\].

## Medical visit

The medical eVISA is only for treatment at a member hospital of MHTC (Malaysia Healthcare Travel Council, the Health Ministry's agency for foreign patients) \[[3](#o3), [220](#o220)\].

### Step 1: Get a hospital appointment

You'll need: medical records and test results, recent scans (X-ray, CT, MRI) digitally or on CD, a written medical history and your medicines with generic names and doses \[[221](#o221)\].

1. Choose a hospital under "Find A Hospital" on [malaysiahealthcare.org](https://malaysiahealthcare.org/find-hospital-filter); MHTC's concierge, WhatsApp +60 18 870 0100, helps choose one and can arrange the appointment, hotel and transport \[[222](#o222), [3](#o3), [223](#o223)\].
2. Send your records and ask for an appointment letter in English with treatment and personal details, a written cost estimate for treatment, stay and follow-up, and an MHTC support letter for the visa \[[222](#o222), [221](#o221)\]. **Need to know:** whether a hospital or MHTC charges for reviewing records or issuing the appointment letter, how long it takes, and how to check a letter is genuine.

### Step 2: Apply for the medical eVISA

You'll need: a passport valid at least 6 months; the appointment letter; the MHTC support letter; a return ticket; proof of accommodation; and a photo \[[222](#o222)\].

1. Apply on [malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/) at least 14 working days before the appointment (see Tourist visa, Step 2) \[[3](#o3)\]. MHTC names Irving Aviation Ltd as its local partner in Bangladesh (contact Abid Rahman Rafsan, +8801762001467) \[[220](#o220)\]. **Need to know:** whether Bangladeshis must go through MHTC's partners, as the High Commission in Dhaka says \[[63](#o63)\], or can apply themselves, and what the partner charges.
2. Pay RM20 visa fee, RM105 processing and the card or e-wallet charge, non-refundable; it takes 3 to 5 working days, and a refusal needs no reasons and has no published appeal \[[3](#o3), [183](#o183), [222](#o222)\].
3. The approval comes by email; print it and keep a copy on your phone \[[222](#o222)\]. A medical multiple-entry visa, where given, lasts 6 months \[[199](#o199)\].

### Step 3: Companions and arrival

Up to 2 companions may come (spouse, parent, child, grandparent, sibling, nurse or domestic helper); at the airport show passport, eVISA, eVAL printout, appointment letter and companions' details \[[3](#o3)\]. The special airport lane is only for MHTC member-hospital patients with an MHTC authorisation letter or a Meet and Greet package \[[222](#o222)\]; Meet and Greet costs RM189 per companion, and the hospital must request it at least 3 working days ahead \[[222](#o222)\].

### Longer treatment

For a government or specialist hospital, IMI gives a Long-Term Social Visit Pass, with one companion \[[192](#o192)\]. The patient, hospital or companion applies with Forms Imm.38 (IMI's visa application form) and Imm.12, every printed passport page, the hospital's support letter, 3 months' bank statements, proof the companion is close family certified by the Malaysian High Commission, the MHTC letter and proof of payment; it takes 25 working days \[[224 (2023)](#o224), [225](#o225), [104](#o104)\]. **Need to know:** which IMI office takes it, the fee, the document you get, and whether a refusal can be appealed.

### Don't work, scams and complaints

No unauthorised work; false statements and using another's pass are offences (see Tourist visa, Scams) \[[199](#o199), [41 (2006)](#o41)\]. **Need to know:** scams on the Malaysia medical-visa route.

1. **About the hospital** (care, staff or bill): complain to the Health Ministry's Private Medical Practice Control Section (CKAPS) at [myckaps.spab.gov.my](https://myckaps.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do), 03-8883 1362, ckaps.aduan@moh.gov.my, or Deputy Director CKAPS, Level 3, Block E1, Complex E, 62590 Putrajaya; family may complain with the patient's written permission. The hospital must reply within 14 days of CKAPS's letter, simple cases take 30 days, and a refund or bill reduction is up to the hospital, a lawyer or a civil court \[[226](#o226), [227](#o227)\].
2. **About an agent who took money:** use the police, NSRC and MACC route (Family visit (VDR), Don't work, scams and complaints) \[[214](#o214), [215](#o215)\].

## Professional Visit Pass

The Professional Visit Pass (PVP) lets a professional sent by an overseas company work on a project or train with a Malaysian company for up to 12 months; it isn't for general workers or operators. You work only for the named company, with no Dependant Pass for family \[[218](#o218), [216](#o216)\]. You can't apply yourself: a registered Malaysian company, organisation or institution applies while you are in Bangladesh \[[216](#o216)\].

### Step 1: The Malaysian company prepares

1. The company registers with ESD \[[218](#o218)\].
2. On Xpats Gateway (the employer-government platform for Employment Pass and PVP applications) it gets JTKSM (Labour Department of Peninsular Malaysia) approval to employ a foreigner under section 60K of the Employment Act, and acknowledgement that it advertised on MYFutureJobs, the job portal of PERKESO (the Social Security Organisation) \[[228](#o228), [229](#o229)\]. A training organiser applies before you travel \[[53](#o53)\].
3. **Check the company** on SSM e-Info as in Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Step 1, item 6 \[[52](#o52), [90](#o90)\]. **Need to know:** how to check a company's ESD registration or that a PVP offer is genuine.

### Step 2: Send your documents; the company applies

You'll need: passport copy; appointment or assignment letter; invitation or support letter; work or training schedule; contract or project papers; academic or professional certificates, prepared as in Employment Pass, Step 2; and proof of experience \[[216](#o216), [230](#o230)\].

1. The company applies through ESD and pays RM1,200 plus tax, then RM90 per quarter of the pass \[[218](#o218), [231](#o231)\]. The bond for Bangladeshis is RM750, by bank guarantee to the Director General of Immigration \[[212](#o212), [232 (2024)](#o232)\].
2. The period approved depends on the task; 12 months is not automatic \[[216](#o216)\]. **Need to know:** how long a standard PVP decision takes.
3. Only the company can appeal a refusal, within 14 days in ESD Online; appeals are decided within 30 working days \[[233](#o233)\].

### Step 3: Bangladesh clearance

**Need to know:** whether a PVP holder needs BMET emigration clearance, as a worker with a self-obtained visa does (see Employment Pass, Step 3) \[[1](#o1)\].

### Step 4: Enter and get the ePASS

1. After approval you get a VDR to enter \[[234](#o234), [233](#o233)\].
2. The company pays for the endorsement in ESD Online; you then go, no appointment needed, to the Expatriate Satellite Centre at KLIA Terminal 1 or 2, or the company gets it endorsed at the MYXpats Centre (TalentCorp and Immigration): Level 4, Surian Tower, No 1, Jalan PJU 7/3, Mutiara Damansara, 47810 Petaling Jaya, Selangor; 9 am to 5 pm, Monday to Friday \[[233](#o233), [235](#o235)\].
3. Always carry the ePASS with your passport \[[233](#o233)\]. Check it by its QR code or at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan) \[[233](#o233)\]. Mistakes are corrected by the company through the MYXpats helpdesk (+603-7839 7171, helpdesk@myxpats.com.my) in 3 to 5 working days \[[233](#o233), [235](#o235)\].

**Scams:** in 2025 a work pass sold through a middleman for RM5,000 had a tampered QR code and no record in Immigration's system \[[N176](#n176)\]; check every pass as in item 3. **Need to know:** any case of a "PVP" sold to general workers as a work visa. False information can mean rejection, cancellation, withdrawal of the pass, immigration action and blacklisting \[[216](#o216)\].

Jockeys, volunteers and exchange or training students (up to 6 months) get PVPs from IMI outside ESD; artistes and film crews apply through PUSPAL, the Communications Ministry's filming centre \[[236](#o236)\].

## Transit

1. **Same flight, staying in the airport:** no visa \[[237](#o237)\].
2. **Changing planes** on one booking (PNR) inside the transit hall: no visa. You need one to pass immigration (separate bookings, collecting bags, changing between KLIA and KLIA2 (Kuala Lumpur International Airport 2)) or to leave the airport \[[238](#o238), [59](#o59)\]. **Need to know:** whether this applies to Bangladeshis as it does to Indians.
3. **Leaving the airport on a layover:** apply on MyVISA for a transit or visit eVISA, with the airport as address and a cover letter explaining the layover; RM20 plus RM105 processing, 2 to 7 working days, refusal without reasons or published appeal \[[3](#o3), [183](#o183)\].
4. Malaysia's 120-hour visa-free transit for travellers from the Indian subcontinent is suspended \[[60](#o60)\].

**Ship crew:** a seaman signing on at a Malaysian port needs a passport valid 6 months from entry, a Seaman Book, a Letter of Guarantee from IMI at the port, and a transit visa \[[239](#o239), [6](#o6)\]. **Need to know:** who requests the Letter of Guarantee, where, its cost and time.

## Employment Pass

For professionals hired by a Malaysian company at a basic salary of at least RM5,000 a month who meet ESD's qualification rule (Step 1); valid for one employer, and only the employer applies \[[16](#o16), [230](#o230), [233](#o233), [240](#o240)\]. A pass issued through ESD is valid only in Peninsular Malaysia, and changing company means a new application \[[240](#o240)\]. Some passes are approved by other bodies: manufacturing by MIDA (Malaysian Investment Development Authority); ICT companies with MSC Malaysia status by the Multimedia Development Corporation; banking, finance and insurance by Bank Negara Malaysia (the central bank); the capital market by the Securities Commission; government contract posts by the Public Services Department; the East Coast Economic Region by ECERDC (East Coast Economic Region Development Council); and Iskandar Malaysia by IRDA (Iskandar Regional Development Authority) \[[241](#o241), [242](#o242), [243](#o243)\]. **Need to know:** what MSC Malaysia status is and how a company gets it.

### Step 1: Check you qualify

By basic salary: Category I RM20,000 a month or more and Category II RM10,000 to RM19,999, each up to 10 years; Category III RM5,000 to RM9,999 (RM7,000 to RM9,999 in manufacturing), up to 5 years, counting time with the same employer, position and category \[[16](#o16), [244](#o244)\]. Only basic salary taxable in Malaysia counts, not allowances, bonuses or pay outside Malaysia \[[16](#o16), [244](#o244)\]. You need a degree and 3 years' relevant experience, a diploma and 5, or a technical certificate and 7 \[[230](#o230)\]. All categories may bring family: a Dependant Pass for a spouse and children under 18, and a Long-Term Social Visit Pass for parents, parents-in-law and children 18 to 25 (see Family passes, Family of an Employment Pass holder) \[[16](#o16)\].

### Step 2: The employer applies

You'll need: a passport valid more than 12 months with 6 empty pages, the whole booklet and both covers scanned in colour; an unedited photo, 3.5 x 5.0 cm, light blue background, 99 x 142 pixels; your highest educational (and any professional) certificate and a detailed resume with your total work experience, in colour; and the contract, stamped by the Inland Revenue Board and signed and dated by both sides \[[230](#o230)\]. From Bangladesh, English certificates must be certified true copies from an embassy or high commission (in Bangladesh or Malaysia) or the employer's HR manager; others must be translated into English and certified by the Malaysian High Commission in Dhaka \[[230](#o230)\]. **Need to know:** the High Commission in Dhaka's fee and time for certifying certificates.

1. **The employer registers with ESD** (5 working days), then gets JTKSM approval and the MYFutureJobs acknowledgement (see Professional Visit Pass, Step 1), in 10 working days; hiring without approval risks a fine up to RM100,000, 5 years' jail or both \[[16](#o16), [228](#o228), [229](#o229)\].
2. **Send the employer** the documents above \[[230](#o230)\].
3. **The employer applies on ESD Online and pays RM2,160;** the decision takes 2 to 5 working days \[[231](#o231), [16](#o16), [233](#o233)\]. Only the employer can appeal a refusal, within 14 days, or else claim 75% of the processing fee back within 14 days \[[233](#o233), [245 (2024)](#o245)\].
4. **The employer applies for your VDR** with the pass; after approval it pays the endorsement: RM200 a year, RM125 processing and RM20 a year visa fee \[[233](#o233), [231](#o231), [246](#o246)\].

### Step 3: Emigration clearance in Bangladesh

You'll need: your application; a visa copy; the contract if any; an attested copy of your OEP registration card (see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Step 1); a declaration on Tk 300 non-judicial stamp paper; and a pay-order for the fees \[[1](#o1)\].

1. Apply yourself at BMET's emigration branch in Dhaka (BMET head office: 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000) or the District Employment and Manpower Office in Chattogram; it takes 3 working days \[[1](#o1), [88 (2021)](#o88)\]. Fees and the smart card checked at the airport are as in Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Step 6 \[[1](#o1)\]. Problems: Director (Emigration), +8802222222415, diremi@bmet.gov.bd \[[1](#o1)\]. **Need to know:** the emigration branch's opening hours, where to buy the stamp paper, which bank the pay-order is drawn on, and what to do if clearance is refused.

### Step 4: Fly and get the ePASS

Register the MDAC within 3 days before arrival \[[4](#o4)\]. On arrival go, no appointment needed, to the Expatriate Satellite Centre at KLIA Terminal 1 or 2 for your ePASS and i-KAD (the Employment Pass holder's card) \[[233](#o233)\]; pass problems go to the MYXpats helpdesk (see Professional Visit Pass, Step 4) \[[235](#o235)\].

### Step 5: Check it is real

Check the ePASS by QR code or at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan), since fakes are sold (see Professional Visit Pass, Scams), and check the employer on SSM e-Info before accepting \[[233](#o233), [N176](#n176), [52](#o52)\]. If you get a new passport, the company must transfer the pass endorsement to it, or Immigration may stop you leaving \[[233](#o233)\]. If cheated, use the route under Family visit (VDR), Don't work, scams and complaints \[[214](#o214), [215](#o215)\]. False statements and using another's pass are offences (see Tourist visa, Scams) \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\].

### Problems with the employer

Unpaid wages and other Employment Act breaches go through the Working for Workers app, the Bangladesh High Commission's labour wing and the Labour Court (see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), If you aren't paid) \[[100](#o100), [148](#o148), [91 (2017)](#o91)\]. JTKSM advises free on 03-8000 8000 or jtksm@mohr.gov.my \[[91 (2017)](#o91)\]. **Need to know:** what an Employment Pass holder can do if the employer cancels or doesn't renew the pass.

## Family passes

The pass depends on who brings the family member: an Employment Pass holder, a Malaysian citizen, a permanent resident or a student. A PLKS (temporary work pass) holder can't bring anyone.

### Family of an Employment Pass holder

**Who gets which pass.** A spouse and children under 18 get a Dependant Pass; parents, parents-in-law, children aged 18 to 25 and a common-law spouse get a Long-Term Social Visit Pass (LTSVP, a pass for a stay of 6 months or more) \[[16](#o16), [192](#o192)\]. A Dependant Pass lasts as long as the Employment Pass, or until the dependant's passport expires if sooner \[[247](#o247)\]. Holders of a Category III Employment Pass issued before 1 June 2026 (when Category III covered a basic salary of RM3,000 to RM4,999) still can't bring dependants \[[244](#o244), [16](#o16)\]. **Need to know:** how long an LTSVP for these relatives is approved for at a time.

1. **Get the family documents translated and attested.**

   You'll need: for a spouse, the marriage certificate (in Bangladesh, the Muslim marriage registration certificate); each child's birth certificate; each person's passport; and, for a common-law spouse, an affidavit or acknowledgement letter from the Employment Pass holder's embassy \[[230](#o230)\].

   1. Translate each certificate into English if needed \[[230](#o230)\]. Take the original and translation, both with the translation agency's and translator's seal and signature, to an approved notary public and then the Ministry of Law \[[248 (2022)](#o248)\].
   2. Each certificate must then be certified as a true copy, dated in the current year, by the Malaysian High Commission in Dhaka or the Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur \[[230](#o230)\].
   3. The Bangladesh High Commission attests a marriage certificate only after it has been attested in Dhaka by both the Ministry of Law and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs \[[249 (2024)](#o249)\]. Have it attested by a notary public and the Ministry of Law first, then hand in two sets at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs' Consular wing in Dhaka (rooms 201/205; 02-47123389; asconsular2@mofa.gov.bd) between 8 am and noon with a copy of your NID or passport, and collect them free the same day, 2 to 3 pm \[[248 (2022)](#o248)\]. A birth certificate must be issued through BDRIS (Bangladesh's online birth and death registration system), verifiable online, and attested by a notary and the Ministry of Law \[[248 (2022)](#o248)\]; for a birth certificate the High Commission also accepts proof of online verification instead of the Dhaka attestation \[[249 (2024)](#o249)\].
   4. At the Bangladesh High Commission (No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur), bring the attested originals, copies of every paper and of your passport and visa, and one extra set of copies \[[249 (2024)](#o249), [122](#o122)\]. Attesting a certificate or affidavit costs RM42 (RM63 urgent) plus RM8.40 per extra page, paid by card at the counter (Maybank Merchant Card Service) or into the High Commission's Maybank account 564427102268 with the original deposit slip \[[250](#o250)\].

   **Need to know:** the Bangladesh High Commission's counter hours for attestation and how long it takes; and the Malaysian High Commission in Dhaka's procedure and fee for certifying family documents.

2. **The employer applies.** Only the Employment Pass holder's company can apply, on ESD Online (the system of Immigration's Expatriate Services Division, which handles professional passes), with the Employment Pass or after it is approved \[[247](#o247), [233](#o233)\].

   You'll need, to give the employer: the attested documents; a colour scan of each family member's passport; and, for an LTSVP, a personal bond form stamped by the Inland Revenue Board (LHDN, Malaysia's tax authority) \[[230](#o230)\].

   1. The employer pays RM540 per person on applying, then RM90 a year for the pass, RM50 processing and the visa fee (RM20 a year for a Bangladeshi) \[[231](#o231), [246](#o246)\].
   2. The LTSVP bond is a promise that the relative will obey immigration law, backed by a Malaysian company representative's pledge of RM750 for a Bangladeshi \[[230](#o230), [212](#o212)\].
   3. A decision takes about 14 working days; the family member gets an ePASS (electronic pass) \[[230](#o230), [233](#o233)\].

   **Check it is real:** scan the ePASS QR code or check it at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/epass?semakan) \[[233](#o233)\]. A false statement to get a pass is an offence \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. **If refused:** ESD's online appeal covers only Employment Pass and Professional Visit Pass refusals \[[233](#o233)\]. Questions: MYXpats Centre, which processes Employment Pass applications \[[251](#o251)\], +603-7839 7171, helpdesk@myxpats.com.my \[[252](#o252)\]. **Need to know:** whether the RM750 is a deposit and is returned, and whether a refused Dependant Pass or LTSVP can be appealed.

3. **Work and study.** To work, a Dependant Pass holder needs their own Employment Pass \[[247](#o247), [230](#o230)\]. From 1 January 2027 the spouse of a Category I Employment Pass holder (RM20,000 a month or more) may work on the Dependant Pass, under conditions still to be set \[[253](#o253), [16](#o16)\]. A spouse on a Dependant Pass applies for Permission to Study online through the Sub-Product menu in ESD Online; paper applications are no longer accepted at Immigration or MYXpats Centre counters \[[252](#o252)\]. LTSVP relatives may not work or study without switching to an Employment Pass or Student Pass \[[230](#o230)\]. **Need to know:** the conditions for Category I spouses once published; the fee, processing time and what a Permission to Study holder receives.

**Need to know:** any official warning about scams in family-pass applications.

### Spouse of a Malaysian citizen

**Before you marry.** A PLKS worker may not marry a Malaysian or another foreign worker in Malaysia; one who does loses the work permit and is sent home \[[7](#o7), [N178 (2023)](#n178)\]. For other foreigners, the Malaysian partner first has Immigration confirm the foreigner's pass on IMI's (the Immigration Department of Malaysia's) pass-confirmation form for marriage, [Permohonan Pengesahan Pas bagi Tujuan Perkahwinan Warganegara Asing](https://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Borang-Pengesahan-Pas-Bagi-Tujuan-Perkahwinan-Dengan-Warganegara-Asing.pdf) \[[254 (2022)](#o254)\]: hand in two copies at any state Immigration headquarters with a copy of the partner's identity card, two passport photos of each of you and a copy of the foreigner's passport with the pass pages; an officer certifies the pass and its expiry on the form \[[254 (2022)](#o254)\]. The Bangladesh High Commission in Kuala Lumpur asks a Bangladeshi for an unmarried certificate from the Deputy Commissioner's office attested by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Dhaka, and the parents' affidavit or consent attested by the Ministry of Law and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, with the consular fee receipt \[[249 (2024)](#o249)\]. **Need to know:** whether the pass-confirmation form has a fee and how long it takes. The marriage must be registered under Malaysian law before the spouse can get permission to work or apply for permanent residence \[[255](#o255), [256](#o256)\].

1. **Apply for the spouse's LTSVP.**

   You'll need, as originals with copies: IMI's [Form Imm.12](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/IM.12.pdf), [Form Imm.38](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/IM38.pdf) (2 copies), [Form Imm.55](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Imm55.pdf) (2 copies), [sponsor's statement](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borang_Kenyatan_Penaja.pdf), [security bond](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Security-Bond.pdf) and [sworn declaration](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Surat_Akuan_Sumpah.pdf), listed on IMI's [new spouse application page](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/permohonan-baru-isteri-atau-suami-kepada-warganegara/); the Malaysian spouse's identity card (MyKad), birth certificate and passport (all pages); the marriage registration certificate; any divorce or death certificate from an earlier marriage; a postcard-size wedding photo; children's birth certificates; the Malaysian spouse's employer letter (or business licence if self-employed); proof of income (3 months' payslips, bank book or tax Form J); and a utility bill \[[192](#o192), [257](#o257)\].

   1. Stamp the security bond with a RM10 revenue stamp \[[192](#o192)\]; have the sworn declaration, with your photo, stamped by a Commissioner for Oaths \[[192](#o192)\].
   2. Go with the Malaysian spouse to the Immigration office nearest your home \[[258](#o258), [257](#o257)\]. Immigration checks every original and accepts only complete applications \[[192](#o192)\].
   3. A decision takes 25 working days under IMI's client charter; the pass runs 6 months to 5 years at a time \[[104](#o104), [192](#o192)\].

   **Need to know:** the fee, and whether a refusal can be appealed.

   **Checks and scams.** Immigration refuses a marriage made only for immigration status and looks closely at large age gaps \[[N179](#n179)\]; it may visit your address, interview people and check documents before granting or extending the pass \[[192](#o192)\]. Anyone selling a spouse pass or marriage registration is a syndicate \[[N179](#n179)\]; report them as under "If Immigration mishandles any family-pass application" \[[259](#o259), [260](#o260)\]. A false statement in the application can bring a fine of up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail, or both \[[192](#o192)\].

2. **Get permission to work.** Once endorsed in the passport, it lets the spouse work, run a business or practise a profession on the LTSVP \[[192](#o192)\].

   You'll need: the Malaysian spouse in person with their MyKad; your passport and pass; a sworn statement; the marriage certificate; the job offer; an employment contract with a RM10 revenue stamp stating the job, period and monthly pay \[[192](#o192)\]; IMI's [Applicant, Citizen Spouse and Employer Information Form](http://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/Borang_Maklumat.pdf); and the employer's latest company registration form from MyData SSM (the online records of the Companies Commission of Malaysia) \[[192](#o192)\].

   1. Apply at the Visa, Pass and Permit Division of Immigration in the state where you'll work, with the LTSVP application or any time after a job offer \[[192](#o192)\].

   **Need to know:** whether the work permission can also be applied for at Immigration headquarters in Putrajaya or the nearest state office; its fee and how long it takes.

**Permanent residence.** A wife can apply for an Entry Permit after 3 years of marriage and over 12 months on the spouse LTSVP; a husband after 5 years of marriage and 5 continuous years on it \[[256](#o256)\]. See Other passes, Permanent residence.

**Residence Pass** (Pas Residen: RM500 for 5 years; allows work, study and business) \[[261](#o261)\]. For relatives of citizens (biological, step or adopted children; a widowed or divorced spouse with a citizen child; parents and parents-in-law) after 3 years on a valid long-term pass, and children and spouses of permanent residents after 5 \[[261](#o261)\].

   You'll need: Form IMM 16A \[[261](#o261)\]; 2 passport photos and a copy of the sponsor's identity card \[[261](#o261)\]; old and new passports and original supporting documents, with copies \[[261](#o261)\]; copies of Bangladeshi documents attested by the Bangladesh High Commission \[[261](#o261)\].

   1. A Malaysian citizen aged 21 or over sponsors you and comes with you to Immigration headquarters in Putrajaya or a nearby Immigration office \[[261](#o261)\].
   2. Pay RM500 plus a multiple-entry visa fee set by nationality \[[261](#o261)\].
   3. Track it on myOnlinePasResiden at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main) \[[186](#o186)\].

   **Need to know:** how long a decision takes, and whether a refusal can be appealed.

### Other relatives of Malaysians and of permanent residents

IMI gives an LTSVP in other family categories \[[192](#o192)\]:

- A foreigner aged 60 or over with no family at home, sponsored by a child, child-in-law or sibling in Malaysia earning RM2,000 or more \[[192](#o192)\], with a personal bond stamped RM10 at LHDN \[[192](#o192)\].
- An abused, divorced or widowed wife of a citizen, with a local sponsor earning RM2,000 or more \[[192](#o192)\]; a widow's sponsor is a close relative of her late husband who comes with her \[[192](#o192)\]; an abused wife adds a police or hospital report, a security bond stamped RM10, and the sponsor's declaration and identity card \[[192](#o192)\].
- The wife of a permanent resident, married over 6 months \[[192](#o192)\].
- A citizen's child or stepchild aged 7 or under, on the citizen parent's application \[[192](#o192)\].
- Close family of a foreign student, with the student's letter from the institution and an embassy letter proving the relationship \[[192](#o192)\]; see Student visa, Family.
- A patient at a government or specialist hospital, with one companion \[[192](#o192)\].

   You'll need: all originals; Forms Imm.12 (or Imm.55) and Imm.38; and the marriage, divorce or birth certificates that apply \[[192](#o192)\].

   1. Apply at the Immigration office nearest your home; only complete applications are accepted, and the charter gives 25 working days \[[258](#o258), [192](#o192), [104](#o104)\].
   2. A dependant's or guardian's LTSVP costs RM90 \[[54](#o54)\]. A false statement is an offence \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\].

   **Need to know:** whether these pass holders may work, the fee for the other categories, and whether a refusal can be appealed.

**If Immigration mishandles any family-pass application.**

1. Complain on IMI's SISPAA at [imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do](https://imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do): choose "Aduan/Maklum Balas Baharu" (new complaint), fill in every field, and track it under "Semak Aduan/Maklum Balas" \[[259](#o259)\]. For example: "I applied for a [pass] at [office] on [date], reference [number]. After [N] working days I have no decision, beyond IMI's client charter time (14 working days for a Dependant Pass, 25 for an LTSVP). Please tell me the status." \[[230](#o230), [104](#o104)\]
2. If an officer asks for money or misbehaves, report to IMI's Integrity Division: Tingkat 4 (Menara), Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya; aduanintegriti@imi.gov.my; 03-8880 1246. Giving your name and contact protects you under the Whistleblower Protection Act 2010 \[[260](#o260)\].
3. Immigration headquarters: No. 15, Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya; 03-8000 8000 \[[262 (2022)](#o262)\].

**Need to know:** how long SISPAA complaints take to answer, and whether the Bangladesh High Commission helps with family-pass cases.

### Workers on PLKS

PLKS workers can't bring family or marry in Malaysia \[[7](#o7)\], and can't apply for permanent residence; PLKS years don't count toward it \[[256](#o256)\].

### Students

See Student visa, Family.

## MM2H (Malaysia My Second Home)

A long-stay pass for people with substantial savings, run by MOTAC (the Ministry of Tourism, Arts and Culture), for citizens of any country with diplomatic relations with Malaysia \[[263](#o263)\], aged 25 or over (21 for the SEZ/SFZ, Special Economic Zone / Special Financial Zone, tier) \[[263](#o263), [264](#o264)\].

**Tiers.** Silver: USD 150,000 fixed deposit and a home of at least RM600,000, 5 years. Gold: USD 500,000 and RM1,000,000, 15 years. Platinum: USD 1,000,000 and RM2,000,000, 20 years \[[265](#o265), [266](#o266)\]. SEZ/SFZ: USD 65,000 (age 21 to 49) or USD 32,000 (50 and over) and a home in Forest City, Johor, 10 years \[[265](#o265), [264](#o264)\].

**Family.** A spouse; children under 21 (biological, step or adopted); unmarried, unemployed children aged 21 to 34; disabled children of any age; parents and parents-in-law \[[263](#o263), [265](#o265)\].

**Sarawak's S-MM2H.** Sarawak runs its own scheme, reportedly on these terms since 2025: age 30 or over, RM500,000 fixed deposit in a Sarawak bank, 30 days a year in Sarawak, a 5-year pass, applied for through the state's one-stop centre or licensed agents \[[N180](#n180)\].

1. **Check the money can legally leave Bangladesh.** Bangladesh Bank sets foreign-exchange rules under the Foreign Exchange Regulation Act 1947 \[[267](#o267)\] and has not approved sending money abroad for MM2H \[[N181 (2024)](#n181)\]; a Malaysian MM2H licence doesn't by itself mean an agent meets Bangladesh's rules \[[N182](#n182)\]. **Need to know:** Bangladesh Bank's written rule on this, and how to ask it for permission.

2. **Choose a licensed agent.** Every application goes through an MM2H operator licensed by MOTAC \[[263](#o263)\]. Check at [mm2h.gov.my/agencies](https://www.mm2h.gov.my/agencies) that the company name, licence number (MM2H plus digits) and licence dates match what the agent shows \[[268](#o268)\]. No agent can guarantee approval; some have taken tens of thousands of ringgit, then stopped answering, and applicants couldn't recover it \[[N183 (2024)](#n183)\].

3. **The agent applies** through the One Stop Centre MM2H on MOTAC's online system; the Ministry of Home Affairs approves through Immigration \[[263](#o263), [269](#o269)\]. Fees: processing RM5,000 for the main applicant and RM2,500 per dependant, plus participation RM1,000 (Silver, SEZ/SFZ), RM3,000 (Gold) or RM200,000 (Platinum) \[[265](#o265)\]; MOTAC's system also lists RM500 plus SST (sales and service tax) per application \[[269](#o269)\]. You need a Certificate of Good Conduct (in Bangladesh, a police clearance certificate; see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Step 4) and Malaysian police screening \[[N184 (2024)](#n184)\]. **Need to know:** whether the RM500 is extra, the full document list and the processing time.

4. **After approval.** Everyone has a medical check at a MOTAC panel clinic or hospital \[[263](#o263)\]. Open the fixed deposit in a licensed Malaysian bank; up to half can later be withdrawn for a home, education, medical care or tourism in Malaysia \[[263](#o263)\]. Buy a home within a year of endorsement and keep it 10 years unless selling to buy a dearer one \[[270](#o270), [263](#o263)\]. Immigration's client charter gives 1 working day to endorse the pass \[[104](#o104)\].

5. **Living on MM2H.** Under 50, spend at least 90 days a year in Malaysia \[[263](#o263)\]. Only Platinum holders may work or run a business \[[271](#o271), [266](#o266), [272](#o272), [264](#o264)\]. Renewal is RM500 a year plus a visa fee of RM0 to RM50 by nationality \[[263](#o263)\]. MM2H years don't count toward permanent residence \[[256](#o256)\].

**If refused.** Any appeal is for the Ministry of Home Affairs \[[263](#o263)\]. **Need to know:** how and by when.

**If an agent cheats you.**

1. Complain on MOTAC's SISPAA at [motac.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do](https://motac.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do): choose "Aduan/Maklum Balas Baharu", give the agent's name and licence number, what you paid and when, attach receipts, and track it under "Semak Aduan/Maklum Balas" \[[273](#o273)\]. For example: "I paid RM[amount] on [date] to [agent], licence MM2H[number], for an MM2H application. The agent has not applied or answered since [date]. Receipts attached. Please investigate and help me recover the money."
2. Or call the MM2H office: +603 8891 7568 or +603 8891 7434; MOTAC, No. 2, Tower 1, Jalan P5/6, Presint 5, 62200 Putrajaya \[[263](#o263)\].

**Need to know:** whether MOTAC has ever recovered money for an applicant cheated by an agent.

## DE Rantau Nomad Pass

For remote workers and freelancers paid from outside Malaysia, run by MDEC (the Malaysia Digital Economy Corporation), for all nationalities except Israel, main applicant over 18 \[[274](#o274)\]. Minimum income: USD 36,000 a year in tech, USD 60,000 otherwise \[[274](#o274), [275](#o275)\]. Up to 12 months at a time and 48 in all \[[274](#o274)\]; holders at the two-year limit will get two more years under conditions to be set \[[253](#o253)\]. No route to permanent residence \[[275](#o275)\]. New applications are closed until 30 November 2026 and reopen on 1 December 2026 \[[276](#o276)\].

**Family.** A spouse or common-law partner, children under 18 (including adopted and stepchildren), a special-needs child of any age, and parents \[[274](#o274)\]. A spouse can't work \[[274](#o274)\].

1. **Prepare the documents** in English or with a certified translation, as clear PDFs, with the same name and income figures throughout \[[274](#o274), [277](#o277)\].

   You'll need, as a remote employee: a passport with 6 empty pages, valid over 14 months \[[277](#o277), [274](#o274)\]; a CV; a signed contract showing remote work, title, duration and salary, with an employer not registered in Malaysia, starting at least 3 months before you apply \[[277](#o277), [274](#o274)\]; 3 months' payslips and bank statements showing the same deposits \[[277](#o277)\]; an income statement or tax return; a Letter of Good Conduct (police clearance; see Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Step 4); the personal bond form; your highest education certificate \[[277](#o277)\]; and a medical insurance enrolment certificate valid in Malaysia for at least 3 months, covering dependants; it can be handed in after approval, before the pass is issued \[[277](#o277)\].

   You'll need, as a freelancer: the passport, CV, Letter of Good Conduct, bond form, certificate and insurance; up to 3 signed client contracts showing scope, duration and payment terms; 3 months' invoices; proof of payment (PayPal, Stripe, Wise or bank records); and 3 months' bank statements above the threshold \[[278](#o278), [274](#o274)\].

   **Need to know:** the minimum insurance cover and accepted insurers, and whether Malaysian clients count.

2. **Apply** at [mdec.my/derantau](https://mdec.my/derantau) and pay RM1,080 plus RM540 per dependant online, not refunded \[[274](#o274)\]. A decision takes 6 to 8 weeks after a complete application; track it at [malaysiadigital.mdec.my](https://malaysiadigital.mdec.my) \[[274](#o274)\]. No appeal, but you can reapply; no one can guarantee approval \[[274](#o274)\].

3. **After approval: enter and get the pass endorsed.** The approval letter is valid 6 months, not extendable; finish every step within it \[[274](#o274)\].

   1. Get an eVISA (a visa applied for online) or, if the letter says so, a VDR (visa with reference) \[[274](#o274)\]. Apply only on IMI's own site, [malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my](https://malaysiavisa.imi.gov.my/); applications through third parties or agents can't be verified \[[3](#o3)\]. For a Bangladeshi an eVISA costs the RM20 visa fee, a RM105 processing charge and a 0.8% (card) or 1.7% (e-wallet) charge, none of it refunded, and takes 2 to 7 working days; see Tourist visa, Step 2, for how to apply \[[3](#o3), [183](#o183)\]. A tourist or social visit pass can't be converted: leave and re-enter \[[274](#o274)\]. **Need to know:** the cost, documents and processing time of a VDR for DE Rantau.
   2. Use a normal Immigration counter, not the automatic gate, to get an entry stamp \[[274](#o274)\].
   3. Within 1 month, upload on the DE Rantau portal, under your approved application: the personal bond with a RM10 revenue stamp, endorsed by LHDN; all passport pages; the entry stamp; the eVISA or VDR; Malaysian health insurance; and an LHDN tax registration slip from mytax.hasil.gov.my \[[274](#o274)\].
   4. Pay Immigration RM90 per 3 months or RM360 a year, any multiple-entry visa charge, and a RM750 bond for a Bangladeshi, refunded when the pass expires \[[274](#o274)\].
   5. Stay in Malaysia until the e-Pass is issued, about 1 week; leaving can cancel the application \[[274](#o274)\].

**Where you can live.** Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan; Sabah or Sarawak need a tourist pass \[[274](#o274)\].

**If something goes wrong.** Contact MDEC's Expats Service Centre (expatctr@mdec.com.my, 03-8315 3106 / 3157) \[[276](#o276)\] or the form at [mdec.my/contact-us](https://mdec.my/contact-us), enquiry type "Foreign Knowledge Workers" \[[279](#o279)\]. For a visa or endorsement problem, complain on IMI's SISPAA at [imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do](https://imi.spab.gov.my/eApps/system/index.do) \[[259](#o259)\].

**Need to know:** any official warning about DE Rantau scams.

## Other passes

**Residence Pass-Talent (RP-T).** A 10-year pass for experienced professionals from TalentCorp (Talent Corporation Malaysia, a government agency for skilled workers) \[[280](#o280)\]. You need 3 consecutive years' work in Malaysia and an Employment Pass with over 3 months left; at least RM15,000 basic a month; Malaysian income tax paid for 2 years; and a degree, diploma or professional certificate with 5 years' experience \[[280](#o280)\]. The holder can change employer, and the spouse can work without an Employment Pass \[[280](#o280)\].

1. Register an account on the RP-T Portal at [rpt.talentcorp.com.my](https://rpt.talentcorp.com.my/), complete the application and upload the documents yourself; no agent may apply for you \[[281](#o281)\]. Name a local contact: a Malaysian citizen aged 21 or over working and living in Malaysia, with a copy of both sides of their MyKad \[[281](#o281)\].
2. Pay TalentCorp online RM540 per person on applying, not refunded; if approved, RM5,400 for you or your spouse and RM4,860 per other dependant; then Immigration RM500 per pass (RM90 for an LTSVP) plus the visa fee \[[282](#o282)\].
3. Approval takes 21 working days from a complete application, the result follows within 10, endorsement takes 7, and Pos Malaysia delivers the pass within 7 more \[[281](#o281)\].
4. No appeal; reapply after 1 year \[[281](#o281)\]. Questions: RPTCare@myxpats.com.my, 03-7839 7171 \[[281](#o281)\]; TalentCorp +603 7839 7000, info@talentcorp.com.my \[[280](#o280)\].

**Malaysia Tech Entrepreneur Programme (MTEP).** For tech founders, through MDEC, at [mdec.my/mtep](https://mdec.my/mtep): 1 year for new founders (from RM2,700) or 5 years for experienced founders (from RM5,400, dependants allowed). You need RM50,000 in personal bank statements, a certificate of good conduct and a sponsor \[[283](#o283)\]: for 1 year, a Malaysia Digital Hub or MTEP recognised partner, which signs a personal bond and gives a letter of offer or tenancy agreement; for 5 years, a Malaysian sponsor or individual who signs a declaration \[[283](#o283)\]. **Need to know:** the processing time, whether a refusal can be appealed, and whether any nationality restriction applies to Bangladeshis.

**Investor Pass.** For investors working with Malaysia's investment agencies; 6 months, extendable by 6, no dependants \[[284](#o284)\]. Apply from outside Malaysia on Xpats Gateway ([xpatsgateway.com.my](https://xpatsgateway.com.my/), the platform for professional passes), after the company is fully registered in ESD Online \[[284](#o284), [285](#o285)\]. Fees: RM1,200 (RM1,296 with tax), a RM90 pass fee for 6 months, RM500 processing and the visa fee \[[284](#o284)\]. A complete application is processed within 5 working days, and the ePASS appears in Xpats Gateway 1 working day after payment \[[284](#o284)\]. **Need to know:** whether a refusal can be appealed, and whether any nationality restriction applies to Bangladeshis.

**Premium Visa Programme (PVIP).** IMI's visa of up to 20 years for investors, entrepreneurs and talent, no age limit, allowing work and study \[[286](#o286)\]. You need offshore income of RM40,000 a month or RM480,000 a year and a RM1 million fixed deposit in a licensed Malaysian bank; the participation fee is RM200,000, plus RM100,000 per dependant \[[286](#o286)\], RM2,000 of it paid at online registration; the pass is RM2,000 a year \[[286](#o286)\]. Dependants: a spouse, children under 25 (biological, step or adopted), disabled children of any age, parents and parents-in-law, plus a domestic helper under the domestic helper rules \[[286](#o286)\]. Only IMI-appointed PVIP agencies apply, on [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/main) \[[286](#o286)\], with your written power of attorney \[[286](#o286)\]; check the agency is registered with IMI before paying \[[286](#o286)\]. PVIP years don't count toward permanent residence \[[256](#o256)\]. **Need to know:** where IMI publishes the list of appointed PVIP agencies, how long an application takes and whether a refusal can be appealed.

**PLS@XPATS.** IMI's online permission to work for professionals on a social visit pass, at [mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats](https://mtp.imi.gov.my/plsXpats); IMI's charter approves it in 3 working days \[[287](#o287), [219](#o219), [104](#o104)\]. Emergency work may be considered by incident, need and policy \[[216](#o216)\]. **Need to know:** who is eligible, the fee and whether a refusal can be appealed.

**Permanent residence (Entry Permit).** Lets a foreigner live in Malaysia without time limit and work, study and run a business without another pass \[[288](#o288), [256](#o256)\]; it can be cancelled for harm to public order, security, health or morality \[[256](#o256)\].

**Who can apply.** A Malaysian citizen's wife, husband or biological child under 18, and "fully foreign" nationals \[[288](#o288), [256](#o256)\]: investors, experts and professionals in fields recognised by a Malaysian regulating agency, with 5 continuous years on an Employment, Student, Professional Visit or Residence Pass and no break over 6 months \[[256](#o256)\]. Check your points first at [app.imi.gov.my/epc](https://app.imi.gov.my/epc) \[[186](#o186)\]. PLKS, MM2H and PVIP holders can't apply \[[256](#o256)\].

   You'll need, as originals with copies: a passport valid 12 months; Form IM.4 (any Immigration office); old and new passports (data page and endorsed pages); the sponsor's MyKad and declaration; 2 white-background passport photos each of you and the sponsor \[[256](#o256)\]. A spouse adds the information form, the marriage certificate registered in Malaysia, a declaration that the marriage continues, MyKad copies of 2 Malaysian referees, children's birth certificates (and MyKad from 12), proof of address (a tenancy or purchase agreement or a utility bill) and the spouse LTSVP deposit receipt; a child under 18 adds the foreign parent's passport, foreign birth certificate, any Malaysian birth registration, parents' marriage certificates, proof of address and the sponsor's proof of income; a fully foreign applicant adds the information form and 2 referees' MyKad copies \[[256](#o256)\]. Copies of Bangladeshi documents (passport, marriage and birth certificates) need attestation by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs or the Bangladesh High Commission, translation into English or Malay, then attestation by a Malaysian mission or Malaysia's Foreign Ministry \[[256](#o256)\].

   1. Download IMI's Entry Permit guideline e-book from [imi.gov.my/index.php/perkhidmatan-utama/permit-masuk](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/perkhidmatan-utama/permit-masuk/) \[[288](#o288)\].
   2. Go with your sponsor and all originals to the nearest Immigration office \[[256](#o256)\]. A fully foreign Bangladeshi pays a RM750 deposit, refundable after 2 years on the permit \[[256](#o256)\].
   3. You and the sponsor are interviewed (not a child), and police run a security check \[[256](#o256)\].
   4. Six months after the acknowledgement, check at [imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/myPermit?semakStatusPermit](https://imigresen-online.imi.gov.my/eservices/myPermit?semakStatusPermit); the letter goes to the sponsor \[[256](#o256)\].
   5. If approved, go with your sponsor to the nearest Immigration office with their identity card, your passport (valid over 12 months) and the letter to amend your current pass \[[256](#o256)\].
   6. Within 30 days, go together to the Visa, Pass and Permit Division at Immigration headquarters, Putrajaya, with the checklist documents, pay RM120 by card, and collect the permit, which never expires \[[256](#o256)\]. Uncollected within 6 months of the letter without good reason, the approval lapses \[[256](#o256)\].
   7. With each new passport, get a new Entry Permit notice stamp at the nearest Immigration office; after more than 6 months abroad it is held while Immigration investigates \[[256](#o256)\].

   **If refused:** within 30 days of the refusal letter, write to the Home Minister through the Chief Secretary, Ministry of Home Affairs, Bahagian Hal Ehwal Imigresen, Aras 4, Setia Perkasa 2, Kompleks Setia Perkasa, 62546 Putrajaya; questions to 03-8886 3316, 3319 or 3170 or rayuanpermitmasuk@moha.gov.my. You can reapply after 1 year \[[256](#o256), [289](#o289)\].

**MACS.** A fast lane at two Johor checkpoints (Bangunan Sultan Iskandar, Johor Bahru; Kompleks Sultan Abu Bakar, Tanjung Kupang) for frequent Malaysia–Singapore travellers; it doesn't replace any visa or pass \[[290](#o290)\]. A foreigner on a long-term pass (Employment, Professional Visit, Student or PLKS) involved in developing Iskandar Malaysia (the Iskandar Regional Development Authority's region) can get a Category B sticker \[[290](#o290), [241](#o241)\]. Book on IMI's appointment system at [sto.imi.gov.my/STO/home.php](https://sto.imi.gov.my/STO/home.php), fill in the [MACS form](https://www.imi.gov.my/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/pdf-NEW-MACS-FORM-1.pdf), and apply at the MACS counter, Level 1, Wisma Sunway Big Box, Iskandar Puteri, Johor (+607-2333016) \[[290](#o290)\]. **Need to know:** the fee and how long it takes.

## Changing visa type

- **Visit pass (tourist or family visit) to PLKS (company worker's pass):** No. The employer gets the VDR and PLKS while you are outside Malaysia; you enter only after approval \[[7](#o7), [5](#o5)\]. A visit pass doesn't allow work \[[291](#o291), [53](#o53)\]. See Work visa: company worker (PLKS).
- **Visit pass to Longer visit pass:** Only in special cases. Only for cases such as illness, an accident or war at home, with proof and a ticket home or onward \[[291](#o291)\].
- **Visit pass to DE Rantau Nomad Pass:** No. A tourist pass can't be converted \[[274](#o274)\]. See DE Rantau Nomad Pass.
- **Visit pass to Student Pass:** No. You must be outside Malaysia when the application is submitted \[[54](#o54)\]. See Student visa (Student Pass).
- **Visit pass to Professional job up to 30 days:** Only arranged before you travel. The company gets PLS@Xpats approval (see Business visit, Step 3) \[[217 (2022)](#o217)\]; without the approval letter you won't be let in for this work \[[217 (2022)](#o217)\]. Longer: see Professional Visit Pass.
- **Student Pass to Student Pass for a higher level:** Yes. Apply for Progression (for example bachelor's to master's) within 3 months of finishing; you may stay while it is processed \[[55](#o55)\].
- **Student Pass to Work pass:** Only through an employer. The employer applies, for example for an Employment Pass \[[292](#o292)\]. Bangladeshis can't get the Graduate Pass \[[55](#o55)\].
- **PLKS to Another employer or sector:** Only with the Home Ministry's permission. See If you want to change employer \[[7](#o7)\].
- **Dependant Pass to Work:** Only as an Employment Pass. The employer applies for an Employment Pass for the dependant \[[230](#o230), [247](#o247)\]. See Family of an Employment Pass holder.
- **Long-Term Social Visit Pass, family of an Employment Pass holder to Work or study:** Only by converting. An employer applies for an Employment Pass, or you get a Student Pass \[[230](#o230)\]. See Employment Pass; Student visa (Student Pass).
- **Long-Term Social Visit Pass, spouse of a Malaysian to Work:** Yes. Get the work endorsement in your passport; see Spouse of a Malaysian citizen \[[192](#o192)\].
- **Any pass, applicant outside Malaysia to Employment Pass:** Yes. The employer applies for the VDR with the Employment Pass \[[233](#o233)\].

**Need to know:** whether a student or a PLKS worker can switch to an Employment Pass without leaving Malaysia.

## Asylum

Asylum gives no legal right to stay or work in Malaysia \[[293](#o293), [294](#o294)\]. There is no national asylum system and the Immigration Act has no provision on refugees; UNHCR (the UN refugee agency) decides claims under its own mandate \[[293](#o293), [41 (2006)](#o41)\], and its recognition gives no legal immigration status \[[293](#o293)\]. Refugees and asylum seekers are treated as undocumented migrants: they can be arrested, detained, fined and deported, and may not work \[[294](#o294)\]. UNHCR can't grant status or work permits or stop an arrest; its document is not a visa and at most may reduce the risk of arrest \[[294](#o294), [295](#o295)\].

**Who counts.** Only someone who fled persecution or serious harm because of race, religion, nationality, social group or political opinion, or who can't return because of war or serious disorder; someone who left for work or study is a migrant \[[293](#o293)\].

**The process.** Apply only on UNHCR's My Services portal, reached from help.unhcr.org/malaysia ("Registering with UNHCR"): create a personal account and submit the request; you get a reference number to quote in every contact, and letters are not accepted \[[295](#o295)\]. Wait to be called for an interview; don't go to the Reception Centre without an appointment \[[295](#o295)\]. UNHCR deals first with requests from 2022 or earlier and the most vulnerable, and can't say when anyone will be interviewed \[[295](#o295), [296](#o296), [293](#o293)\]. At the interview you must tell the truth; being coached or misleading UNHCR harms the case \[[293](#o293)\]. A refusal can be appealed once, within 30 days; then the case closes and UNHCR's help and documents end \[[293](#o293)\]. An unregistered pregnant asylum seeker pays full foreigners' fees for antenatal care and childbirth at government facilities \[[296](#o296)\].

**Scams.** All UNHCR services are free; anyone asking money for an interview or letters is lying \[[295](#o295), [293](#o293)\]. Fake UNHCR cards and work permits are sold \[[N158](#n158)\]. Scan a card's large QR code with the free UNHCR Verify Plus app (Google Play or App Store); "Incompatible QR Code" means it is not genuine \[[297 (2024)](#o297)\].

**Contact.** UNHCR Malaysia, 570 Jalan Bukit Petaling, 50460 Kuala Lumpur, 03 2118 4800; detention hotline +6012 630 5060 \[[297 (2024)](#o297)\].

## Having a child in Malaysia

**PLKS workers.** A PLKS worker may not marry in Malaysia or bring family, and the government may revoke the pass of a worker who marries \[[7](#o7), [37 (2022)](#o37), [34 (2022)](#o34)\]. The FOMEMA check within 30 days of arrival includes a pregnancy test; a confirmed pregnancy can't be appealed, and a worker found unfit is sent home by the employer \[[24](#o24), [129](#o129), [133](#o133)\]. An employer may not dismiss a pregnant worker except for wilful breach of contract, misconduct or closure \[[145 (2024)](#o145), [136 (2022)](#o136)\]. **Need to know:** what happens to a PLKS worker who becomes pregnant after the arrival check, including at renewal.

The steps below are for parents lawfully in Malaysia with family, such as an Employment Pass holder with dependants or the spouse of a Malaysian \[[247](#o247), [192](#o192)\].

1. **Register the birth with JPN** (Jabatan Pendaftaran Negara, the National Registration Department) within 60 days: free, then RM50 as a late registration \[[168](#o168), [298](#o298)\].

   You'll need: form JPN.LM01 (original; take it at the JPN office, as the website copy is only a guide); the hospital's birth confirmation form, or a doctor's letter for a home birth; the mother's antenatal card, both passports and the marriage certificate (originals and copies); an AM80 statutory declaration if there was no antenatal care; and a utility bill if you live away from the address on your documents \[[168](#o168)\].

   1. Find your office at [jpn.gov.my/hubungi-kami/direktori-cawangan](https://www.jpn.gov.my/hubungi-kami/direktori-cawangan/) (choose your state) \[[299](#o299)\]. Counters open Monday to Friday 8 am to 4.45 pm in Johor, Melaka, Negeri Sembilan, Pahang, Penang, Perak, Perlis, Selangor, Kuala Lumpur and Putrajaya; in Kedah, Kelantan and Terengganu, Sunday to Wednesday 8 am to 4.45 pm and Thursday 8 am to 3.15 pm \[[300](#o300)\].
   2. A parent, the guardian, or someone who knew of or saw the birth hands in the documents and gets the birth certificate \[[168](#o168)\].

   A child born before the parents' marriage was registered takes the mother's status \[[301](#o301)\]. The child is not Malaysian unless a parent is a citizen or permanent resident at the birth, or the child would otherwise be stateless \[[302](#o302)\]; JPN decides from the parents' status \[[301](#o301)\]. Act A1752, not yet in force, will limit this to a citizen parent \[[302](#o302)\].

   **Need to know:** whether a hospital birth needs a police report, how long JPN takes, how to check a certificate is genuine, and whether Act A1752 is in force.

2. **Register the birth with Bangladesh,** which Bangladeshi law requires for a child born abroad \[[303](#o303)\].

   You'll need: the JPN certificate attested by Wisma Putra (Malaysia's foreign ministry); copies of both parents' passports; the marriage certificate (kabinnama), e-apostilled (authenticated online by Bangladesh's foreign ministry), or if older, attested by the Law and then Foreign Ministry in Dhaka; the signed printed application; and 2 recent passport-size colour photos on white \[[303](#o303)\].

   1. **Attest the JPN certificate** at the Consular Division, Wisma Putra No 1, Jalan Wisma Putra, Precinct 2, 62602 Putrajaya, 03-8000 8000, sah_admin@kln.gov.my; walk-in Monday to Thursday 8.30 am to 4 pm, Friday 8.30 am to 12 pm and 2.45 to 4 pm. You or someone with your authorisation letter brings an identity card or passport, and usually collects it the same or next working day \[[209 (2023)](#o209)\].
   2. **E-apostille the marriage certificate** through the Ministry of Foreign Affairs' e-Apostille service on MyGov, [mygov.bd](https://www.mygov.bd/services?category=sector&data=service-sector-67): up to five days, with a QR code for checking \[[304](#o304)\].
   3. **Apply** at [bdris.gov.bd](https://bdris.gov.bd/) (Bangladesh's birth and death registration system) with your permanent Bangladesh address, uploading the child's foreign birth certificate and a parent's passport or NID; without uploads it is rejected \[[305](#o305), [303](#o303)\].
   4. The child and both parents take the printed, signed application and documents to the Bangladesh High Commission, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur \[[303](#o303), [122](#o122)\], and pay RM5 by card at the consular counter or by deposit to its Maybank account 564427102268 \[[303](#o303)\].
   5. **Check it is real** at [everify.bdris.gov.bd](https://everify.bdris.gov.bd/), which shows whether a certificate is digital (17 digits) \[[305](#o305)\].

   **Need to know:** the Wisma Putra and e-Apostille fees, and how long the registration takes and how it is collected.

3. **Get the child a Bangladeshi passport:** a 48-page e-passport valid 5 years for anyone under 18 \[[151](#o151)\].

   You'll need: the child in person; the JPN certificate (original, plus a copy attested by JPN); the Bangladeshi birth registration in English showing Malaysia as birthplace, with its online verification copy; both parents' NID numbers, NID copies and online-verified NID copies; copies of both parents' passports and visas; the marriage certificate attested by the Law Ministry, the Foreign Ministry and the High Commission; a photo of each parent; and, under age 6, a 3R or 4R lab-printed photo on grey \[[306 (2024)](#o306), [151](#o151)\].

   1. Book the compulsory appointment with ESKL (Expat Services (Kuala Lumpur) Sdn. Bhd., the High Commission's passport company) at [expatservicesmy.com/#/book-appointment](https://www.expatservicesmy.com/#/book-appointment) or 03-92120267 \[[306 (2024)](#o306), [151](#o151)\], then go to Southgate Commercial Centre, Level 2, Block E, No 2, Jalan Dua, off Jalan Chan Sow Lin, 55200 Kuala Lumpur \[[151](#o151)\].
   2. Pay RM545 (RM485 government fee plus RM60 ESKL charge) \[[306 (2024)](#o306), [151](#o151)\]; a Maybank cash deposit goes to 564427102268 with the child's name on the receipt \[[306 (2024)](#o306)\].
   3. Track it at [epassport.gov.bd/authorization/application-status](https://www.epassport.gov.bd/authorization/application-status) with the Application or Online Registration ID and the child's date of birth \[[307](#o307)\]; questions to the passport WhatsApp, +60104303020 \[[151](#o151)\].

   **Need to know:** how long the passport takes, how it is collected, and how to spot fake passport agents.

## Sending money home

**Use a licensed remitter, never hundi** (sending through an unlicensed agent) \[[19](#o19)\]. In 2022 Bangladesh Bank was reported to warn that hundi is a punishable offence, and money received in about 480 bKash and Nagad agent accounts suspended over hundi could not be cashed \[[N78 (2022)](#n78)\]. A money-sending shop must display its Bank Negara Malaysia (BNM, the central bank) licence or its certificate as a licensed company's agent; without one it is not authorised \[[308](#o308)\]. Licensed remitters such as MoneyGram, Western Union, NBL Money Transfer, Agrani Remittance House, Merchantrade and CBL Money Transfer cost about 5% to 5.5% to send RM610; banks about 9% to 16% \[[309](#o309)\].

You'll need: the receiver's bKash number (a Bangladeshi mobile-money account) and full name as registered with bKash \[[40](#o40)\]; for Touch 'n Go eWallet (a Malaysian wallet app), a passport valid 12 more months and a valid work visa \[[N185](#n185)\].

1. **Check the licence** in BNM's List of MSB (money services business) Licensees, [bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/msb-licensees](https://www.bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/msb-licensees), which also lists head offices and branches, or for an agent shop the List of Approved MSB Agents, [bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/approved-msb-agents](https://www.bnm.gov.my/regulations/fsp-directory/approved-msb-agents) \[[310](#o310), [311](#o311), [312](#o312)\].
2. **Send.** Ria, CBL Money Transfer, Merchantrade, NBL Money Transfer, WorldRemit, Wise, Touch 'n Go eWallet (no Malaysian bank account needed), Western Union, MoneyGram and others send to bKash \[[40](#o40), [N185](#n185)\]. At a partner bank, exchange house or agent, give the bKash number and registered name and hand over the cash \[[40](#o40)\]. Up to Tk 250,000 per transfer, any number of times \[[40](#o40)\].
3. **The family** gets the government's 2.5% incentive on legal remittances \[[40](#o40), [N77](#n77)\] and can cash out at BRAC Bank, City Bank and Q-Cash ATMs for Tk 7 per thousand \[[40](#o40)\].

**Need to know:** what the receipt shows (such as a tracking reference), and how long the money takes.

**If the money doesn't arrive.**

1. Complain to the remitter's or bank's Complaints Unit by phone (note the officer's name, date and time), then in writing with your name, account number and receipt copies; keep a copy. BNM won't take a complaint that skipped this step \[[311](#o311), [313](#o313)\].
2. No reply in 14 days: go to BNMLINK, BNM's customer centre, at [bnmlink.bnm.gov.my](https://bnmlink.bnm.gov.my/) or Ground Floor, D Block, Bank Negara Malaysia, Jalan Dato' Onn, 50480 Kuala Lumpur (9 am to 5 pm, Monday to Friday), or call BNMTELELINK, 1-300-88-5465 \[[313](#o313), [311](#o311)\]. For a bank, also ABMConnect (Association of Banks in Malaysia), 1-300-88-9980 \[[311](#o311)\].
3. Report hundi businesses to BNMTELELINK, 1-300-88-5465 \[[311](#o311)\].

**Need to know:** whether BNMLINK takes complaints against a licensed remitter; the Bangladesh Bank circular that sets this year's 2.5% incentive; and how a worker without a valid pass can send money legally.

## Going home without valid papers

Without a valid pass you can go home under Malaysia's repatriation programme, PRM 2.0 (Program Repatriasi Migran 2.0), 1 May 2026 to 31 May 2027, at Immigration offices in Peninsular Malaysia and Labuan \[[314](#o314), [49](#o49)\], paying a compound (a fixed fine instead of prosecution) \[[49](#o49), [N186](#n186)\]. Otherwise it is arrest, detention in an immigration depot until removal, and deportation \[[41 (2006)](#o41), [N187](#n187)\].

No legalisation programme is open: the Labour Recalibration Programme (RTK), which let employers legalise undocumented workers, ended on 31 December 2023 \[[N90](#n90)\], and PRM 2.0 only lets you leave \[[314](#o314)\]. Anyone but an employer offering to get you a pass is breaking the law (Scams) \[[152 (2021)](#o152)\]. In August 2026 Bangladesh asked Malaysia to register undocumented Bangladeshis \[[N188](#n188)\]. **Need to know:** whether Malaysia will open a new legalisation round.

### Step 1: Get a passport or Travel Permit

With a valid passport, go to Step 2. If it has expired or been lost or damaged, get a Travel Permit (a one-way "out pass" home) from the Bangladesh High Commission \[[303](#o303)\].

You'll need: a copy of your old passport; a copy of your NID or online birth registration; two colour photos; and your phone number \[[303](#o303)\]. Without an NID, a citizenship certificate from your UNO (Upazila Nirbahi Officer, the sub-district's chief officer), or the Deputy Commissioner for Cox's Bazar, emailed to the High Commission's labour wing at counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, ss.lab.kl@gmail.com and fsl.bhc.kl@gmail.com \[[148](#o148)\].

1. Go yourself (no one can go for you) to the High Commission, No 8, Lorong Yap Kwan Seng, 50450 Kuala Lumpur, on an office day, 9:30 am to 1 pm \[[303](#o303), [122](#o122), [148](#o148)\].
2. Pay RM38, the only fee, to the High Commission itself, by card at the consular counter or by deposit to Maybank account 564427102268 \[[315](#o315), [303](#o303)\].
3. Collect it, usually the same day after 4 pm; it is valid 3 months \[[148](#o148), [303](#o303)\].

**Need to know:** how to get the UNO certificate from Malaysia, its cost and time; and whether the permit is same-day or takes 1 to 2 working days, as the High Commission has also said \[[316 (2023)](#o316)\].

**If it fails:** consular section +60326040949, or Minister (Labour) Syed Shariful Islam, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, +601 2290 3252 \[[122](#o122), [102](#o102)\].

### Step 2: Buy a ticket home

You'll need: your passport or Travel Permit, and the fare.

1. Buy a confirmed ticket to Bangladesh dated 5 to 14 days after the day you go to Immigration \[[49](#o49), [N189](#n189), [N190](#n190)\].
2. **Check it is real** on the airline's website with the six-character PNR (booking reference) and your last name; on Biman, "Manage Booking" \[[N133](#n133)\].
3. **If you'll miss the date,** change it beforehand: on Biman, Manage Booking, Exchange, new date, pay any fare difference or fee \[[N133](#n133)\].

**Need to know:** where to buy safely, the usual fare, and whether Immigration accepts a changed date.

### Step 3: Go to Immigration

You'll need: your original passport or Travel Permit; a copy of the photo page; a copy of your last Malaysian pass; old and new passports if you have both; the confirmed ticket; and a debit or credit card or Touch 'n Go eWallet with enough for the compound \[[N189](#n189), [49](#o49)\].

1. Go yourself, without appointment, to one of the 14 Immigration Enforcement offices (headquarters and state offices); no one can apply for you \[[314](#o314), [49](#o49), [N189](#n189), [N190](#n190)\]. Contacts: [imi.gov.my/index.php/hubungi-kami-2/hubungi-kami](https://www.imi.gov.my/index.php/hubungi-kami-2/hubungi-kami/).
   - Putrajaya (headquarters): No. 15, Tingkat 1-7, Blok 2G4 (Menara & Podium), Persiaran Perdana, Presint 2, 62550 Putrajaya, 03-8000 8000 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Kuala Lumpur: Kompleks KDN, No 69, Jalan Sri Hartamas 1, off Jalan Duta, 50550 Kuala Lumpur, 03-62057400 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Selangor: Tingkat 2, Kompleks PKNS, 40550 Shah Alam, 03-5650 0000 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Negeri Sembilan: Wisma Persekutuan, Jalan Dato' Abdul Kadir, 70000 Seremban, 06-7620000 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Johor: Blok 1, Kompleks KDN, Taman Setia Tropika, 81200 Johor Bahru, 07-2338400 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Penang: Jalan Kelasah, 13700 Seberang Jaya, 04-3973011 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Perak: Kompleks KDN, Persiaran Meru Utama, Bandar Meru Raya, 30020 Ipoh, 05-5017100 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Pahang: Kompleks KDN, Bandar Indera Mahkota, 25200 Kuantan, 09-5717999 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Melaka: Kompleks KDN, Jalan Seri Negeri, Hang Tuah Jaya, 75450 Ayer Keroh, 06-2322662 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Kedah: Bangunan KDN, Bandar Mu'adzam Shah, 06550 Alor Setar, 04-7333302 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Perlis: Kompleks KDN, Persiaran Wawasan, 01000 Kangar, 04-9762636 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Terengganu: Wisma Persekutuan, Jalan Sultan Ismail, 20200 Kuala Terengganu, 09-6221424 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Kelantan: Kompleks Imigresen Negeri Kelantan, Jalan Hospital, 15000 Kota Bharu, 09-7482120 \[[211](#o211)\].
   - Labuan: Unit E002, Tingkat 1, Aras Podium, Kompleks Ujana Kewangan, Jalan Merdeka, 87008 Labuan, 087-425 307 \[[211](#o211)\].
2. Arrive early: at Seremban, queues start before the 8 am opening \[[N191](#n191)\].
3. Pay the compound by debit card, credit card or Touch 'n Go eWallet only: RM500 for entering or staying without a valid pass or for overstaying, RM300 for breaking pass conditions, plus RM20 for the Special Pass (Pas Khas, a pass to leave within a set time); under-18s pay only the RM20 \[[49](#o49)\].
4. Leave on your ticket within the Special Pass's time \[[49](#o49), [148](#o148)\].

**Need to know:** the opening hours of the other Immigration offices.

**Who can't use PRM 2.0:** anyone who registered in an earlier programme but didn't leave, is blacklisted by Immigration, or is wanted or under an arrest warrant \[[N192](#n192), [N193](#n193)\]. An overstaying spouse of a Malaysian goes instead to Immigration's Visa, Pass and Permit Division \[[N193](#n193)\]. **Need to know:** whether these rules still apply in the extension to May 2027.

**Scams.** PRM is run by Immigration alone, with no agents; don't pay any broker, and beware anyone asking money for special treatment \[[N190](#n190), [N189](#n189)\].

**If something goes wrong:**

1. PRM line, 03-8880 1269 \[[314](#o314)\].
2. The High Commission's labour wing: Minister (Labour) Syed Shariful Islam, counselorkl@probashi.gov.bd, +601 2290 3252, or Minister (Labour) Md. Siddiqur Rahman, ministermalaysia@probashi.gov.bd, +603 2604 0934 \[[102](#o102)\].
3. Family in Bangladesh: Probashi call centre (Wage Earners' Welfare Board), 16135 free, +8809610102030 from abroad, 24 hours \[[79](#o79)\].
4. Tenaganita (see If you aren't paid): +603 7770 3671; emergency +6012 335 0512 and +6012 339 5350 \[[N141](#n141)\].

### If you lose your passport in Malaysia

You'll need: the passport number or a copy; your new passport or Travel Permit (Step 1); a police report; and a plane, bus or ferry ticket out dated within 7 days \[[158](#o158)\].

1. Report the loss at any police station, orally to the officer (who writes it and reads it back) or on form Pol. 55, free at the counter; any report must be in Malay or English; buy a certified copy for RM2, at once or within 3 days \[[317](#o317)\].
2. Report at once, yourself, without agents, to the Enforcement Division at your state Immigration office (Step 3) \[[158](#o158)\].
3. Pay RM100 for a Special Pass \[[158](#o158)\].

**Need to know:** how long the Special Pass takes.

### If you are detained

With a valid passport you need only a ticket; the depot (detention centre) sets the flight, takes you to the airport and clears you \[[148](#o148)\]. Without one, the depot gets you a Travel Permit from the High Commission free \[[148](#o148)\]. High Commission officers visit prisons and detention camps \[[N96](#n96)\].

Visitors need Immigration's permission letter: for the Klang Valley (Kuala Lumpur area) from the Depot and Deportation Management Division at Putrajaya headquarters, elsewhere from the state Enforcement Division; the detainee's number or the police report number helps \[[154 (2021)](#o154)\]. Detainees may phone, write or receive family with the depot commander's approval \[[N110](#n110)\].

### If your family in Bangladesh asks for help

A close relative can ask the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and Overseas Employment to help bring a worker home; it acts through the High Commission, the Welfare Board or BMET, free, within 7 working days \[[95](#o95)\].

You'll need: proof the person is a migrant worker; their workplace and home address in Malaysia; the recruiting agency's name and address; and the employer's mobile number \[[95](#o95)\].

1. Write to the Ministry, Probashi Kallyan Bhaban, 71-72 Old Elephant Road, Dhaka-1000, or secretary@probashi.gov.bd; phone +88-02-41030444 \[[96](#o96)\].
2. No reply: the grievance officer, Joint Secretary (Admin and Finance), 02-41030224, 01712992086, adsadmin@probashi.gov.bd, has 30 working days \[[95](#o95)\].

**Need to know:** whether the Ministry acknowledges an application and how the family can check its progress.

### Bans after you leave

Immigration's Director General can bar a person for a set period or for ever, and a person whose pass is cancelled is removed and barred \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. Deported people are blacklisted and can't return: for 1 to 5 years by offence, or for life in serious cases \[[N194](#n194), [N195](#n195), [N111](#n111), [N196 (2017)](#n196), [N197 (2024)](#n197)\]; Immigration's blacklist also records fingerprints (BioSL) \[[N196 (2017)](#n196)\]. A ban or pass cancellation can be appealed within 7 days by a written petition, with the grounds in detail, to the Minister of Home Affairs \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]: Blok D1 & D2, Kompleks D, Pusat Pentadbiran Kerajaan Persekutuan, 62546 Putrajaya, 03-8886 8000 \[[318](#o318)\]. Removal for an expired or cancelled pass can't be appealed \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\]. Returning unlawfully after removal can bring a fine up to RM10,000, up to 5 years' jail and whipping \[[41 (2006)](#o41)\].

**Need to know:** how long the ban lasts after PRM 2.0; whether the petition goes to the Ministry directly or through Immigration, and its cost.

## If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job

PERKESO (SOCSO, see Step 7 of the work visa) pays for injuries, disability and death in Malaysia, and the labour wing chases employers for compensation \[[319](#o319), [121 (2024)](#o121)\].
